# FIXES Archive

Historical archive of completed FIX-NNN items, moved out of `docs/FIXES.md` by `pnpm fixes:archive`. Append-only — newest block on top.

## Archived 2026-07-13

### BUGS — Fix These First

- [x] 🔴 S — **page_views anon-writable + session_id exposed (RLS off + GRANT ALL) — LOCK** — RLS is OFF on page_views and anon holds a blanket GRANT ALL, so anon can read session_id AND INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE the table. Proven on local: anon DELETE page_views => HTTP 204 (authorized). Reader (api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts) and writer (api/track-view/route.ts:46) both use createAdminClient (service_role, rolbypassrls=true verified) so locking is safe — tracking and the status dashboard are unaffected. Fix: ENABLE RLS (no SELECT policy) + REVOKE anon/authenticated writes. Clears linter rls_disabled_in_public + sensitive_columns_exposed. Staged un-applied in supabase/migrations/20260531000000_rls_hardening_safe.sql. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-05-31-rls-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-451-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **6 transparency tables are anon-WRITABLE (RLS off + GRANT ALL) — enable RLS + public-read + REVOKE writes** — proposal_cosponsors, industry_codes, lobbying_disclosures, irs990_officers, irs990_filings, irs990_grants_out are RLS-OFF with a blanket GRANT ALL TO anon, so anon can TRUNCATE/DELETE the IRS-990 corpus on prod right now. Proven: anon DELETE irs990_filings => HTTP 204 vs RLS-on proposals => HTTP 401. All writers are createAdminClient pipelines (service_role). Fix: ENABLE RLS + SELECT USING(true) + REVOKE anon/authenticated INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE (preserves the already-public read surface, closes the write hole). Staged un-applied in supabase/migrations/20260531000000_rls_hardening_safe.sql. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-05-31-rls-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-452-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **Duplicate territory jurisdiction rows (district / unincorporated_territory split, AS/GU/MP/PR/VI)** — Same class + playbook as [[FIX-482]] (DC merge), now for all five non-voting-delegate territories. Each of AS/GU/MP/PR/VI has a duplicate jurisdiction pair sharing one fips (60/66/69/72/78): a resolver-split type='district' row (the STATE_DATA convention from [[FIX-321]]) and the canonical type='unincorporated_territory' row ([[FIX-422]] 50-states+territories model). seedJurisdictions keys on (fips_code,type,parent_id), so STATE_DATA's type='district' never matched the canonical unincorporated_territory row and pipelines split each territory's officials across both rows; the /jurisdictions territory filter (which expects unincorporated_territory) rendered them wrong. Verified materialized on local 2026-06-04: 5 dup pairs, district rows each hold 2 empty 'State Senate'/'State House' gbs + 1 stray official, unincorporated_territory rows hold the bulk of officials but 0 gbs. Fix = one audited merge path (winner=unincorporated_territory, loser=district, matched on name+fips): repoint all jurisdiction-FK refs onto the winner; per loser gb, since the winner has no same-type gb, REPOINT gb.jurisdiction_id onto the winner and rewrite its [[FIX-477]] synthetic openstates xsr external_id (gb/<loser-juris>/<type> -> gb/<winner-juris>/<type>) rather than delete; then delete the loser jurisdiction. STATE_DATA root-cause fix flips the 5 entries type='district' -> 'unincorporated_territory' so the next data:jurisdictions run converges instead of re-splitting. Cross-ref [[FIX-482]], [[FIX-422]]. <!--id:FIX-487-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **1,000-row PostgREST cap silently undercounts aggregate/global modes** — Correctness (surfaced by the 2026-06-06 buffer-churn audit; separate from the perf work in [[FIX-503]]). PostgREST silently caps SELECTs at 1000 rows, so unpaged .select() aggregations over large sets produce wrong (low) numbers with no error. Sites: treemap-pac global modes; matrix/route.ts:113-116; hierarchy/route.ts:73-78; treemap/route.ts:67-77 entity mode; graph/entities/route.ts:165-174 (entity_connections from/to fetch for the result id set). Also proposals/page.tsx:224-228 topic filter caps at 1000 AND drops the filter entirely on zero match (line 230) → shows ALL proposals instead of none. Fix per site: paginate with a stable .order('id') (as [[FIX-503]](g) does for the OFFSET loops), or move the aggregation server-side into a counting RPC. Audit each for whether a cap changes a user-visible number. <!--id:FIX-510-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **FR metadata->>sector is dead → sector RPCs silently return empty** — Correctness, confirmed on prod 2026-06-06. financial_relationships rows for relationship_type='donation', to_type='official' carry metadata keys {source, tx_count, aggregated, fec_committee_id, donor_fingerprint} but NO 'sector' key (sampled; a count probe seq-scans the whole table and times out, consistent with ~0 matches). get_group_sector_totals and chord_sector_vote_for_officials read metadata->>'sector' off FR → they return empty/Other for every row. officials/[id]/page.tsx:548 already falls back to entity_tags industry, so its breakdown survives, but the two RPCs do not. Fix: derive sector from entity_tags(tag_category='industry') for the donor entity (the canonical source) instead of FR metadata, OR backfill the sector key. Until then those graph views are blank. Related to the metadata projection in [[FIX-503]](c). <!--id:FIX-512-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **C:/Program Files/Git/proposals topics filter Kong 400 → silent-empty page (server-side RPC)** — The /proposals topics filter fetched ALL entity_tags matches for the active topics (PostgREST silently caps at 1,000) and stuffed the UUIDs into `.in("id", …)` — up to 1,000 UUIDs ≈ 37 KB request URL, over Kong's request-line buffer → nginx 400 'Request Header Or Cookie Too Large'. The error was swallowed by Promise.all + `const { data }` → the page shipped a 200 with an empty list, so /proposals?topics=<any common topic> rendered EMPTY for every user. Fixed by get_topic_proposal_page(p_tags,p_status,p_type,p_agency,p_q,p_sort,p_offset,p_limit): joins entity_tags × proposals server-side, returns one page + count(*) OVER() total; the request URL is now constant-length. Also fixes the zero-match-shows-ALL bug (zero matches → zero rows, never all). SECURITY INVOKER (entity_tags is anon-readable). This is the proposals site of [[FIX-510]], implemented here and escalated from 'undercounts' to 'always empty for common topics'; the other [[FIX-510]] sites (treemap-pac, matrix, hierarchy, treemap entity mode, graph/entities) remain open under [[FIX-510]]. <!--id:FIX-514-->

Name proposals!initiative_details_proposal_id_fkey!inner. Latent twin of the FIX-616 franklin fix. Also fixed /api/initiatives and /initiatives. <!--id:FIX-617-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Bound IE (Schedule E) amounts + quarantine corrupt billion-$ rows** — FEC's public Schedule E corpus carries vexatious/fake filings (fake committees like "THE COURT OF DIVINE JUSTICE"/"THE COMMITTEE OF 300", and a serial prankster filing $1B–$9.98B "IEs" as "Bettis, Shawn"/"Warren Buffet Apple Inc"). These are genuine source rows (CSV columns align cleanly — NOT a parse bug), so they land as ie_support/ie_oppose financial_relationships and pollute total_ie_*_cents, search (gated on IE>0), and IE leaderboards/treemaps (e.g. "APPLE INC. DJIA/SP/DOW USA" carrying ~$9B). Fix: (1) durable upper-amount ingest guard in streamIndependentExpenditures rejecting exp_amo > $50M (real 2024 max single disbursement is ~$30M MAGA Inc; junk starts at $114M/row) + rejectedHighAmount/Cents counters; (2) quarantine existing rows via scripts/quarantine-ie-junk.sql (DELETE where amount_cents > $1B — 4 confirmed-fake aggregates, all containing a >$50M tx so the guard prevents recreation) + rebuild_financial_entity_ie_totals. <!--id:FIX-672-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **status-cron queries dead enrichment_queue.enrichment_type column** — PostgREST string filter on a non-existent column spams the prod Postgres error log every status-cron tick. `getPipelines()` in apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts filtered `.eq("enrichment_type", ...)` — the column was renamed to `task_type` at cutover, and the value "summarize" is actually "summary" (producers: packages/data/src/drain/apply.ts, packages/data/src/pipelines/ai-summaries/index.ts). Build does not catch it (string filter). Fixed: `task_type`/`tag`+`summary`. <!--id:FIX-678-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Saved views vanish after save (FIX-770 regression): destructive reload catch wipes rows** — Regression from FIX-770 (2eacc2fb). Saving a view on /search or /graph made it briefly appear then vanish on prod. Root cause: FIX-770's shared refresh bus makes every save/delete trigger a reload GET on every mounted SavedViewsRail; the pre-existing reload catch did setItems([]) on any failed GET, so one transient GET failure (prod authenticator role 8s read-timeout under cache starvation, see [[FIX-499]]) wiped the just-saved row on every surface. Pre-FIX-770 a save never triggered a reload, so the destructive catch only ran on mount where there was nothing to wipe. Server round-trip (auth, RLS, v2 payload) verified healthy via authed curl, so NOT auth asymmetry and NOT read-path v2 filtering. Fix: non-destructive reload catch (keep prior rows, flag stale) plus keep session-only fallback rows across reloads. References [[FIX-770]]. <!--id:FIX-782-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Saved views still vanish on prod after FIX-782: post-save reload GET races the auth-cookie rotation → anon empty-200 wipe** — FIX-782 (non-destructive reload catch) shipped + deployed but the bug persists on prod: saving on /search or /graph still makes the view vanish, and the /graph Save View never lists the row. Prod evidence: the POST persists (rows land in user_custom_groups owned by the user, is_public=false) but the client's post-save reload GET returns 200 with an empty/anon list — that request authenticates as ANON while the mount GET authes fine. The only difference is timing: the reload GET fires immediately after the POST, and the POST's middleware rotates the session cookie writing it to the RESPONSE only (not the forwarded request; see apps/civitics/middleware.ts setAll) so the chained GET races a rotated-out token. FIX-782's catch only guards GET REJECTION; a GET that succeeds with {groups:[]} still runs setItems([]) and wipes. The /graph path has no optimistic insert so it depends wholly on that racing GET. Fix: stop re-fetching the whole list after our own write — the bus broadcasts the confirmed POST row object (upsert) / deleted id (remove) and each rail applies the delta, immune to the cookie race. Keep the single mount fetch (authes fine) + FIX-782 non-destructive catch. Also add graph-path optimistic insert via the broadcast. FOLLOW-UP: the middleware cookie-rotation pattern (response-only setAll) is a latent auth race for any POST-then-GET flow — investigate separately. References [[FIX-782]] [[FIX-770]]. <!--id:FIX-784-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Per-user authed API endpoints are CDN-cached by the next.config catch-all (stale-after-write + cross-user leak)** — THE real cause of the saved-views 'revert on refresh' (FIX-782/784 chased client-side theories; the server was always fine). next.config.mjs headers() has a catch-all that stamps CDN-Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=600 on everything except a short denylist (api/auth, api/admin, auth, profile, dashboard). /api/graph/custom-groups (and other per-user authed GET routes) are NOT excluded, so the Vercel/Cloudflare edge caches a user's saved-views list for 300s, and the cache key does NOT vary by Cookie. Result: a save/delete persists in the DB (POST returns 201) but a refresh serves the stale edge-cached list → the view 'reverts'. SECURITY: because the cache doesn't vary by user, one user's saved views (and other per-user responses) can be served to another. Confirmed by the live response headers on prod (cf-cache-status DYNAMIC, cdn-cache-control s-maxage=300). Fix: denylist the clearly per-user authed API routes (graph/custom-groups, graph/me, graph/my-representatives, notifications, follows, api/profile, constituent-status, officials/claim-status, representatives) from the cdnHot catch-all and pin them CDN no-store. FOLLOW-UP: audit the ambiguous public-or-private authed GET routes (comments, positions, statements, initiatives, investigations, questions) individually. References [[FIX-784]] [[FIX-782]]. <!--id:FIX-786-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **FIX-786 audit: no-store 4 more per-user authed API routes (positions/statements/questions/investigations)** — FIX-786 security audit follow-through. Four more authed GET routes vary by the signed-in viewer and were still CDN-cached by the next.config catch-all (same cross-user disclosure class as [[FIX-786]]): api/positions (returns the caller's own stance -3..3), api/statements + api/questions (their RPCs embed my_vote keyed to auth.uid()), api/investigations (RLS status<>archived OR created_by=auth.uid() → creators see own archived rows). Fix: add to the userScopedApi CDN no-store denylist. api/positions uses an EXACT source (not /:path*) and a not-followed-by-slash exclusion so its PUBLIC /api/positions/rollup aggregate stays cached. Verified via response headers. References [[FIX-786]]. <!--id:FIX-787-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **CDN cache hotfix: per-user pages cross-user cached + comments read-your-own-writes broken** — Prod, authed (2026-07-11 captures): the cdnHot(300,600) catch-all in next.config.mjs headers() stamps CDN-Cache-Control on every route not in the [[FIX-786]]/[[FIX-787]] denylist — which only ever listed API routes. Exposed per-user PAGES: /desk, /admin/* (only api/admin was denylisted), /dashboard/notifications (the /dashboard rule is exact-match), and the whole /initiatives family (index has the per-user Mine tab, /new + /problem are auth-redirect pages, /[id] SSRs isAuthor + the viewer's upvote/follow state) — the [[FIX-786]] cross-user shared-cache security class at page scope. Also /api/comments/* thread reads cached 300s, breaking read-your-own-writes for every commenter. Hotfix: add these to the denylist + pin cdnNoStore. Comments no-store is the durable end-state (client-island fetches — crawlers do not execute JS, so edge-caching them never reduced crawler load). <!--id:FIX-795-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **plum-book: escape comma-bearing names in the ilike OR-filter** — plum-book builds full_name.ilike.<name> conditions joined by commas into a PostgREST .or(); a name with a comma ('joseph knouff, jr.') breaks the logic-tree parser (failed to parse logic tree, column 514) → the whole ilike chunk's official lookup fails and those plum_id bindings silently drop. Fix: double-quote + backslash-escape each value (full_name.ilike."<escaped>"). Exact case-insensitive match semantics unchanged (no wildcards). <!--id:FIX-821-->

### DB PERFORMANCE / COMPUTE

- [x] 🟠 M — **Buffer-churn quick wins: index-align hot read-path queries** — Prod is cache-starved (shared_buffers 256MB, heap cache-hit ratio 53.9% measured 2026-06-06, IOWait-bound Pro Small). Code-only query fixes that let existing indexes serve hot read-path queries instead of seq-scanning. All plan shapes confirmed on prod via EXPLAIN. (a) display_name ILIKE seq-scans (the trgm index is partial WHERE entity_type<>'individual'): add .neq('entity_type','individual') in api/graph/entities/route.ts:96-100, api/graph/search/route.ts:41-45, api/graph/snapshot/route.ts:145-150 (prod cost 219k→27). (b) entity_tags entity_id-only lookups skip idx_entity_tags_entity(entity_type,entity_id) leading col: thread entity_type into api/graph/snapshot/route.ts:114 fetchEntityTags and add .eq('entity_type','financial_entity') at api/search/entity/route.ts:166 (prod cost 26989→2.8); switch industry filter .ilike→.eq(tag.toLowerCase()) at api/search/route.ts:557-561 (tags are lowercase slugs). (c) officials/[id]/page.tsx:680 add .gt('amount_cents',0) so contract/grant top-10 uses partial financial_relationships_amount DESC index (prod cost 371k→39; also drops NULL-amount rows that DESC-sorted first); page.tsx:311-325 project sector:metadata->>sector instead of full metadata jsonb over up to 50k donor rows (sector key is dead, see sector-column FIX). (d) display-only count:'exact'→'planned'/'estimated': api/search/route.ts proposals(~425)+financial(~533), api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts:118-121, app/page.tsx:364-371 donor fallback. (e) chord compare mode chord/route.ts:489-492 cap entityIds .slice(0,12)+UUID-validate (per-entity query fan-out on user CSV). (f) connections depth-2 connections/route.ts:285-290 swap both fetchAllPaged pulls for get_connection_counts RPC (chunk ids<=500). (g) OFFSET-no-order .range() loops double-count without stable order: add .order('id') at agency-staffing/route.ts:75-94+99-119, sector-affinity/route.ts:66-83, small-dollar/route.ts:71-86. Aggregate-RPC replacements for the loops are separate design FIXes. <!--id:FIX-503-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Index migrations: total_donated_cents partial DESC + summary_plain search** — Two index migrations (follow-up to [[FIX-503]]; design/decide here, ship in a migration session). (1) financial_entities has NO index on total_donated_cents (confirmed prod): the entity_type<>'individual' ORDER BY total_donated_cents DESC NULLS LAST top-N (homepage top donors, treemap) sorts ~78k rows live (prod cost 75505). Add partial btree (total_donated_cents DESC NULLS LAST) WHERE entity_type<>'individual' to match the existing partial-index family. (2) proposals title/summary_plain ILIKE '%q%' search seq-scans (confirmed prod cost 20063, no trgm on summary_plain) — api/search/route.ts:438 and the count at :425. DECISION NEEDED: pg_trgm GIN on summary_plain (keeps current substring ILIKE semantics, large index on long text) vs switch to FTS tsvector/websearch_to_tsquery (smaller, better ranking, changes match semantics from substring to lexeme). Title already short; summary_plain is the cost. Recommend FTS if product can accept word-match; trgm if substring is a hard requirement. <!--id:FIX-504-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **statement_timeout missing on chord/get_group/treemap RPCs** — Confirmed prod (pg_proc.proconfig NULL): all graph aggregation RPCs run with NO per-function statement_timeout except get_group_donor_totals (30s). - [x] 🟠 M — **USASpending writer → direct-pg (kills the enrichment-phase statement-timeout retry storm)** — Root-caused from the 2026-06-14 nightly enrichment-phase (run 27490940863, killed at the 2h cap, 125 `canceling statement due to statement timeout`). The USASpending writer (packages/data/src/pipelines/usaspending-bulk/writer.ts) still wrote via `createAdminClient().upsert()` in 500-row chunks + an `.in("external_id",…)` ref-lookup read — the pre-[[FIX-462]] PostgREST path, subject to prod's 8s `authenticator`/`service_role` role cap. From 09:35–09:51 it retried the same financial_relationships chunks ~100× (all timing out), then `recipient ref lookup read failed: canceling statement` fatally killed BOTH the contracts and assistance runs, and the phase never reached the daily MV/AI tail. Fixed: both writer fns now take a `pg.Client`; the caller (index.ts flushBatch) opens one `withDirectClient` per file (raised 90min session timeout, lib/direct-pg-upsert.ts). Lookup uses `= ANY($1)` (one query, no URL chunking); relationship upsert uses `bulkUpsert` on the FULL usaspending_award_id unique index (migration 20260424000000); the new-entity primary_source refresh runs on the same client. Verified local (typecheck + direct-pg smoke: insert/dedup/ON-CONFLICT-update all pass). Cross-ref [[FIX-462]] [[FIX-444]]. Sibling of [[FIX-587]]. <!--id:FIX-586-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **LittleSis hop-1 resolve/insert + primary-source refresh + FEC donation_totals → direct-pg** — Same 2026-06-14 enrichment-phase root cause. Three remaining admin-PostgREST paths timed out at the 8s role / 100s gateway caps on the now-large financial_entities/financial_relationships tables: (1) LittleSis `resolveOrInsertHop1` (littlesis/writer.ts) — per-entity `resolve_entity_by_canonical` RPC + single-row INSERT, 50-concurrent (`resolve_entity_by_canonical failed for LS:…` ×N); now routed through a new `withDirectPool` (lib/direct-pg-upsert.ts, node-pg Pool max 10, per-conn 90min statement_timeout) using positional SQL. (2) `upsertSourceRefs`'s primary_source refresh (the `n=90484 [primary-source] refresh failed` line) → new shared `refreshPrimarySourceDirect`. (3) FEC `rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals` (the `upstream request timeout` at the gateway cap) → added to the lib/heavy-rebuild.ts allow-list, called via `runHeavyRebuild`. Verified local (typecheck + LittleSis hop-1 direct-pg smoke: inserted=2 rpcErrors=0). Cross-ref [[FIX-462]] [[FIX-463]] [[FIX-443]]. Sibling of [[FIX-586]]. <!--id:FIX-587-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **rebuild_entity_connections full mode no longer fits 4h — donations_full hits 90min statement_timeout, votes_full 72min** — Separate workflow (rebuild-entity-connections.yml), cancelled at its 4h budget 3 Sundays running (5/31 fail, 6/7 + 6/14 cancelled; Wed incrementals still pass ~1.5h). Run 27496446207: `donations_full` FAILED at exactly 5400s (the 90min session cap) → donation edges NOT rebuilt on full runs (almost certainly the source of [[FIX-494]] House-donor-edges-missing — only Wed incremental touches donations); `votes_full` ran 71.6min though its function-level `SET statement_timeout='15min'` (migration 20260525000000:361) — confirming the per-function GUC is a NO-OP through the session pooler (the script's session 90min governs, the [[FIX-444]] hazard). Levers: make the statements faster or batch them, NOT raise the session cap (trades a statement timeout for a wall-clock one). Plan: keyset-batch donations_full re-aggregate by from_id window (mirror the incremental `_dirty_from_ids` scope + LittleSis keyset paging); EXPLAIN votes_full's DISTINCT-ON+COUNT-FILTER (verify votes_derivation_idx is used on the full path; keyset by official_id if O(all votes)); add an explicit alert/non-zero-exit when the donations* chunk specifically fails (it fails-open per-chunk today → silent gap). New migration modelled on 20260525000000. NOTE: collides with in-flight FIX-583/584 uncommitted edits to packages/data/src/scripts/rebuild-entity-connections.ts — sequence AFTER that lands. <!--id:FIX-588-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Pause autovacuum on entity_connections during the full rebuild (FIX-588 follow-up)** — The 2026-06-14 mode=full rebuild (run 27511207480) STILL hit the 4h cap, but the windowing worked: donation windows 1-13 ran ~4min each, then windows 14-16 cliffed to 40-57min each (donations 3h08m total → no room for the rest). Root cause confirmed via pg_stat_user_tables: `entity_connections last_autovacuum=22:27:26` — exactly the window 13→14 boundary. The donations prepare() DELETE leaves ~2.7M dead tuples; FIX-331's aggressive autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor=0.05 trips autovacuum MID-rebuild and its VACUUM I/O competes with the window INSERTs on the cache-starved Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers). Fix (script-only, rebuild-entity-connections.ts): in full mode `ALTER TABLE entity_connections SET (autovacuum_enabled=false)` before the chunks, re-enable + one manual `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` after the post-refreshes, and a belt-and-braces re-enable in the finally so a cancelled/failed run never strands it off. Keeps FIX-331's steady-state aggressiveness for the request path; only pauses during the bulk rebuild. With windows uniformly ~4min, donations ≈64min and the whole full rebuild fits well under 4h. Runtime: re-run mode=full on prod to verify completion + that the post-refreshes (group_donor_rollup MV) run so the graph group route shows House donor edges ([[FIX-494]]). Cross-ref [[FIX-588]] [[FIX-331]]. <!--id:FIX-590-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Harden the rebuild against GHA cancel/timeout — orphaned backends + stranded autovacuum** — Surfaced 2026-06-15 running mode=full ad-hoc: when GHA cancels a run (manual OR the 4h timeout-minutes cap — same path), it kills the node client but Postgres keeps executing the in-flight query as an ORPHANED backend that hammers prod until the session statement_timeout (observed run-1 finalize() orphaned 1h10m + run-2 prepare() DELETE orphaned 16m, together I/O-saturating Pro Small → request-path 503s / 57014 on the live site). Two compounding bugs: (1) GHA's SIGKILL skips the script's `finally`, so FIX-590's `autovacuum_enabled=false` is left stranded → the table never gets autovacuumed. (2) the donations prepare()→windows split is NON-atomic: if a run is cancelled after prepare()'s DELETE-all commits but before the windows finish, donation edges are left empty/partial (the old monolith DELETE+INSERT was one transaction → a cancel rolled back to the prior good data). Fix landed (script-only, rebuild-entity-connections.ts): tag the connection `application_name='entity_connections_rebuild'`; a SIGTERM/SIGINT handler opens a fresh connection, `pg_cancel_backend(myPid)` + re-enables autovacuum (GHA sends SIGTERM before SIGKILL, so this catches the timeout case); and a STARTUP RECONCILE that, before doing anything, cancels+terminates any orphaned backend carrying our application_name (workflow concurrency is cancel-in-progress:false so it's always a prior orphan, never a peer) and unconditionally re-enables autovacuum — self-healing after any dirty exit. Incident recovery (2026-06-15): cancelled both orphans via pg_cancel_backend (run-2 prepare DELETE rolled back → 4.48M donation edges preserved, no data loss), manually re-enabled autovacuum, refreshed group_donor_rollup. RESIDUAL (low-risk now, follow-up): the non-atomic empty/partial-donations-on-cancel (bug 2) is only fully closed by making each window an atomic DELETE+INSERT for its from_id range (needs a `(connection_type, from_id)` index on entity_connections for an efficient ranged DELETE) instead of the upfront prepare() DELETE-all; exposure is small now that donations finish in ~1h vs the 4h budget. OPERATIONAL: the full rebuild saturates Pro Small I/O for its whole duration — run ONLY at low traffic (the 08:00 UTC=midnight PT schedule exists for this); do not trigger ad-hoc during active hours. Cross-ref [[FIX-590]] [[FIX-588]] [[FIX-589]] (compute is the durable relief). <!--id:FIX-591-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Polymorphic entity engagement rollup (claimed/engaged/active-community)** — Per-(entity_type,entity_id) nightly rollup feeding display-only engagement badges. Display-only, never feeds standing. Mirrors official_homepage_stats_mv. PR B of SoF engagement effort. <!--id:FIX-618-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **entity_connections autovacuum stranded OFF on prod — 5.75M dead tuples, 0 autovacuums** — LIVE PROD STATE (read-only audit 2026-06-22, docs/audits/2026-06-22-nightly-rebuild-failure-confirmation.md): `pg_class.reloptions` on entity_connections = `{autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor=0.05,autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor=0.02,autovacuum_enabled=false}` — autovacuum is DISABLED and stranded. `pg_stat_user_tables`: autovacuum_count=0, vacuum_count=0, last_autovacuum/last_vacuum NULL, n_dead_tup=5,755,294 vs n_live_tup=2,496,534 (≈70% dead / 2.3x live). This is the [[FIX-591]] failure mode (full rebuild sets autovacuum_enabled=false, a dirty exit fails to re-enable). The 2026-06-21 11:16 UTC GHA full rebuild was SIGTERM-cancelled at the 4h cap; the manual 2b recovery (run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts, [[FIX-640]]) that finished donations does NOT manage autovacuum, so the flag stayed off and the DELETE+INSERT churn's dead tuples are never reaped. Impact: entity_connections (4079 MB, the user-facing graph-edge table) bloats unboundedly; every from_id index/seq scan reads ~2.3x the live pages on the cache-starved Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers) — directly worsens the request-path graph reads ([[FIX-503]] family) and the next rebuild. Remediation (LOW-TRAFFIC ONLY — heavy I/O on a 4GB table, see memory no-heavy-prod-ops-during-active-hours): `ALTER TABLE entity_connections SET (autovacuum_enabled=true); VACUUM (ANALYZE) entity_connections;` then confirm reloptions. Durability: (a) add the FIX-590 re-enable+VACUUM tail to run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts (the manual recovery path lacks it); (b) investigate why FIX-591's SIGTERM re-enable handler did not restore autovacuum on the 06-21 cancellation (handler may not have fired, or 2b ran after and is the one that left it off). Cross-ref [[FIX-590]] [[FIX-591]] [[FIX-588]]. <!--id:FIX-650-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Nightly enrichment statement-timeout cascade — rule tagger (96min on 2.4GB entity_tags) + refresh_spending_totals + get_official_bipartisan_stats hit 8s/100s caps** — ROOT CAUSE of the 2026-06-22 nightly enrichment-phase failure (run 27936517705, cancelled at the 120min cap; docs/audits/2026-06-22-nightly-rebuild-failure-confirmation.md). The crawl was already mitigated 4-10h prior ([[FIX-637]] rate-limit landed 06-21 22:44 UTC, N+1 collapses by 06-22 05:25 UTC), yet enrichment still cascaded through the same role(8s)/gateway(100s) statement_timeout class as the pre-crawl 06-14 incident — i.e. STRUCTURAL, not crawl-transient. GHA enrichment-phase log: (1) 09:01:03 `refresh_spending_totals warning: canceling statement due to statement timeout` (daily MV refresh, fails-open); (2) 09:01→10:37 the RULE-BASED TAGGER burned ~96 MINUTES then died — `Rule-based tagger fatal error: canceling statement due to statement timeout` — that single step ate ~80% of the 2h budget; (3) 10:50:14 `tag-ai failed: get_official_bipartisan_stats failed after 5 attempts: upstream request timeout` (the ~100s PostgREST gateway cap × 5 retries); (4) 11:00:37 SIGTERM during AI Summaries (never got compute). entity_tags grew 20MB (2026-05-24)→2.4GB / 3.39M rows by 2026-06-22 — the rule tagger scans it on the cache-starved Pro Small and intermittently blows statement_timeout. These three callsites are NOT among the four writers [[FIX-586]]/[[FIX-587]] routed to direct-pg. Fix is the same pattern: route refresh_spending_totals (MV refresh → direct-pg REFRESH like [[FIX-636]]/[[FIX-641]] did for the donor MV), the rule-based tagger (window/keyset by entity_id over entity_tags, or run on the postgres role via direct-pg to clear the 8s cap), and get_official_bipartisan_stats (direct-pg or materialize) off the capped admin-PostgREST path. A compute-tier bump ([[FIX-589]]) would speed the underlying scans but does NOT raise the 8s/100s caps, so direct-pg/windowing is the durable fix. Cross-ref [[FIX-586]] [[FIX-587]] [[FIX-589]] [[FIX-444]]. <!--id:FIX-651-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Quantify unmatched-target IE (Schedule E) drop** — The IE ingest silently drops every Schedule E row whose target candidate isn't a matched Civitics official (indep-exp.ts candidateSet filter + index.ts byFecId lookup), bounding how complete total_ie_*_cents can be. Instrument streamIndependentExpenditures with keptCents/droppedUnmatchedCents (durable, logged every run) and add read-only script data:analyze:ie-drop (analyze-ie-drop.ts) that mirrors the pipeline's candidateSet construction (loadOfficials → buildMatchIndex) and reports per cycle: total/valid/kept/dropped IE $ + top-10 dropped cand_ids. Measurement only — capturing the dropped money (synthetic candidate entities, null to_id) is a follow-up. Measured: ~5.7% of valid IE $ (~$595M across 2020/22/24/26) hidden by the matched-official filter; only 1–2% for recent cycles, larger historically. <!--id:FIX-673-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **jurisdiction_page_cache covers 62/10509 jurisdictions (0.6%) — 99% of jurisdiction pages fall to slow live path and blow the 8s role timeout** — ROOT CAUSE of the recurring prod `canceling statement due to statement timeout` (no statement text) log lines. Attributed via pg_stat_statements on prod 2026-06-27. `get_jurisdiction_page(p_id)` is the highest-traffic request-path RPC (3635 calls). It reads `jurisdiction_page_cache`, falling back to `get_jurisdiction_page_live()` on a miss — but the cache holds only **62 of 10,509 jurisdictions (0.6%)**, so 99.4% of requests run live. Live path measured at **9.5s** on prod (EXPLAIN ANALYZE, cold cache) vs the 383ms warm-cache mean. `authenticated`/`authenticator` role `statement_timeout` is **8s**, so the live path is cancelled mid-flight → the no-text cancellations in the logs (PostgREST-wrapped tail max 7947ms, right at the kill boundary, stddev 970ms). Cache last refreshed 2026-06-27 10:02 but only ever populates ~62 rows — refresh hook is scoped to a tiny allowlist. FIX: (a) expand the refresh to cover all *reachable* jurisdictions (likely far fewer than 10.5k — many are leaf rows with no page link); (b) speed up `get_jurisdiction_page_live` to fit under 8s cold; (c) both. Prefer (a) — materialization pattern in `packages/db/CLAUDE.md`. NOTE: `financial_entities` (~2.3M rows), the original prime suspect, is NOT involved — its request-path queries top out at 3.5s and are well-indexed. Related: [[FIX-681]] (inner statement_timeout guard is structurally ineffective). See `project_consolidating_rpc_null_ordering`, `project_prod_buffer_churn_baseline`. <!--id:FIX-680-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Per-statement statement_timeout guard on get_*_page RPCs is structurally ineffective; agency/gb/official have no page cache at all** — The page-consolidating RPCs each carry an inner `SET statement_timeout` (jurisdiction/agency/gb = 3s, official = 5s) intended to bound the live compute. It does NOT bound total function time: `statement_timeout` is **per-statement**, and these plpgsql functions run many sub-statements each individually under the limit, so the guard never trips while the function total exceeds the 8s role timeout. Measured on prod 2026-06-27: `get_agency_page` ran 5.2s and `get_jurisdiction_page_live` ran 9.5s despite their 3s inner guards. Separately, only `get_jurisdiction_page` has a cache table — `get_agency_page` (29 calls, 3.1s mean), `get_gb_page` (89 calls, 1.4s mean), and `get_official_page` (767 calls, 0.2s mean but 7.95s tail) always run live with no materialized fallback, so their cold/heavy-entity tails blow the 8s timeout too. FIX: replace the ineffective per-statement guard with a real total-time budget (e.g. a single bounding statement or an explicit deadline check / early short-circuit), and/or give the three uncached page RPCs the same cache-table treatment as jurisdiction. Pairs with [[FIX-680]]. <!--id:FIX-681-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Bot-shed empty-shell leaf pages (robots noindex + Upstash caps) — residual get_jurisdiction_page/get_official_page timeout source** — Phase-0 audit for [[FIX-680]]/[[FIX-681]] (docs/audits/2026-06-27-page-rpc-timeout-phase0.md) found the recurring jurisdiction/official `canceling statement` log lines are crawler-induced, NOT user-facing. `page_views` is a JS beacon blind to bots (0 bot rows, 1,133 human rows since April); the real load is pg_stat_statements: 3,685 get_jurisdiction_page calls/6d (~120x the 45 human jurisdiction views/30d) = crawler/SSR one-shot walks over the full leaf set. Root cause: cold-buffer PostGIS geometry reads of empty-shell jurisdictions (Stark County get_jurisdiction_page_live 14.9s cold -> 76ms warm; boundary geometry totals 492MB across ~10.4k district/county leaves >> 256MB shared_buffers, so a crawl cannot stay resident). The content-bearing jurisdiction set is only 62/10,509 and is ALREADY fully cached (FIX-663) — so the [[FIX-680]] 'expand the cache' premise is moot; a 10k/~1GB payload cache would NOT stay resident (it shifts the cold read, not removes it) and its nightly refresh would re-read ~492MB of STATIC geometry for zero content change (the no-heavy-prod-ops hazard). get_official_page is the same shape: 26,915 officials, fast warm (33-551ms), rare 7.95s crawler tail — a 27k-row cache is over-engineering. DURABLE FIX = stop crawlers walking content-less leaves: robots noindex/Disallow on empty-shell jurisdiction/district/county + low-content official leaf pages (nothing to index), Upstash request caps on those routes, optionally Turnstile on the heaviest-geometry ids. Real users hit the 62 cached content jurisdictions (fast), so this is log-noise reduction + I/O relief, not a user-facing regression. agency/gb were the cacheable types (bounded 121/418, resident) and shipped in [[FIX-681]]. <!--id:FIX-683-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Materialize content-bearing official ids so the sitemap can list them (FIX-683 follow-up)** — Deferred from [[FIX-683]]. The sitemap drops the ~10k empty jurisdiction/district shells and lists content jurisdictions+proposals+institutions, but OMITS officials: filtering the ~27k officials to the content-bearing subset (>=1 vote / financial_relationship / entity_connection) at request time EXPLAINs at 3.0–3.5s on the cache-starved Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers) — over the anon role statement_timeout (~3s), so get_sitemap_official_ids 500s with 57014 and the daily sitemap regen logs a canceling-statement (the crawl noise FIX-683 cuts) and serves officials=[]. The function get_sitemap_official_ids(int) IS deployed (prod migration 20260627001100) but is no longer called by sitemap.ts. Fix = the materialization pattern (packages/db/CLAUDE.md): a small content_bearing_official_ids table (or MV) refreshed on the nightly tail (after entity_connections + the MV refreshes, like refresh_jurisdiction_page_cache / the donor rollup), then rewrite get_sitemap_official_ids' body to read it (O(1), same signature) and re-add the sitemap call. Content officials are currently still discoverable via internal links (jurisdiction rosters + institution pages, both in the sitemap) and empty officials are deindexed by FIX-683 item-3 noindex, so this is sitemap-completeness, not a correctness gap. Verify: get_sitemap_official_ids(2500) <200ms on prod; sitemap officials segment repopulates; nightly refresh count sane. <!--id:FIX-685-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Port entity_connections rebuild into a pg_cron SQL procedure** — Move rebuild_entity_connections out of the timeout-prone GHA runner and into the database via pg_cron. New procedure public.run_entity_connections_rebuild(p_mode text) reuses the existing per-chunk SQL functions unchanged (votes/donations windowed trio/cosponsors/etc.), guarded by a SESSION-scoped advisory lock, with data_sync_log observability (running→complete/failed/skipped) keyed to pipeline entity_connections_rebuild and the donations window-loop ported faithfully from packages/data/src/scripts/rebuild-entity-connections.ts. Governed by the chunk functions' statement_timeout instead of a GHA wall-clock budget so no external runner can SIGTERM it mid-rebuild. PR1 of the cron-resilience program. Pairs with [[FIX-589]]. <!--id:FIX-687-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **FEC financial_entity totals rebuilds are full-table UPDATEs that saturate the Pro Small — make them targeted/chunked** — The FEC bulk pipeline finishes with three full-table UPDATEs on financial_entities (rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals / _ie_totals / _received_totals). Each builds a temp table by scanning the ENTIRE financial_relationships donation set (~2M+ rows) then rewrites the whole ~540k-row financial_entities table. On the cache-starved Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers) donation_totals alone ran ~80 min holding a RowExclusiveLock + saturating disk I/O, which timed out unrelated request-path RPCs (e.g. get_quality_counts) → live-site degradation. Surfaced live during the [[FIX-701]] D/B re-capture; had to abort the rebuilds mid-run and instead hand-run a TARGETED, index-scoped UPDATE (correlated subquery over financial_relationships_donor_rollup_idx) for only the ~1,827 affected recipient entities — sub-second, zero I/O storm. Durable fix options (FIX-704-style): (a) scope each rebuild to only the entity ids touched by the current run (pass the affected from_id/to_id set), or (b) move to pg_cron chunked/incremental like entity_connections. Also covers the donor-side gap: a SCOPED run skips donor total_donated_cents overwrite, so after [[FIX-701]] ~232k individual donors' totals understate their D/B slice until a (targeted) donation-totals rebuild runs. Related: [[FIX-717]] / [[FIX-718]] (large MVs → incremental). <!--id:FIX-726-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Restore CDN caching on statements/questions by splitting per-viewer my_vote out of the cached payload** — Follow-up to [[FIX-787]]. api/statements and api/questions are hot entity-page reads but were pinned CDN no-store (FIX-787) to stop the my_vote cross-user cache leak. On the cache-starved prod Small this pushes those reads to the DB on every hit, including the majority anon traffic that has no my_vote. Restore edge caching by splitting the response: cache the public list (no per-viewer fields) at the CDN, and fetch the viewer's my_vote via a separate small uncached/no-store request (or a client-side merge). Then the hot public payload is cacheable again while per-viewer state stays private. References [[FIX-787]] [[FIX-786]]. <!--id:FIX-788-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Elections pipeline: change-detection + set-based writes (drop ~27k per-row UPDATEs/run)** — runElectionsPipeline rewrote all five election-status columns for every active official (~27k) every run via per-row PostgREST UPDATE, ungated — a nightly enrichment-light write sink that collapsed under IO pressure (2026-07-12 incident, light SIGTERM'd at elections). Fix: fetch current values, only write rows whose values actually changed (usually ~0), and write the changed set via a chunked direct-pg UPDATE…FROM (withDirectClient) rather than N single-row round-trips. Value-computation logic (calendar.ts) unchanged. <!--id:FIX-819-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **enrichment_queue: VACUUM + task_type-leading claim index** — Nightly enrichment-tail staging (enqueue_enrichment) hung ~69 min on 2026-07-12; prod pg_stat_statements showed enrichment_queue disk-bound (~12.7% cache hit, ~5-8s ops at the 8s timeout) on a ~150k-row, churn-bloated table. Fix: (A) VACUUM (ANALYZE) to reclaim claim/submit dead-tuple bloat (out-of-band, can't be in a migration), optional per-table autovacuum scale-factor tightening; (B) add (task_type, priority DESC, entity_updated_at DESC) WHERE status='pending' so claim_enrichment_batch seeks one task's pending rows instead of scanning+filtering the whole pending partition (drain throughput on the 140k backlog). <!--id:FIX-820-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Align enrichment_queue pending indexes to entity_updated_at DESC NULLS LAST** — claim_enrichment_batch's ORDER BY … entity_updated_at DESC NULLS LAST keeps a residual (harmless, NOT NULL) Incremental Sort because idx_enrichment_queue_pending_by_task (FIX-820) + idx_enrichment_queue_pending declare entity_updated_at DESC = NULLS FIRST; Postgres won't fold the null-orderings even on a NOT NULL col. Realign both index DDLs to DESC NULLS LAST to drop the sort node. <!--id:FIX-822-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **ai-tagger: server-side anti-join for untagged entities (drop the load-all + in-mem filter)** — runProposalAiTagger/runOfficialAiTagger/backlog-seeder onlyNew path loads the whole proposals/officials table + the full AI-topic-tagged id set into Node then filters in memory — two full scans on the cold Micro to feed a maxCostCents-capped run. Replace with a server-side NOT EXISTS anti-join (ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT n) via withDirectClient, using idx_entity_tags_entity; behavior-preserving (topic tags are entity-derived, onlyNew=untagged). Leaves onlyNew=false full-retag unchanged. <!--id:FIX-823-->

- [x] 🟡 M — **Duplicate District of Columbia jurisdiction rows (fips-11 district/federal_district split)** — Two 'District of Columbia' jurisdiction rows share fips 11: a type='district' row and the canonical type='federal_district' row (the FIX-422 50-states+1 row). Investigation (2026-06-04) shows this is NOT an orphan dup — it is a load-bearing split: on prod the district row holds the REAL elected DC councilmembers (14 officials) + 2 gbs (DC State House/Senate), while the federal_district row holds 34 candidate-tier officials + 2 DUPLICATE DC State House/Senate gbs. openstates bulk-people resolves DC to one row and FEC/candidate ingest resolves to the other (CLAUDE.md already notes fips/abbr collide across the two DC rows). A correct fix is a data merge — dedup the two gb pairs (4→2), rewrite officials.governing_body_id + jurisdiction_id onto the federal_district winner, delete the district row — PLUS a resolver root-cause fix so openstates/FEC stop re-splitting DC on the next run. That exceeds this bullet's original S/M sizing and reaches into pipeline resolver code, so [[FIX-K]] is redirected here. Exact prod merge SQL drafted; load-bearing prod DELETE awaits explicit confirmation per destructive-SQL rules. Cross-ref [[FIX-477]] (forced jurisdiction_id-keyed external_ids), [[FIX-422]]. <!--id:FIX-482-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Congress committees pipeline aborts at membership-clear — .in() over ~230 committee UUIDs overflows PostgREST URL** — Surfaced running data:committees against local Docker (2026-06-04): step 7 clears current memberships with a single .delete().in('committee_id', <all ~230 touched committee UUIDs>), which overflows the PostgREST request URL ('URI too long') and throws, aborting the whole pipeline before any membership row is written. Latent on prod too (the membership feed matches all 230 committees). Fix: chunk the .in() at 100 ids/delete (same convention as the openstates writer LOOKUP_CHUNK_SIZE). Found while landing [[FIX-478]]/[[FIX-481]]. <!--id:FIX-484-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Token-align the issue-category data-viz palette (officials issue bars) — phase 1.6 chart follow-up** — Phase 1.6 (FIX-564) migrated officials/[id] to the FIX-552 token system but intentionally left the ISSUE_KEYWORDS categorical palette as raw hex — the 7 per-issue colors at officials/[id]/page.tsx:165-224 (#10b981 healthcare, #06b6d4 climate, #f59e0b economy, #8b5cf6 education, #64748b defense, #f97316 immigration, #a855f7 justice) are consumed as inline backgroundColor: stat.color for the issue-bar fills in VotesTab (mini bars) and the issueStats list. This is a data-viz categorical palette, NOT chrome: it needs ~7 mutually-distinguishable hues that read against paper, so it can't be a one-prop swap to a single semantic token (bg-ink etc. would make every issue bar identical). Two shapes to decide between: (a) derive a token-aligned categorical ramp from the brand palette (accent / civic-blue / green-ink / amber + 3 derived hues), or (b) define a dedicated --c-viz-1..7 token set in globals.css (channel triplets, terminal rebind) and reference via rgb(var(--c-viz-N)). Either way the inline hex goes away and the bars stay distinguishable + on-brand. Pure styling; zero behavior/query/schema changes. Cross-ref [[FIX-552]] (tokens), [[FIX-563]] (alpha-capable tokens), [[FIX-564]] (officials phase 1.6), [[FIX-566]] (sibling comment-subsystem reskin follow-up). If more categorical data-viz palettes surface elsewhere (e.g. graph node colors, agency/sector charts), fold them into the same --c-viz-* set rather than re-deriving per surface. <!--id:FIX-567-->

Add the IE columns to the SELECT, and in the result mapping give IE precedence as the displayed amount (IE → federal spending → donations) with a new `amount_label='independent_expenditure'` rendered as "Ind. expenditures" on the search card. Gate on IE>0, not entity_type (FEC committee type 'U' falls through to 'other'). <!--id:FIX-667-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Map FEC committee type 'U' (IE-only) to a real entity_type instead of 'other'** — `cmteTypeToEntityType()` in the FEC pipeline does not map committee type 'U' (independent-expenditure-only, non-qualified), so 154 IE spenders (518 ie_support/ie_oppose rows) land as `entity_type='other'` on prod rather than `super_pac` — surfaced while verifying [[FIX-666]]. The IE read-surfaces (search/donor page) are already robust to this because they gate on `total_ie_* > 0` not entity_type, so this is cosmetic/classification only. Decide whether type 'U' should map to `super_pac` (most are IE-only super PACs) or a distinct value, and backfill existing 'other' rows that have a committee type 'U' source. <!--id:FIX-669-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Donor IE target list reconciles (no-op; 18/22 misobservation)** — Post-ship verification of FIX-666/667/668 reported UDP (FEC C00799031) rendering 18 Supports / 22 Opposes IE badges vs a verification count of 9 ie_support + 12 ie_oppose rows. Investigated read-only via scripts/diagnose-ie-udp.sql (local + prod). Findings: C00799031 resolves to exactly ONE financial_entity (no duplicate); donor page fetches ieRows filtered by from_id=entity.id and aggregates by to_id+direction, so badge count = distinct (target,direction) and structurally CANNOT exceed row count. Prod renders 9 support + 11 oppose badges (12 oppose rows, one target opposed in both cycles) — fully reconciled and internally consistent. The 18/22 was a misobservation. Closed no-op; no render/data bug. See diagnostic branch (b). <!--id:FIX-671-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Materialize financial_entities.total_received_cents (inbound donation totals)** — total_received_cents is hardcoded to 0 at FEC indiv write time (packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/writer.ts:124) and has no maintained rebuild, so the donor-page headline 'Total received' is always $0 even when inbound donation rows exist. Mirror FIX-666's IE-totals materialization: new rebuild fn rebuild_financial_entity_received_totals() (temp-table SUM of financial_relationships WHERE to_type='financial_entity' AND relationship_type='donation' GROUP BY to_id, two-pass UPDATE keyed on to_id), add to ALLOWED_REBUILDS in heavy-rebuild.ts, wire into the nightly after the IE rebuild (fec-bulk/index.ts:1386), backfill script rebuild-received-totals.ts + data:rebuild:received-totals alias. Column already exists — no DDL. See [[FIX-666]]. <!--id:FIX-675-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Donor page: context-aware empty state for IE-only super-PAC recipients** — On an IE-only super-PAC donor page (showIe true, topRecipients.length 0) the 'Top recipients' section shows a bare 'No outbound donations on record' (apps/civitics/app/donors/[id]/page.tsx:693-695) which reads like missing data. It is correct — an IE-only super PAC makes no direct candidate contributions — so render an explanatory note for the IE-only case (independent-expenditure-only committee; spends to support/oppose rather than contributing directly; see Independent expenditures below) while keeping the bare message for non-IE entities. See [[FIX-675]]. <!--id:FIX-676-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **UDP (and IE-only super PACs): inbound donor rows never landed — targeted indiv→committee backfill** — Investigation under [[FIX-675]]: United Democracy Project (FEC C00799031, super_pac) has ZERO inbound financial_relationships rows of ANY type on local AND prod — only IE outbound (ie_support/ie_oppose). So 'Total received' and 'Top donors' are genuinely empty, not a read-path bug. The indiv→committee path works (5,960 committees have inbound donation rows locally) — UDP simply was not populated. Two gaps: (1) UDP received few/no ITEMIZED INDIVIDUAL contributions in indiv24 (a targeted off-peak indiv stage re-run scoped to super-PAC recipients would surface any that exist — do NOT run the full 8GB indiv stage inline; see fec-bulk indiv stage + NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192). (2) UDP's real funding is COMMITTEE-TO-COMMITTEE transfers (AIPAC bundling), which the individual-contribution indiv path does not capture at all — that is the trackAIPAC committee-to-committee attribution effort, explicitly larger/out-of-scope. Recommend: scoped off-peak indiv→committee backfill first; committee-to-committee transfer ingest tracked separately. <!--id:FIX-677-->

No destructive migration, no row rewrites (filter is unconstrained JSONB — no schema change needed). Saved views render on BOTH surfaces: the /search rail (replacing the W1 disabled stub) with save-current-view + delete, and the graph sidebar mount ([[FIX-762]]) where a view loads into the browser and [+] adds it as a live group via the compiler. Anon behavior mirrors the existing custom-group fallback: session-only, no persist. Route POST accepts v1 (GroupBuilderWidget back-compat) and v2. <!--id:FIX-763-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Browse W3: zero-query /search landing — category tiles + strips + START FROM row over the cheap rollup** — Wave 3 of the search/browse explorer redesign (Screen 1). Zero-query /search (no q, no scope, no facets) renders a cacheable BrowseLanding instead of the W1 48-row scoped-browse SSR (~140KB RSC per the [[FIX-751]] ship report): big search input, START FROM discovery-path row, per-kind category tiles (counts from browse_facet_counts __total__ rows + sub-links deep-linking into W1 explorer scope states), and three capped strips — COMMENT PERIODS CLOSING (reuses the homepage open_comment + metadata->>comment_period_end read, ClosingSoonPanel shape), RECENTLY ACTIVE (top-N entity_search_index by activity_at), MOST CONNECTED (top-N by connection_count). Honesty kicker shows the envelope refreshed_at (LIVE INDEX · refreshed HH:MM UTC). Landing data = one browse_facet_counts read + 3 capped index reads — NO get_browse_page fan-out, NO get_connection_counts, NO exact request-path COUNT — s-maxage cacheable. Any q/scope/facet still routes to the W1 ExplorerPage unchanged. Companion: By Place/By Topic roots + typeahead sweep. Cross-ref [[FIX-751]] (W1 explorer), [[FIX-749]] (/api/browse + rollup envelope), [[FIX-683]] (empty-leaf crawl-cost precedent). <!--id:FIX-767-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Browse W3: discovery-path roots — By Place (lazy jurisdiction tree) + By Topic (tag taxonomy) + By Money/By Time presets** — Companion to the W3 landing (FIX-767). Adds a discovery-path root registry distinct from the static By-Branch BROWSE_TREE (scope-tree.ts): (1) By Place — a lazy jurisdiction hierarchy (country→state→county/city) whose children are fetched per level from jurisdictions.parent_id (listChildJurisdictions) via a new cheap /api/browse/jurisdictions endpoint, so the ~10.5k nodes never load eagerly; (2) By Topic — the proposal tag taxonomy sourced the same way /api/graph/tag-groups does (entity_tags entity_type=proposal, tag_category=topic, top-N by distinct-proposal count); (3) By Money + By Time ship as facet-preset SHORTCUTS (not trees) that jump straight into explorer states (financial kinds + industry/bracket facets; recency / comment-window sorts). Registry-driven like By Branch — rendered in the landing START FROM row and, for the two trees, the explorer ScopeRail. NOTE: entity_search_index carries no tag facet column, so By Topic deep-links proposals via a text-query bridge; precise per-tag faceting would need an index column (migration) — flagged as a follow-up, not built this wave. Cross-ref [[FIX-751]] [[FIX-752]] (scope compiler + kind-direct paths), [[FIX-749]] (registry). <!--id:FIX-768-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Browse W3: ⌘K GlobalSearch → cheap typeahead + /api/search consumer sweep & deprecation** — Retargets the ⌘K GlobalSearch dropdown and the investigations EntitySearchPicker off the 8-searcher /api/search fan-out and onto a cheap typeahead (mode=typeahead on /api/browse): ~14 results grouped by kind, trigram/tsv over entity_search_index + the existing capped financial-individual passthrough, NO facet counts, NO connection-count RPC, NO exact totals. FIX-555 dropdown styling, 300ms debounce, and keyboard nav are untouched — only the data source and result mapping change; view-all still routes to /search?q=. Consumer sweep of every remaining /api/search caller (grep apps + packages): GlobalSearch + EntitySearchPicker migrate; EntityBrowse (packages/graph pre-W2 selector) is already unmounted by [[FIX-762]] and tracked for retirement in [[FIX-765]] — annotated, not migrated; AdvancedSearchPage (legacy /search page) is unconsumed after W1 and tracked in [[FIX-753]]. /api/search kept in place with a @deprecated route comment (no live consumers after this wave); /api/search/entity KEPT (detail rail, per [[FIX-751]] decision 7). Nav Search ⌘K entry + hardcoded /search? links checked against the W1 legacy translator + W2 token map. Cross-ref [[FIX-752]] (legacy translation), [[FIX-753]] [[FIX-765]] (cleanup ledgers). <!--id:FIX-769-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Viewer overlay doesn't cover own ratings — no indication of your existing agree/valuable (★) rating anywhere** — Found in the FIX-795 post-deploy QA (2026-07-12): the authed engagement round-trip persists every write, but the UI never shows the caller's OWN existing rating — the agree axis and the ★ Valuable-to-the-discussion axis render unselected on refresh even when a rating is tracked server-side. Root cause is a [[FIX-788]] coverage gap, not a regression: cached list payloads are viewer-independent by design ([[FIX-787]] audited comments as genuinely public — no my_rating field has ever existed there), and the /api/viewer/engagement overlay only returns statement ballots + can_answer. RatingControls (EntityComments.tsx) and the QASection rating rows seed from the public rating_summary aggregate with local-only optimistic state, so own-state dies on refresh. Fix: extend the overlay route to return the caller's ratings for the on-screen comment/question/answer ids (both axes), extend fetchViewerEngagement + the consuming components to merge it — the designed extension point per the FIX-788 rule: new personalized fields go in the overlay, never a cached payload. Audit the other engagement marks (initiative upvote/sign state, evidence ratings) for the same gap while in there. <!--id:FIX-798-->

### OFFICIALS

- [x] 🟡 S — **Congress pipeline doesn't flip is_active when a member leaves office** — Surfaced during [[FIX-403]] prod verification 2026-05-27. The Congress.gov officials pipeline (packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/officials.ts) only ingests CURRENT members (the members API returns currentMember=true rows). When a sitting member leaves office (loses re-election, resigns, dies) and Congress.gov flips them to currentMember=false, our pipeline simply stops seeing them in the feed — it does NOT flip the existing officials row's is_active to false. Result: former members retain is_active=true indefinitely until manually corrected. Confirmed via prod query during FIX-403 smoke: zero is_active=false officials carry primary_source='congress_gov' (all inactive officials trace to other sources like FEC). Action: (a) decide the detection mechanism — either (i) fetch the currentMember=false delta from the Congress.gov members API and flip is_active on matched bioguide_ids, or (ii) a reconciliation pass that compares the set of bioguide_ids in the current feed against existing is_active=true congress officials and flips any that fell out of the feed (with a guard against false-positives from a partial/failed API fetch — only flip when the full member list fetched successfully). Option (ii) is more robust to API shape changes. (b) Consider whether former members should also get tier/term-end treatment — cross-ref [[FIX-249]] ('former' tier on officials, term-end tracking) which is the broader home for this. This FIX is the congress-specific detection half; FIX-249 is the schema/display half. Pre-existing behavior, not introduced by FIX-403 — just confirmed during its verification. Cross-ref [[FIX-249]] (former-tier schema), [[FIX-248]] (candidate→elected promotion, the inverse transition this pipeline already handles). <!--id:FIX-409-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **/officials/[id] 404s on some municipal officials despite valid rows (likely .single() PGRST116, not a filter)** — Surfaced during [[FIX-399]] prod verification 2026-05-27: /officials/[id] returns Next.js not-found for some municipal (Legistar) officials whose rows DO exist and have primary_source populated — e.g. Jennifer Samuels (Legistar Seattle, id 9504546c…). NOTE: the FIX-399 report hypothesized "the loader filters municipal officials out" — that is INCORRECT. getCachedOfficial (apps/civitics/app/officials/_lib/get-official.ts) has NO municipal/jurisdiction filter; the query is .from('officials').select(...embeds...).eq('id', id).single(). The likely real cause is .single() throwing PGRST116 because >1 officials row matches the id-adjacent identity — Legistar officials are prone to cross-body / casing duplicates (cf. [[FIX-375]] casing-dupe residue), and .single() errors (returns {data:null, error:PGRST116}) on >1 rows, NOT on the id .eq (which is unique). Investigate first: (a) is there genuinely a duplicate officials row for the affected people? (a single id .eq can only match one row by PK — so if .single() fails it implies the EMBED join (jurisdictions!jurisdiction_id or governing_bodies!governing_body_id) is fanning out to multiple rows, OR the row genuinely 404s for a different reason like an RLS/embed mismatch). (b) Reproduce with the exact failing id and capture the PostgREST error (the loader currently swallows it — only checks `if (!data) return null`, ignoring `error`). Surface the error first. (c) Fix per root cause: if embed fan-out, constrain the embed or switch to a two-step fetch; if genuine 404 for another reason, address that. Also: regardless of root cause, the loader should LOG the PostgREST error instead of silently returning not-found (mirrors the [[FIX-394]] "throw on .error so silent-zero surfaces" lesson). User-facing impact: valid municipal official pages 404, AND the /officials route currently surfaces zero local-category SourceBadges as a result. Cross-ref [[FIX-399]] (surfacing case), [[FIX-375]] (casing-dupe residue hypothesis), [[FIX-394]] (swallowed-PostgREST-error pattern). <!--id:FIX-412-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **governing_body membership pollution — audit + tier-scope institution rosters** — The `/institutions/[id]` GoverningBodyView computes its roster, party balance, and 'Active members' stat from `officials.governing_body_id` + `is_active` only, so legislature pages over-count members massively. Local: US Senate gb shows ~1,430 'active' (100 `tier='elected'` + 1,330 `tier='candidate'`); US House ~6,042 (436 elected + 5,606 candidates); Office of the President ~2,341 (2 elected + 2,339 candidates). FEC candidate ingest (cn{yy}.zip, FIX-246) parks every candidate on the body it runs for with `tier='candidate'`, so `tier` cleanly labels the pollution. **Hypothesis (verify on prod):** ingest is correct; bug is consumer-side — institution queries never filter `tier`. Fix scopes display surfaces to current members = `is_active = true AND tier = 'elected'` via a shared predicate (≥2 consumers: this + future [[FIX-468]] graph group-expansion). Mirrors [[FIX-439]] (chamber-scoping the recent-votes RPC for exactly this mis-linking). No schema changes, no ingest changes this PR. Cross-refs [[FIX-249]] (tier column), [[FIX-457]] (is_active display guard). Investigation-first: read-only audit (local + prod) drives the code change; audit doc at docs/audits/2026-06-03-gb-membership-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-470-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Official detail page → governing-body (institution) cross-link** — The official detail page renders no link to the official's governing body. Fetch the gb (id, name, short_name via officials.governing_body_id) in the existing SSR parallel batch and render the body name as a link to /institutions/{gb_id} in the header/info area, matching existing label/link styling. Same link for candidate-tier officials. Cross-ref [[FIX-442]]. <!--id:FIX-474-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Model DC as unicameral council (legislature_unicameral), not State Senate/House** — File-only follow-up surfaced during the [[FIX-482]] DC merge. The District of Columbia has a single legislative chamber — the Council of the District of Columbia (13 members) — but is currently modeled with the generic state-shaped pair of governing_bodies (a 'DC State Senate' legislature_upper + 'DC State House' legislature_lower), and DC councilmembers are parked on the 'State Senate' gb. Correct modeling is a single legislature_unicameral gb named 'Council of the District of Columbia', which needs openstates chamberKey handling (openstates exposes DC's council under a single-chamber key, not upper/lower) so ingest stops creating/feeding the two-chamber shape. No code in this pass — captured so the modeling work isn't lost. Cross-ref [[FIX-482]]. <!--id:FIX-489-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **NE/GU/VI unicameral legislature merges + seeder chamber-shape root cause** — Extension of [[FIX-489]] (DC-only bullet) to all four mis-modeled unicameral jurisdictions, per Craig's 2026-06-10 approval. Cowork verified on local that NE/GU/VI are modeled exactly like DC: a State Senate/State House pair with the roster (NE 49; GU/VI empty) parked on the legislature_upper row and an empty legislature_lower sibling. Convert-in-place: the populated upper row becomes type=legislature_unicameral with the proper name (Nebraska Legislature; Guam Legislature (I Liheslaturan Guåhan); Legislature of the Virgin Islands), slug/short_name regenerated, synthetic xsr external_id rewritten gb/<juris>/<type>; refs off the empty lower sibling repointed, sibling deleted. Root causes fixed in the same commit: (1) openstates Phase 0 unconditionally resolves an upper+lower pair for every STATE_DATA entry (writer inserts the missing chamber), so the pair re-splits on every run; (2) the people/bills OCD jurisdiction id hardcodes the state: prefix — DC is district:dc and the five territories are territory:xx, so those fetches return zero items today (live-probed 2026-06-11); (3) unicameral members are exposed under org_classification=legislature, never upper/lower. Data-merge half: data:merge-unicameral-legislatures (plan/apply per env, prod gated). Cross-ref [[FIX-489]] [[FIX-487]] [[FIX-482]]. <!--id:FIX-548-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Federal officials missing metadata.state/state_abbr undercount state-filtered groups** — Found during the FIX-495 alias verification (2026-06-11): /api/graph/group?chamber=senate&state=TX returns 1 member (John Cornyn) instead of 2 — Ted Cruz's officials row has neither metadata->>'state' nor metadata->>'state_abbr', so the FIX-175/FIX-124 state OR-filter misses him. Identical undercount on the legacy role_title path, so this is a pre-existing data gap, NOT a FIX-495 regression. The FIX-124 state_abbr backfill apparently didn't cover all sitting federal officials. Audit actives on the federal House/Senate gbs for NULL metadata state keys (local + prod) and re-run/extend the backfill; the durable fix may be deriving state from jurisdiction or district rather than metadata. Cross-ref [[FIX-495]] [[FIX-124]] [[FIX-175]]. <!--id:FIX-550-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **Officials index + detail to Public Record tokens, incl. claim island stampb idiom + /admin/grants ledger styling** — Phase 1.6. Migrate apps/civitics/app/officials/page.tsx + components and the officials/[id] detail tree to the FIX-552 token system: paper surfaces, ink text, hairline rule borders, Fraunces serif titles/entity names, mono tabular-nums numerals, party squares, accent-red hovers. Claim island (FIX-558 ClaimProfileSection) and verified/claimed indicators adopt the desk-mockup .stampb badge idiom with StampMark — green-ink claimed, blue informational, red pending/action. /admin/grants gets paper tokens + ledger table (2px ink header rule, hairline rows), 404/auth behavior byte-identical. Reskin only — zero behavior/layout/query changes. Chart re-theming beyond simple color props is follow-up. Cross-ref [[FIX-552]], [[FIX-557]], [[FIX-558]], [[FIX-563]]. <!--id:FIX-564-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Engagement badges + Most-engaged sort on officials** — EngagementBadges component on OfficialCard + Most-engaged sort on OfficialsList, sourced from the engagement rollup (FIX-618). Officials surface only; institutions/jurisdictions = PR B2. <!--id:FIX-619-->

### PROPOSALS

- [x] 🟠 S — **OpenStates bill ingestion passes `""` instead of `null` for empty dates → silent insert failures** — During the [[FIX-445]] backfill, the openstates pipeline logged `proposal insert 0-18: invalid input syntax for type date: ""` (AZ, repeatable) and the prod run reported 37 failed bill inserts. Some bills come back from the OpenStates `/bills` API with an empty-string `first_action_date`/`latest_action_date`; `openstates/index.ts` forwards `bill.first_action_date ?? null` / `bill.latest_action_date ?? null`, but `??` only catches `null`/`undefined` — an empty string `""` passes through and Postgres rejects it as an invalid `date`, failing the whole chunked insert (so good rows in the chunk are lost too). Fix: coerce empty-string dates to `null` before building `StateBillInput` (e.g. `bill.first_action_date?.trim() || null`). Same guard likely warranted anywhere else raw API date strings are forwarded. <!--id:FIX-449-->

OPM? a hardcoded EOP list?) and seed xsr accordingly, OR confirm they should remain unattributed (some EOP offices have ambiguous provenance). 8 rows = trivial seed once source is chosen. Cross-ref [[FIX-410]]. <!--id:FIX-415-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Agency Q&A surface — generalize get_entity_questions/QASection to (entity_type, entity_id)** — The Q&A lane is officials-only: get_entity_questions hard-filters entity_type='official' and takes p_official_id; QASection + AskComposer/AnswerComposer + /api/questions + /api/comments all hardcode entity_type:'official'; the answer-gate has_active_official_grant checks target_type='official'. Agency detail pages render no Q&A. Franklin S2 authored an FDEQ dam-relicensing Q&A (anchored on agency ag-fdeq) — it's co-located at packages/data/src/seed/franklin/data/s2/qa.json but is NOT seeded (would be invisible dead data) and was deferred from the [[FIX-609]] S2 seed. To ship it: generalize the read RPC to (entity_type, entity_id) keeping the official overload; render QASection on the agency page; add a synthetic agency-rep grant path (agencies aren't users — map the seeded FDEQ answerer to a user holding an 'official'/agency grant on ag-fdeq); generalize the /api routes; surface the SF-P7 'demo officials don't answer' note so a REAL user's question to a synthetic agency gets the disclaimer (only seeded questions get seeded answers). Then wire s2/qa.json into the seed loader. <!--id:FIX-610-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **agency search/select queries dead agencies.slug column** — PostgREST select on a cutover-dropped column makes agency search return zero results and spams the prod error log. `agencies.slug` no longer exists. apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts selected `slug` (and mapped `slug: a.slug`), and apps/civitics/app/api/search/entity/route.ts selected `slug` for `profile_url`. Build does not catch it (string select). ### GRAPH

- [x] 🟢 M — **`'former'` tier on officials (term-end tracking)** — FIX-246's `tier` CHECK is `IN ('elected', 'candidate')`. When term-end tracking lands, widen to `IN ('elected', 'candidate', 'former')` and add a backfill that sets `tier='former'` where `term_end < CURRENT_DATE`. Requires deciding whether former officials stay searchable by default (probably yes — they appear in historical financial_relationships) and whether profile pages render the "Former" badge differently from the existing `is_active=false` Former pill. <!--id:FIX-249-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Make LittleSis canonical-binding incremental — drop wall-clock cost of Phase 2** — FIX-292 split LittleSis into its own GHA job to give it a fresh 120-min budget, but the underlying cost is that LittleSis's matcher index build + `resolve_entity_by_canonical` per-row loop runs the full 600K-entity dump on every Sunday cron, even when the upstream dump's SHA256 is unchanged (FIX-251's `skipSync='dumps_unchanged'` short-circuits the *download* but the canonical-binding pass still re-runs against the full local table since FIX-280 ships). Incrementalize: (a) gate the matcher index rebuild + RESOLVE_BATCH loop on `pipeline_state.littlesis.last_indexed_at` vs `financial_entities.updated_at` since that watermark — only LS rows whose hop-1 target may have changed need re-binding; (b) alternatively, persist `external_source_refs` decisions and skip re-resolving any ls_id that already has a binding (already partially handled by `preloadKnownLittleSisIds` at `packages/data/src/pipelines/littlesis/index.ts:154` — verify all paths route through it). Target: drop Phase 2 wall-clock from ~75-90 min to <15 min on stable-dump Sundays. Surfaced as a follow-up to FIX-292. <!--id:FIX-294-->
- [x] 🟢 M — **LittleSis intra-source dupe merge — shared-edge heuristic path (merged_into field absent)** — Follow-up to FIX-313. The 2026-05-25 investigation scanned all 440,048 records in the public LittleSis entities dump (https://littlesis.org/database/public_data/entities.json.gz) — no `merged_into` field (or any of `merged_id`/`merged` alternates) is present in the public dump. Top-level keys observed: `aliases, blurb, end_date, extensions, id, name, parent_id, primary_ext, start_date, summary, tags, types, updated_at, website`. FIX-313's path (a) is therefore infeasible against the public dump (the field may exist in the internal DB but isn't published). **Path (b) shared-edge heuristic**: for clusters of ≥2 LS-bound FE rows sharing canonical_name, merge only when their `external_relationships` neighborhoods overlap above a threshold. Suggested threshold: ≥2 shared neighbor entities (winner = LS row with higher edge count, tiebreak by lowest ls_id for determinism). Conservative — most common-surname false positives (Robert Smith × N) lack neighborhood overlap. Open question: do we hit the LittleSis API for the missing `merged_into` field per LS-id (rate-limited, ~3,310 prod clusters × 2 ids = 6,620 calls)? Probably not worth it given path (b)'s independent signal. Cross-ref [[FIX-313]] (parent), [[FIX-271]] (canonical FK-rewrite pattern), [[FIX-273]] (LS dedup race that this cleans up the residue from). <!--id:FIX-380-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Graph-seed guard — stop silent drop of un-graphable kinds in search add-to-graph** — Selecting an institution/jurisdiction/meeting/initiative in /search and clicking Add to graph silently adds nothing — the /graph handoff whitelist (official/agency/proposal/financial) drops every other kind with zero user feedback. Introduce one shared GRAPH_SEEDABLE_KINDS constant + type guard, filter the add-to-graph payload at the search source (SearchActionBar + AdvancedSearchPage seed/add paths), show an 'N of M can be graphed' hint and disable the affordance when zero are seedable, and make /graph's whitelist console.warn the dropped ids/kinds instead of a bare return. Cross-ref [[FIX-468]] (umbrella — widening the seedable list to institutions/meetings/jurisdictions is its later phases) + [[FIX-442]]. <!--id:FIX-472-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **gb group-expansion in /api/graph/group** — C1 of [[FIX-468]] Wave A. New cohort branch in apps/civitics/app/api/graph/group/route.ts keyed by `governingBody=<slug-or-uuid>` (slug canonical, UUID fallback forever — gb UUIDs differ local vs prod). Branches on gb.type: `committee` reuses the FIX-139 official_committee_memberships path (existing `committeeId` param becomes an internal alias); every other type resolves via officials.governing_body_id + the FIX-470 `currentGoverningBodyMembers()` predicate. - [x] 🟠 M — **Graph group route fails open to zero donors on slow queries — donor-less group renders under load** — Live-confirmed on prod 2026-06-04 during the FIX-490 browser click-through: /api/graph/group?governingBody=senate returned 51 nodes/50 edges on a warm hit, then 1 node/0 edges (meta.donorCount=0, totalDonatedUsd=0, memberCount=100 — members resolve fine) on identical queries minutes later; a cache-busted no-store run took 8.5s wall and still returned the clean-looking zero-donor payload. Fix directions (investigate, then pick): (a) surface per-step query errors instead of continuing (grep Vercel logs for [withDbTimeout] to confirm the swallow site), distinguish 'no donors' from 'donor fetch failed' in the response, client retries or shows a degraded-state hint; (b) perf-fix the underlying aggregation (pre-aggregated per-gb donor rollup / materialization per the documented pattern) so the timeout window closes. - [x] 🟠 S — **Browse W2 defect: view saved from /graph is invisible in /graph's own saved-views rail (both-surface break)** — W2 decision 4 required saved views to render on BOTH /search and the graph sidebar mount, but a view saved from /graph (ADD ALL AS LIVE GROUP) appears in the /search rail yet NOT in /graph's own Saved views section (confirmed on prod 2026-07-07). Diagnosed: on /graph the SavedViewsRail IS mounted (SidebarBrowser → FocusTree browserSlot, which replaces the legacy GroupBrowser), so it is not a mount gap. Root cause is that each SavedViewsRail instance owns an independent fetch + items state with no shared invalidation — /search fetches once on mount, the graph mount refreshes only via a local savedNonce — and the GET /api/graph/custom-groups client fetch sets no cache:no-store, so an in-session re-fetch after a save can serve a stale browser-cached list. Fix: a shared saved-views refresh bus (module-level subscribe/notify) that every mounted rail re-reads on any save/delete, plus cache:no-store on the read, so a save on either surface is immediately visible on both. Verify the round-trip both directions (save on /graph → visible both; save on /search → visible both). FIX FIRST, own commit. Cross-ref [[FIX-763]] (saved views v2), [[FIX-762]] (sidebar mount). <!--id:FIX-770-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **Single opacity-resolver pipeline for hover/highlight/type-toggle + edge hover dollar labels** — G3. Four channels fight over dimming in visualizations/ForceGraph.tsx: hover sets attr('opacity'), the connection-style effect sets attr('stroke-opacity') + style('display'), SharedConnectionsBar highlight sets style('opacity') (inline style silently wins over attrs). Hover handlers are bound inside the [nodes,edges]-dep effect and close over a STALE edgeOpacityFn/connections — toggling a connection type doesn't rebuild them, so mouseleave restores last-load opacities. Rework: (a) one resolveNodeOpacity/resolveEdgeOpacity pair, ctx = {connection settings, hover target, shared-connections highlight, disabled types}; (b) applied through ONE channel — inline style('opacity') everywhere, style('display') for fully-disabled types computed in the same pass; (c) handlers read settings through a ref so no stale closures; (d) SharedConnectionsBar highlight + clear go through the same resolver. Spec: hover dims non-neighbors to 0.12; leaving hover restores CURRENT settings; toggle a type off/on then hover behaves identically to fresh load; pinned shared highlight survives hover and clears fully. Same commit: edge hover labels gain amounts — 'Type — $47.2M · 1,234 txs' via amountUsd + [[FIX-802]] txCount (tx segment omitted when txCount absent); vote edges keep type + date when occurredAt present. <!--id:FIX-807-->

- [x] 🟡 S — **Graph G4: force-sim never settles — cooling tune + spurious restart fix** — The force simulation never comes to rest — nodes drift indefinitely (2026-07-11 browser QA; clicking nodes is a moving target). Two causes: (1) default d3 cooling (alphaDecay 0.0228, alphaMin 0.001) takes ~5s+ per reheat; (2) the type-cluster effect restarts the sim at alpha 0.3 on every focusEntities identity change — GraphPage passes a fresh .filter() array every render, so unrelated re-renders reheat the layout. Fix: faster cooling (alphaDecay/alphaMin/velocityDecay) so layout settles within a few seconds of load or drag and still reheats on drag/expand; memoize focusEntities in GraphPage; guard the disabled type-cluster branch so it only restarts when tearing down an actually-mounted force. No other physics behavior change; Physics sliders keep working. <!--id:FIX-815-->
