### INFRASTRUCTURE & PERFORMANCE

- [x] 🟢 S — **Tighten enrichment_queue.entity_type CHECK + update stale schema comment** — Surfaced during [[FIX-381]] investigation gate. The [supabase/migrations/20260420030000_enrichment_queue.sql:11](../supabase/migrations/20260420030000_enrichment_queue.sql#L11) comment says `entity_type TEXT NOT NULL, -- 'proposal' | 'official'` but the queue legitimately holds four entity_type values in prod: `proposal` (137,479), `official` (8,362), `financial_entity` (2,580), `agency` (25). Writers exist for all four: `seed-backlog.ts` seeds FE industry-tag rows at line 487; the agency monthly enrichment pass adds agency rows; drain-apply.ts handles `tagCategory = queueRow.entity_type === 'financial_entity' ? 'industry' : 'topic'` at line 152. The data shape is correct; the comment is stale and there's no CHECK constraint to lock the discriminator going forward. Action: (1) add CHECK constraint `entity_type IN ('proposal','official','financial_entity','agency')`, (2) refresh the schema comment to enumerate all four. Also reconsider whether `entity_id` should migrate from TEXT to UUID — all current writers pass UUID-shaped strings, but TEXT widens the surface in a way that no consumer needs. Defer the TEXT→UUID change to a separate FIX if scope grows. Cross-ref [[FIX-381]] (extended FK-rewrite template surface, parent), audit doc `docs/audits/2026-05-25-fe-fk-surface-audit-prod.md`. <!--id:FIX-391-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Drift check for source_priority duplication (SQL function vs TS const)** — [[FIX-398]] deploy report (2026-05-26) flagged a maintenance hazard: source_priority is duplicated between the SQL function (supabase/migrations/<FIX-397-ts>_primary_source_materialization.sql) and a hand-inlined TS const in apps/civitics/app/api/attribution/[type]/[id]/route.ts. Required because PostgREST can't apply SQL functions inside .select() so the API route needs the priority logic client-side to sort/annotate xsr rows. If the priority list churns (new source added; existing source reranked) both sides must stay in sync or the API's is_primary flag will diverge from the materialized primary_source column. Today's mitigation: hand-sync + cross-reference comment. Options for a durable fix: (a) move source_priority to a shared TS module (packages/db/src/source-priority.ts) and expose it to SQL via a tiny RPC that the SQL trigger can CALL; awkward because plpgsql calling out for a constant is wasteful per-row. (b) hand-publish the priority list from a single shared TS file generated alongside db:types via a small codegen step; both SQL function and TS const are written from the same source. (c) extend pnpm db:types to emit a constant alongside the database types based on a marker in the migration. (d) accept the duplication, add a CI check that diffs the two and fails if they drift. Recommend (d) as cheapest — a small jest/vitest test that parses the SQL function's CASE branches and asserts they match the TS const. CI catches drift immediately; no codegen complexity. Cross-ref [[FIX-397]] (where the SQL function lives), [[FIX-398]] (where the TS const lives). <!--id:FIX-407-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **legistar city normalizer concatenates multi-word cities (austintexas) — ugly source labels** — Surfaced during [[FIX-399]] SourceBadge integration 2026-05-27. The legistar city normalizer concatenates multi-word city names without a separator, producing ugly source labels. Example: legistar:austintexas:matter renders as "Legistar Austintexas" via resolveSource()'s title-case-segment fallback ([[FIX-399]]). The registry correctly handles hyphenated city segments (legistar:new-york-city:* → "New York City") but bare-concatenated names have no word boundary to split on. Root cause is upstream in the legistar pipeline writer's client/city slug derivation — "austin" + "texas" should produce "austin-texas" (hyphen-separated) to match the new-york-city convention. Action: fix the city/client slug normalizer in the legistar pipeline (packages/data/src/pipelines/legistar/) to hyphen-separate multi-word city names, then a one-shot UPDATE migration to rewrite existing external_source_refs.source values (legistar:austintexas:* → legistar:austin-texas:*) and any metadata.legistar_client references. The SourceBadge/resolveSource layer is data-driven and intentionally should NOT special-case individual cities — the fix belongs at the writer so the slug is correct everywhere (xsr source, source_url construction, etc.). Low priority — cosmetic label only; the badge still renders and links correctly. Cross-ref [[FIX-399]] (where the ugly label surfaced). <!--id:FIX-411-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **governing_bodies anon RLS gates by is_active — only 53 of 255 bound rows visible to anon** — Surfaced during [[FIX-402]] verification 2026-05-27. After [[FIX-402]] dispatched 255 legistar bindings to public.governing_bodies, only 53 of the 255 bound rows are visible to anon callers — the table's anon RLS policy filters on is_active=true (the other 202 carry is_active=false). Latent today (no governing_bodies detail page renders attribution), but blocks the visible badge surface the moment such a page exists. Two valid resolutions: (a) audit whether those 202 rows should be is_active=true (the presence of legistar bindings suggests they're real bodies), or (b) relax the anon read policy if the columns aren't sensitive. Decide alongside whoever scopes the gb detail page (filed separately in this batch). Cross-ref [[FIX-402]]. <!--id:FIX-413-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **attribution-binding-rate.ts diag harness transient TypeError on head:true count path** — Surfaced during [[FIX-402]]/[[FIX-406]]/[[FIX-410]] verification 2026-05-27. The diag script packages/data/src/scripts/attribution-binding-rate.ts intermittently throws TypeError: <!--id:FIX-414-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **audit-summary-context paginates without ORDER BY — double-counts rows** — `src/scripts/audit-summary-context.ts` fetches all proposals + external_source_refs via `.range(from,to)` with **no `.order()`**. PostgREST/Postgres range pagination over an unordered scan returns rows in an unstable order across pages, so rows get double-counted (and others skipped) — even on a static table, and badly under concurrent writes. During [[FIX-435]] it reported prod `full_summary=1261` / `openstates total=5926` when exact head-counts were 538 / ~2,900 (~2.3x inflation); an earlier run mid-write reported `full_summary=2`. The script's absolute per-source numbers are unreliable. Fix: add a stable `.order('id')` (or keyset-paginate on id) to every paginated select in the audit, and re-baseline the docs/audits numbers. Until fixed, treat its counts as approximate and prefer SQL head-counts (`count(*) filter (...)`) for ground truth. <!--id:FIX-448-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **attribution-coverage: prod financial_entities total count returns 0 (bound > total)** — In `diag:attribution-coverage` against prod, the `financial_entities` row shows `bound=98569 total=0 0.0%` — bound exceeding total is impossible. The exact head-count (`select('id', { count: 'exact', head: true })`) over the ~1.1M-row `financial_entities` table returns count 0/null on prod, almost certainly the documented ~8s role-read / PostgREST gateway cap (see [[project_prod_oom_jsonb_rollup]]) timing out the COUNT and returning null→0. NOT a transient fetch-failed (so the FIX-414 `withRetry` correctly does not retry it). Local is fine (total=1147991). Fix options: (a) use a planner-estimate count for huge tables (`count: 'estimated'` / 'planned'), or (b) route the exact count via direct pg.Client like lib/heavy-rebuild.ts. Surfaced during FIX-448/FIX-414 (Wave 1 diagnostic-tooling correctness). Read-only diagnostic, no data impact — just an inaccurate printed total. <!--id:FIX-450-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Codify out-of-band xsr_public_read policy (drift; not in any migration)** — external_source_refs is anon-readable on local AND prod via an xsr_public_read USING(true) policy that exists in NO migration — 20260421000007_stage1_08_rls.sql:49 states xsr has RLS enabled with no policies. Out-of-band drift: a fresh DB rebuild loses it and re-breaks request-path attribution (packages/db/src/attribution.ts:59 createServerClient; apps/civitics/app/api/attribution/[type]/[id]/route.ts:120 createPublicClient). This is the durable form of the FIX-398 admin-client workaround. Fix: CREATE POLICY xsr_public_read idempotently in a migration. All 19 writers are createAdminClient. Staged un-applied in supabase/migrations/20260531000000_rls_hardening_safe.sql. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-05-31-rls-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-453-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **pipeline_state plum_book read silently zero on institutions page (RLS on, no policy)** — apps/civitics/app/institutions/[id]/page.tsx:488 reads pipeline_state key=plum_book_state via createServerClient (RLS-respecting). pipeline_state is RLS-on with no policy, so anon silently gets zero rows (verified: prod anon REST => 0 rows) and the plum last-change date never renders (fails-open via maybeSingle). A blanket policy is unsafe — pipeline_state also holds kill_switches, cost_config_overrides, watermarks, manual_trigger_*. Fix: key-scoped SELECT policy USING(key=plum_book_state) (exposes only that benign date) OR switch the single read to createAdminClient (page is already force-dynamic). Same silent-zero class as FIX-398. Narrow policy staged un-applied in supabase/migrations/20260531000000_rls_hardening_safe.sql. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-05-31-rls-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-454-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Flip SECURITY DEFINER views to security_invoker (proposal_comment_stats safe; institutions decision-gated)** — Linter security_definer_view on two app views. proposal_comment_stats: flip to security_invoker is SAFE — identical rowset because the view filters is_deleted=false and civic_comments anon policy is USING(is_deleted=false); staged in supabase/migrations/20260531000000_rls_hardening_safe.sql. institutions (UNION gb+agencies): flip is DECISION-GATED — under the current is_active gate it would HIDE the 202 inactive Legistar gbs and 404 their /institutions/[id] detail pages (6 anon consumers, detail pages do not filter is_active); only safe under Option A USING(true). Held commented in supabase/migrations/20260531000100_rls_isactive_decision.sql. (geography_columns/geometry_columns are PostGIS exceptions, left as-is.) Cross-ref [[FIX-413]]. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-05-31-rls-audit.md. <!--id:FIX-455-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Expand tests.yml into a full push/PR CI gate (typecheck + lint + build + all tests)** — Surfaced during [[FIX-407]] CI wiring. The repo had NO push/PR CI before FIX-407 — all five GHA workflows (nightly, audit, sync-canary-check, platform-snapshot, rebuild-entity-connections) are scheduled data-pipeline crons. FIX-407 added .github/workflows/tests.yml (push to main + PR) but it only runs the single @civitics/data drift-guard test (pnpm --filter @civitics/data test). Gaps with no pre-merge gate: (1) TypeScript typecheck — Vercel builds strict TS, so type breaks only surface at deploy; (2) lint; (3) pnpm build (turbo); (4) the other node:test suite, packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.test.ts, is still hand-run only. Action: extend tests.yml (or split into ci.yml) to run turbo typecheck + lint + build and all tsx --test suites on push/PR, and broaden the @civitics/data test script (or add per-package test scripts) so indiv.test.ts is covered. Cross-ref [[FIX-407]] (added the workflow), [[FIX-392]]. <!--id:FIX-458-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Stand up the ESLint toolchain so CI lint can be a blocking gate** — Follow-up to FIX-458. 'turbo run lint' is wired into tests.yml as a non-blocking (continue-on-error: true) step because the lint toolchain was never installed: no package declares an eslint devDependency, there is no .eslintrc / eslint.config.* anywhere, the packages/* lint scripts run a bare 'eslint src/' (binary not found), and the Next apps' 'next lint' drops into interactive ESLint-setup (which hangs/cancels in CI). To promote lint to blocking: (1) add eslint + a shared flat config (e.g. in @civitics/config or root) and wire each package's lint script to it; (2) add eslint.config.mjs / .eslintrc to apps/civitics + apps/social so 'next lint' is non-interactive; (3) clear whatever findings surface; (4) flip the lint step in .github/workflows/tests.yml from continue-on-error: true to a blocking step. Until then the lint step is informational only and does NOT fail the gate. See [[FIX-458]]. <!--id:FIX-459-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **fixes:sync can record off-trunk commits as shipped (git log --all scans unmerged branches)** — Root cause of the 2026-05-31 silent-zero-sweep-tail incident: `scanCommits()` in `scripts/fixes-sync.mjs` uses `git log --all`, which scans **every ref including unmerged PR branches**. A sync run (b68c310f) on a clone where PR#1's branch was reachable wrote done.log rows for FIX-425/428/429/430/431/432 against branch SHAs that `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main` reports false for — the buggy code (search date-range no-op, graph edge truncation, institution plum date, notify-followers fan-out, ai-tagger cost gate) sat unshipped on prod while done.log + the archive both said 'done'. The only signal was the open PR. **Guard (this FIX, FIX-461):** (1) `--check` now fails when any currently-completed FIX-ID's only resolvable commit SHAs are off-trunk (detection — would have failed CI on b68c310f's push-to-main); uses 'any completing SHA is an ancestor' semantics so the FIX-435 orphan-plus-landed duplicate is not a false positive, and skips sentinel SHAs (backfill/reopen) + shallow-clone-missing objects. (2) `scanCommits()` filters out completions whose commit is not an ancestor of trunk (origin/main, fallback main) so premature rows can't be written in the first place; falls back to current behavior when no trunk ref resolves. (3) `pnpm fixes:check` wired into `.github/workflows/tests.yml` (fetch-depth:0) as a blocking job. Tests added in test-fixes-sync.mjs for the pure decision core. <!--id:FIX-461-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Nightly fec-phase times out every Sunday (FEC bulk + IRS 990 exceeds 120-min budget)** — Recurring, confirmed against prod data_sync_log + GHA run logs. The nightly-sync `fec-phase` job (.github/workflows/nightly.yml) is cancelled at ~120m wall-clock **every Sunday** — runs 2026-05-17, 2026-05-24, 2026-05-31 all hit the timeout-minutes:120 ceiling; weekdays finish in 10-45m. Sunday is the heavy day (FEC bulk individual donations + IRS 990). At kill time (run 26705214757, 08:19 UTC) the job was mid indiv-to-committee upsert with many chunks failing `canceling statement due to statement timeout` (Postgres statement_timeout on per-chunk upserts), which drags wall-clock until the job SIGTERM. NOT OOM, NOT an upstream API error, NOT the FIX-430 ai-tagger code (that runs in enrichment-phase, which ran 0s — `needs: fec-phase` skipped it). Because fec-phase is cancelled, **enrichment-phase never runs on Sundays** (LittleSis, EDGAR weekly, USASpending, CourtListener, MV refreshes, AI taggers/summaries all skipped) → prod data progressively staler each week. This is the same failure mode FIX-292 split the job to fix, now recurring within the single fec-phase budget. **Secondary observability bug:** the Sunday kill writes ZERO data_sync_log rows — runNightlySync writes only a terminal (complete/partial) row, never a 'running' start-row, so mark-killed.ts finds no orphan and writes no synthetic nightly_killed row. Every Sunday kill is therefore classified `missing` (not `killed`) by the canary — confirmed: canary_check meta rows show missing_dates 2026-05-17 → 2026-05-24 → 2026-05-31. The canary DID alert (missing_count:1 each Sunday), so it was not silent. **Remediation options (needs decision, see also [[FIX-292]]):** (a) split fec-phase further so FEC-bulk-indiv and IRS-990 each get their own 120-min job; (b) raise/bypass statement_timeout for the bulk indiv/indiv-to-committee chunk upserts (route via direct pg.Client like lib/heavy-rebuild.ts) and/or enlarge chunk size to cut round-trips; (c) decouple Sunday enrichment-phase from fec-phase completion so enrichment still runs when FEC bulk overruns; (d) raise timeout-minutes (blunt). Also fix mark-killed/runNightlySync to write a 'running' start-row so kills register as `killed` not `missing`. Investigation: docs/audits or this session's notes; run logs gh run view 26705214757. <!--id:FIX-462-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Officials/votes pipeline burns ~27 min/run on prod statement_timeout failures** — Surfaced during [[FIX-462]] investigation (gh run 26705214757, fec-phase 2026-05-31). Before FEC bulk even starts, the daily Phase-1 Congress pipeline wastes ~27 min on queries that EVERY time fail with `canceling statement due to statement timeout`: the `promote-candidates` stage (runOfficialsPipeline) failed on ~165 consecutive officials at ~8s each (06:21→06:43, ~22 min), then the House/Senate vote `skip-check` loads (runVotesPipeline) timed out (06:43→06:48, ~5 min). Same root cause as FIX-462's indiv upserts and [[FIX-444]]: prod PostgREST role/statement_timeout (~8s) on queries that have grown slow against now-large tables (officials, votes). Each failure burns the full timeout with zero progress. FIX-462 (direct-pg for the indiv upserts) leaves fec-phase comfortably under its 120-min budget even with this 27-min sink present, so it is non-fatal post-FIX-462 — but it is pure waste and a latent re-timeout risk as data grows. Fix: route the promote-candidates upsert and the votes skip-check existence loads through a direct `pg.Client` with a raised SESSION statement_timeout (precedent: lib/heavy-rebuild.ts `selectDirect`/`runHeavyRebuild`), and/or make the skip-check a single batched existence query instead of per-roll loads. Investigation needed: identify WHY each query is slow (missing index? full scan?) — the timeout may be masking a query that should be sub-second with the right index. Files: packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/* (officials promote-candidates, votes skip-check). <!--id:FIX-463-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Parallel-session collision guards: pre-push fixes:check, session:check, per-FIX worktree workflow** — Three collision classes all stemmed from >1 writer touching one checkout's `.git` (VSCode git extension + an agent contending): a stranded PR whose FIXes `fixes:sync` recorded as shipped while the code sat unmerged ([[FIX-461]]), stale `index.lock`, and a corrupted `.git/config` line. Enforce a serialize-by-default model: (1) tracked `.githooks/pre-push` runs `pnpm fixes:check` and aborts the push on FIXES.md/done.log drift, wired via `core.hooksPath` from a root `prepare` script (no husky, no new deps); (2) `pnpm session:check` — read-only start-of-session reconciliation (fetch, ahead/behind, worktrees, unmerged branches, open PRs, fixes:check); (3) `pnpm session:worktree <id>` / `:done <id>` — per-FIX worktrees in a sibling dir outside the repo, both `.env.local` seeded to LOCAL Docker, `:done` refuses to remove unmerged work without --force. Primary VSCode checkout stays parked on `main` and agents never commit there; `main` advances only by ff from a rebased branch; `fixes:sync` runs only after merge. CLAUDE.md updated (first-step + Parallel sessions convention). Tooling+docs only, no app deploy. <!--id:FIX-464-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **fixes-sync trailer regex case-insensitivity let prose shadow the real Fixes: trailer** — The trailer regexes in scripts/fixes-sync.mjs were case-insensitive (`/im`), so a wrapped prose line beginning with a lowercase `fixes:` (e.g. `fixes:sync recorded ... (FIX-461)`) matched as a trailer and — non-global, first-match-wins, and appearing before the real `Fixes:` line — shadowed it, attributing the commit to the wrong FIX. Surfaced when the [[FIX-464]] commit body mentioned `fixes:sync` in prose: sync then wanted to log FIX-461 instead of FIX-464 and skip flipping FIX-464's checkbox. Fix: make fixesRe/closesRe/verifiedRe/verifiedPerFixRe case-SENSITIVE (git trailer keys are always capitalized). Regression test added in test-fixes-sync.mjs. <!--id:FIX-465-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Split tests.yml so docs-only commits skip the unit gate but still hit the fixes-integrity guard** — Every push to main triggers tests.yml, whose expensive unit job (pnpm install + typecheck + lint + @civitics/data suite, several minutes) runs even for pure docs/status commits (chore(fixes): sync … touching only done.log + FIXES.md). The autonomous loop pushes these in tight bursts, so the heavy job spins up just to be cancelled by cancel-in-progress. Fix: move the cheap fixes-integrity guard (FIX-461) into its own .github/workflows/fixes-integrity.yml (no path filter — must fire on the docs-only commits it polices), and add paths-ignore: ['docs/**'] to tests.yml on.push + on.pull_request so the unit job skips docs-only changes (mixed docs+code commits still run it). Can't use a single workflow-level paths-ignore because that would also skip the guard on the exact docs-only done.log commits that are the FIX-461 failure mode. Caveat: if branch protection with required checks is later enabled, a paths-ignore-skipped workflow reports no status, so a required 'unit' check would block docs-only merges unless a skipped→success companion is added. <!--id:FIX-466-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **FHWA (and future late-added regulations_gov agencies) stay primary_source NULL — FIX-410 seed was one-shot** — Surfaced during [[FIX-415]] prod verification 2026-06-02. After FIX-415 bound the 8 sourceless agencies via Federal Register, exactly one prod agency remains primary_source NULL: FHWA (id e1578a8c…), which DOES carry source_ids->>'regulations_gov_agency_id'='FHWA'. It should have been bound to regulations_gov by [[FIX-410]], but FIX-410's xsr seed lived only in migration 20260527000000 as a one-time INSERT…SELECT — agencies created AFTER that migration applied (FHWA is a regulations.gov insert-on-miss row, name=='FHWA') never get an external_source_refs row, so rebuild_all_primary_sources finds no winner. Out of FIX-415's scope (that FIX targets agencies with NO source signal; FHWA has one). Durable fix: mirror the FIX-415 pipeline pattern for regulations_gov — in agency-enrichment Pass 2 (or the regulations.gov pipeline), idempotently seed an external_source_refs(source='regulations_gov', external_id=source_ids->>'regulations_gov_agency_id', entity_type='agency') row for any agency carrying the key but missing the xsr, then refresh_primary_source_for_entities('agency', ids). regulations_gov is priority 5 so it correctly wins over federal_register (9999) for any agency that has both. Cross-ref [[FIX-410]], [[FIX-415]]. <!--id:FIX-467-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **fixes-integrity GHA + pre-push false-red on every fix commit** — The two-push standard loop (Fixes: code commit, then a separate fixes:sync status commit) guarantees the code-commit push is transiently out of sync with FIXES.md/done.log. fixes-integrity.yml ran the full `fixes-sync.mjs --check` on every push, so it red-failed on every fix commit, going green only on the follow-up sync commit (alternating failure/success in the run history). Same false-positive in .githooks/pre-push, which ran the full `pnpm fixes:check` on every push. Fix: added a `--check-trunk` mode that enforces ONLY the trunk-ancestry guard (the dangerous [[FIX-461]] silent-zero-sweep-tail case) and ignores drift. CI now splits by event — pull_request runs full `--check` (gates unsynced merges, code+sync evaluated together), push-to-main runs `--check-trunk`. pre-push hook points at new `fixes:check:trunk` script. Drift is still caught where it matters (PRs). Files: scripts/fixes-sync.mjs, .github/workflows/fixes-integrity.yml, .githooks/pre-push, package.json. Split of [[FIX-461]]/[[FIX-466]]. <!--id:FIX-469-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **PostgREST max_rows sweep — .limit(>1000) call sites silently capped at 1000** — ~10 call sites pass .limit(N>1000) but an explicit limit does NOT override the PostgREST max_rows=1000 server ceiling (confirmed in [[FIX-470]]: a .limit(2000) party-balance query returned exactly 1000 of 1921 rows). Per-site triage: (a) completeness-required (aggregations/sums/member lists) → .range() pagination with a stable .order() on a unique key, or move to an RPC; (b) top-N with ORDER BY (the cap returns the true top 1000) → honest .limit(1000) + comment; (c) provably <1000 → honest limit + comment. Per-site classification table in the commit body. Cross-ref [[FIX-470]]. <!--id:FIX-476-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **session:worktree:done leaves merged artifact-only worktrees on disk** — `pnpm session:worktree:done` died half-done tearing down a *merged* worktree: gitignored build artifacts (node_modules/.next, the seeded .env.local copies) make `git worktree remove` treat the tree as dirty and refuse without --force, so the safety check passed but removal aborted and the dir was left on disk (surfaced by the Wave 1 [[FIX-472]]..476 session, whose fix-wave1 worktree stranded). Fix splits the two meanings of --force in done(): after the unmerged-safety gate passes, an empty `git status --porcelain` (porcelain ignores gitignored files) means only disposable ignored artifacts remain → auto `--force` removal; a non-empty porcelain (real tracked edits or untracked files) still refuses without an explicit --force. Part of the [[FIX-462]] parallel-session collision-guard line. <!--id:FIX-479-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **session:worktree:done leaves dir on disk when git worktree remove --force itself fails** — Distinct residual surfaced tearing down the [[FIX-477]]/[[FIX-479]] worktree: after [[FIX-479]] correctly auto-forces removal of a merged, porcelain-clean (artifact-only) tree, `git worktree remove --force` can ITSELF fail on a fully-installed worktree — a transient file lock on the freshly-built node_modules/.next (esbuild service, Defender scan) returns non-zero. git deregisters the worktree (it drops from `git worktree list`, .git pointer gone) but leaves the physical dir on disk, so done() dies after the unrecoverable remove and the dir strands (observed on the FIX-477 session: dir survived, `git worktree remove` then reported 'not a working tree'). core.longpaths=true is already set, so this is a lock/rmdir race, not the MAX_PATH limit. Fix: in done(), when `git worktree remove --force` returns non-zero, fall back to a manual recursive delete of wtDir (rm -rf / `cmd /c rmdir /s /q`) + `git worktree prune`, and only die if the manual delete also fails. The scratch tests in [[FIX-479]] passed because their node_modules was a single shallow file, not a real install — so add a test that installs (or simulates a deep/locked tree) before teardown. <!--id:FIX-480-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **@civitics/data tsc debt — ai-tagger TS2345 + ungated pipeline typecheck** — Full `tsc --noEmit` over @civitics/data reported exactly ONE error (2026-06-04): ai-tagger.ts classifyProposal required title:string / metadata:Record<string,string> but the paged ProposalRow is title:string|null / metadata:Record<string,unknown> (TS2345). Fixed by widening the param + coalescing (title ?? '(untitled)', String(metadata?.agency_id)). A `typecheck` script already exists in packages/data/package.json. NOTE: the @civitics/data pipeline typecheck is NOT gated in the pre-push hook or CI — whether to add it is a separate call (report-only). <!--id:FIX-483-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Gate @civitics/data typecheck in pre-push** — The @civitics/data package has no build step in CI (it runs as standalone tsx scripts, never bundled), so a TypeScript error in a pipeline or script only surfaced at runtime when that pipeline ran. Added a `pnpm --filter @civitics/data typecheck` step to .githooks/pre-push, ahead of the existing fixes:check:trunk gate, with the same fail-fast/abort-on-nonzero behavior (bypassable with --no-verify for genuine WIP). The package is clean post-[[FIX-483]]. Landed alongside [[FIX-482]]. <!--id:FIX-485-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Worktree QoL: session:worktree --install flag + cd allow-rules** — Two ergonomics fixes for the per-FIX worktree flow. (a) `pnpm session:worktree <id> --install` now runs `pnpm install` in the fresh tree (shared content-addressed store hardlinks, ~40-50s) so the worktree is buildable out of the box instead of failing tsx/build until a manual install; the printed landing recipe reflects whether deps are installed and documents the `supabase --workdir <wt> migration up --local` note for applying a worktree's migrations to shared local Docker. (b) Added `Bash(cd …/civitics-worktrees/**)` allow-rules (relative + absolute forms) to .claude/settings.local.json so cd into a worktree no longer prompts, matching the existing Edit/Read allow-rules for that path. Cross-ref [[FIX-479]], [[FIX-480]]. <!--id:FIX-486-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **fips uniqueness guard for state-level jurisdictions** — Schema guard to prevent the [[FIX-482]] / [[FIX-487]] dup-jurisdiction class from recurring. Partial unique index on jurisdictions(fips_code) WHERE type IN ('state','federal_district','unincorporated_territory') — the three state-level row kinds that must be 1-per-fips. Deliberately excludes county/district/city/precinct etc. which legitimately repeat a fips (e.g. DC county 11001, Guam county 66010, the DC Delegate District at fips 11). Ships LAST and only when clean: created/applied only after the DC ([[FIX-482]]) and territory ([[FIX-487]]) merges have landed on BOTH envs, since a residual dup on prod would fail the CREATE UNIQUE INDEX mid-push. Cross-ref [[FIX-482]], [[FIX-487]]. <!--id:FIX-488-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **proposals introduced_at ORDER BY has no index -> full-sort timeout** — The PostgREST query behind the /proposals list (all+newest), the newest carousel, and the home-page fallback runs ORDER BY introduced_at DESC NULLS LAST LIMIT/OFFSET with no selective WHERE. There is no index on proposals(introduced_at) anywhere in migration history, so every request full-scans + sorts the whole proposals table; as the table grew this began hitting statement_timeout on prod. Fix: btree index on (introduced_at DESC NULLS LAST) to match the sort exactly (a backward scan of a default ASC index gives NULLS FIRST and won't match). Cross-ref [[FIX-444]] (prod gateway/timeout caps). <!--id:FIX-502-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Pipeline buffer-churn: count→EXISTS, unindexed seq-scan counts, exact→estimated** — Pipeline-side buffer-churn (2026-06-06 audit; follow-up to [[FIX-503]]). (1) packages/data fec-bulk: pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts:1469 does 100× count(*) where an EXISTS/LIMIT 1 probe suffices — swap to EXISTS. (2) cron-run-postrun-extract + cron-run-anchor-verify run unindexed-source seq-scan count(*) per source — collapse into one GROUP BY RPC. (3) baseline-probe uses exact count where estimated (planner reltuples) is sufficient. These run against the cache-starved Pro Small and evict the request-path working set. Verify each fingerprint in pg_stat_statements before/after. <!--id:FIX-511-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Crawler hardening for /proposals facet space (robots /*?, sitemap, noindex, page clamp)** — ClaudeBot (Anthropic crawler) walked the faceted /proposals URL space plus /officials/[id] on 2026-06-06/07 — every ?status&type&agency&topics&sort&q&page combo is a unique cache-miss SSR — driving ~430K Vercel invocations on Jun 7 (656K of the 1M Hobby cap), ~10x Fluid CPU, a Supabase egress spike, and prod IOWait that timed out the littlesis pipeline. Durable fix so the temporary Vercel Firewall ClaudeBot challenge can later relax: robots.txt `Disallow: /*?` (param-free URLs stay crawlable); app/sitemap.ts (canonical bounded walk — ClaudeBot 404'd on /sitemap.xml; static pages + capped proposals/officials/institutions/jurisdictions, <=10k URLs, 5s per-query timeout, degrades to static-only); generateMetadata noindex,follow on any filtered/paginated /proposals view (bare stays indexable); page param clamped to [1,500]. Topics correctness bug surfaced by the same crawl is [[FIX-510]]. <!--id:FIX-513-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **20260420040000 fresh-replay fails: pg_trgm SCHEMA public vs extensions.gin_trgm_ops** — On a fresh local replay, 20260420040000_reconcile_prod_drift.sql declared `CREATE EXTENSION pg_trgm SCHEMA public` (line 15) while its trigram GIN index DDL references `extensions.gin_trgm_ops`, so replays failed with 'operator class extensions.gin_trgm_ops does not exist' (0001 lands pg_trgm in public). Changed the declaration to SCHEMA extensions and added an idempotent `ALTER EXTENSION pg_trgm SET SCHEMA extensions` guard before the index block, so the file is consistent regardless of where 0001 put the extension. Replay-only in effect — prod has this migration recorded as applied and never re-runs it (`db:push:prod` won't touch it), so there is no prod-side change. Editing this one existing migration was explicitly authorized (2026-06-07). Surfaced after a Docker reinstall wiped the local data vhdx and forced a from-zero replay; see [[FIX-516]] for the broader replay-from-zero breakage. <!--id:FIX-515-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Migration chain no longer replays from zero — 7 seed-dependent migrations hard-fail on empty DB** — A fresh `supabase start` / `supabase migration up --local` on an empty DB can no longer build the schema: 7 migrations RAISE EXCEPTION because they depend on pipeline-seeded data (governing bodies, jurisdictions, FE rows) absent on a from-zero replay. Files: 20260525051720_executive_seed_and_delegate_jurisdictions.sql ('Office of the President governing body not found — run seedGoverningBodies first'); 20260525053643_norton_dc_delegate_jurisdiction.sql; 20260525211524_backfill_dc_jurisdictions_fix383.sql; 20260525223048_backfill_territory_jurisdictions_fix385.sql; 20260525234504_officials_casing_dupe_merge_fix375.sql; 20260525235544_org_misclassified_indiv_merge_fix379.sql; 20260602000000_fix463_promote_candidate_collision_handling.sql. Consequence: local dev DBs must be rebuilt by cloning prod (schema+data dump+restore via supabase db dump), not by replay — done for the 2026-06-07 Docker-reinstall recovery. To restore replayability: guard each seed-dependent migration with `IF EXISTS (…seed…) THEN … ELSE RAISE NOTICE … END IF`, or move the seed data into idempotent SQL seeds that run before the dependent migrations. [[FIX-515]] fixed only the pg_trgm replay failure; these 7 were left untouched (out of scope, not authorized to edit). <!--id:FIX-516-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Sitemap covers only ~1000 of each large entity set (PostgREST 1000-row cap) while ?page crawling is blocked** — app/sitemap.ts (shipped in [[FIX-513]]) uses the publishable client, so each segment SELECT is capped at PostgREST max_rows=1000 (same cap as [[FIX-510]]). With robots.txt now blocking ?page= (also [[FIX-513]]), the sitemap is the primary crawler discovery path, yet it lists only the most recent ~1000 proposals (of 78k), ~1000 officials (of 27k), and ~1000 jurisdictions (of 10k); institutions (716) are fully covered. Older entity detail pages are still reachable via on-page links but not via the sitemap. If SEO indexing of the long tail matters, widen coverage by paginating the sitemap with .range() loops up to the 10k URL ceiling, or generate it server-side with a direct pg client / admin client (note: decision was publishable-only for the initial ship). Low priority — clean detail URLs remain crawlable; this only affects discoverability breadth. <!--id:FIX-517-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Pin Supabase CLI version to stop bundled-image major bumps outrunning config** — CLI 2.78.1 silently bumped its bundled DB image to postgres:17.6.1.084 while config.toml still pinned major_version=15, crash-looping local on a PG15-volume/PG17-image mismatch (multi-hour prod-clone recovery, 2026-06-08; local now on PG17 matching prod, commit 66d8413f). Pin the supabase CLI version (scoop hold / package-manager lock) or document a don't-auto-update note + a startup check comparing the bundled image major against config major_version, so the next bump (PG18+) fails loud at install rather than silently at stack-cycle. See reference: local DB recovery notes. <!--id:FIX-530-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Harden data-layer preload reads against silent-zero / log-and-continue degradation** — Shared rowsOrThrow/selectAllOrThrow helpers in @civitics/db (packages/db/src/read-helpers.ts); migrate the confirmed footguns — littlesis/writer.ts preloadKnownLittleSisIds (silent-zero, no error check: a dead local Kong made it return an empty Map and the pipeline re-matched all 440k entities as if from scratch), congress/votes.ts buildOfficialMaps (console.error + continue with partial maps, plus an unchunked .in() that can exceed the ~200-id Kong cap), enrichment/seed-backlog.ts fetchAll (console.error + break returns partial silently). Same class as FIX-422 (loadSnapshot county skip); surfaced by the FIX-294 local rerun. Full data-layer read audit doc at docs/audits/2026-06-09-read-degradation-audit.md. Fail-loud is the intended behavior change: transient gateway errors now throw (canary/reaper records, next run retries) instead of silently degrading to everything-unmatched. <!--id:FIX-545-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **CI guard: fail on new silent-zero PostgREST preload reads** — scripts/check-silent-reads.mjs greps packages/data/src + packages/db/src for the footgun shape — a `const { data }` / `const { data: x }` destructure with no `error` in the same destructure on a .from().select() read (excluding .single()/.maybeSingle()/.rpc()/writes) — prints offenders file:line and exits 1. Opt-out via a `// reads-ok: <reason>` line comment for deliberate cases. Wired as `pnpm check:reads` and a step in .github/workflows/tests.yml so the [[FIX-545]] sweep can't regrow as new pipelines are added. <!--id:FIX-546-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Official stats aggregation reads are dead/truncated — move to GROUP BY RPCs** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-545]] audit. Three sites build per-official vote/donor context with raw PostgREST reads that are broken in practice: (1) tags/ai-tagger.ts runOfficialAiTagger lines ~349-351 selects financial_relationships.official_id + donor_type — columns that DO NOT EXIST post-cutover, so the read 400s on every nightly run and is silently swallowed (officials tagged with zero financial context since cutover; FIX-426 class); its votes .in(officialIds) is also unchunked (>200-id URL cap) and its result truncates at the 1,000-row PostgREST cap. (2) ai-summaries/index.ts fetchOfficials votes/donor block — same dead official_id column on financial_relationships, same truncation on votes. (3) enrichment/queue.ts aggregateOfficialStats — correct to_id/from_id columns but unchunked .in() with ALL active official ids and per-chunk results way past 1,000 rows (931k votes / 28.6k officials). Durable fix is the FIX-298 shape: one GROUP BY RPC per axis (get_official_donor_rollup already exists; add a vote-count rollup or reuse get_official_bipartisan_stats) + Map lookup. Behavior change: officials will suddenly get real vote/donor context, so tag/summary output shifts — verify a sample before/after. Deliberately NOT folded into FIX-545 because making these fail loud without the rewrite would hard-break the nightly (the FR read errors 100% of the time). <!--id:FIX-547-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **next.config.mjs serverExternalPackages unrecognized on Next 14.2** — apps/civitics/next.config.mjs uses the top-level serverExternalPackages key, which only exists from Next 15; on the app's Next 14.2 it is an unrecognized option (build warns 'Unrecognized key(s)') and the setting silently does nothing. The Next 14.2 spelling is experimental.serverComponentsExternalPackages. The four listed packages (mapbox-gl, @deck.gl/core, @deck.gl/layers, @deck.gl/mapbox) are still live dependencies of @civitics/maps and are browser-only WebGL libs, so keeping them out of the server bundle remains correct — move the list under experimental.serverComponentsExternalPackages. <!--id:FIX-549-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **cron-run-anchor-verify selects non-existent financial_relationships.source — donor data sections silently degraded** — Found during the FIX-511 count-site triage (2026-06-11): financial_relationships has NO source column — the pipeline source lives in metadata->>'source'. The FIX-511 pass fixed the COUNT sites (the per-source PostgREST exact head-count loops were printing 42703 errors, replaced by a direct-pg GROUPING SETS on metadata->>'source'), but the DATA sections of cron-run-anchor-verify.ts still list source in their select() column strings: the Musk outflow top-20, the Simons→DCCC and Stryker→DCCC relationship dumps, and the Trump inflow paged aggregate (plus their byType keys read r.source). PostgREST 42703s the whole select, so those anchors render as empty/missing rather than failing loud. Fix: alias the column as source:metadata->>source in each select string (PostgREST JSON-operator select syntax) and keep the byType keys unchanged. Cross-ref [[FIX-511]] [[FIX-236]] [[FIX-240]]. <!--id:FIX-551-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **check:reads CI red — fec-bulk senate-coverage probe missing error check** — FIX-511's LIMIT-1 donation probe destructured data without error, so a gateway blip mid-loop silently undercounted the FIX-178 Senate coverage metric and tripped the FIX-546 check:reads gate. Fixed by checking error, counting probe failures, and flagging them on the printed coverage line. <!--id:FIX-562-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Jurisdictions /[id] SSG times out on Vercel, errors deploys** — Recurring: Vercel production builds intermittently FAIL with 'Static page generation for /jurisdictions/<uuid> is still timing out after 3 attempts' (Next 30s SSG worker cap) while prerendering jurisdictions/[id] against the cache-starved prod DB (256MB shared_buffers, ~54% hit ratio — see prod buffer baseline). When it trips, turbo exits 1 and the WHOLE deploy errors, so unrelated changes never go live until a manual redeploy clears the flake (hit during the FIX-579/580 investigations PR2 deploy 2026-06-14; redeploy succeeded). jurisdictions/[id] is '● SSG' (prerendered) — unlike the force-dynamic investigations routes — so it does heavy per-page DB work at build time. Fix options: apply the CLAUDE.md generateStaticParams discipline to jurisdictions/[id] (try/catch → [], Promise.race 5s timeout, limit ≤50, publishable key) so it falls back to on-demand ISR instead of failing the build; and/or raise staticPageGenerationTimeout in next.config; and/or trim the per-page build-time query. Until fixed, every deploy is one slow-jurisdiction-query away from erroring. <!--id:FIX-581-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **session:worktree installs deps by default (--no-install to skip)** — `pnpm session:worktree <id>` now runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline` by default (was opt-in via `--install`); pass `--no-install` to skip. A fresh worktree has no node_modules and agents can't `pnpm install` by hand, so default-on is what makes the tree usable. `--frozen-lockfile` keeps the guardrail intact — install provably can't add a package or mutate the lockfile; new third-party code still only enters via `pnpm add`/`npm i`/`yarn add` (still denied). The blanket `Bash(pnpm install*)` deny in settings.local.json was downgraded to `ask`. <!--id:FIX-582-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Platform Costs egress: device-filter defect + honest NIC-proxy relabel** — The dashboard 'Database Egress' read 101% of the Pro 250 GB limit (~272 GB MTD). Root cause: egress is sourced from the Prometheus counter node_network_transmit_bytes_total (raw NIC transmit on ens5), an UPPER BOUND that includes replication, PITR/WAL, and intra-AWS traffic — NOT Supabase billable egress (bytes to clients). Two fixes: (1) parsePrometheusText summed ALL device= rows with no filter (latent — prod exposes only ens5 today, but lo/docker/veth/br/cni would inflate on other hosts); added labelExcludes to PromMatch and a NETWORK_VIRTUAL_DEVICES exclude list, plus a parser unit test. (2) Relabeled the metric honestly: snapshot writes egress with source='estimated' (gray '~ Est.' badge), platform_limits display_label → 'Egress (≈ NIC transmit, upper bound)', and computePlatformUsagePayload excludes source='estimated' rows from any_critical/any_warning so it no longer trips a false red banner. Investigation confirmed Supabase exposes NO public Management API endpoint for billable egress (api.supabase.com/v1 probed 2026-06-19: every egress variant 404s). Kill-switch wiring deferred — see [[FIX-603]]. <!--id:FIX-602-->
- [x] 🟢 M — **Platform Costs: edge-triggered email alerts on metric threshold crossings** — The platform-snapshot cron emailed only on kill-switch auto-trip FLIPS. Added per-metric threshold alerts: new platform_alert_state table (metric_key PK, last_status, last_alerted_at; RLS deny-all + service_role grants mirroring supabase_prometheus_state) debounces so the 10-min cron emails ONCE on escalation (healthy→warning, healthy→critical, warning→critical), updating state silently on de-escalation/unchanged. New renderMetricAlertEmail() in lib/email.ts reuses renderNotificationEmail with a /dashboard?tab=operations CTA. Gated behind the existing EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED + ADMIN_EMAIL (sendEmail no-ops without RESEND_API_KEY/RESEND_FROM). source='estimated' rows are skipped so the NIC egress proxy from [[FIX-602]] never pages. PREREQ (Vercel dashboard env, manual): EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED/ADMIN_EMAIL/RESEND_API_KEY/RESEND_FROM must be set in Vercel prod env; RESEND_FROM=alerts@civitics.com needs a verified Resend domain. <!--id:FIX-604-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Vercel wiring: set platform_plan.vercel=pro so the card renders against Pro limits** — pipeline_state.platform_plan carried only {"supabase":"pro"}, so the Platform Costs card rendered Vercel metrics against FREE limits (e.g. origin_transfer_bytes vs 10 GB instead of the Pro 1 TB). Added an idempotent data-action script (data:set-vercel-plan-pro[:prod]) using upgradeServicePlan(db,'vercel','pro'), which spread-merges so the supabase override is preserved → {"vercel":"pro","supabase":"pro"}. Runtime data action: run local AND prod separately. PREREQ (Vercel dashboard env, manual): confirm VERCEL_API_TOKEN + VERCEL_TEAM_ID (the only two the code reads, not VERCEL_TOKEN/VERCEL_PROJECT_ID) are set in Vercel prod env. Verify post-deploy: curl the platform-snapshot cron, then platform_usage WHERE service='vercel' should flip to source='api', non-zero; the card shows origin_transfer_bytes vs Pro 1 TB. <!--id:FIX-605-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Vercel usage metrics never populate — VERCEL_API_TOKEN lacks account-level usage/billing scope** — Surfaced verifying [[FIX-605]]. After setting platform_plan.vercel='pro' the card renders the correct Pro 1 TB limit, but every vercel platform_usage row is EMPTY because getVercelUsage() (packages/db/src/vercel-usage.ts) can't fetch. Probed prod 2026-06-19 with the .env.local VERCEL_API_TOKEN (valid — /v2/user = civitics-platform, defaultTeamId=team_eOKy21...): /v1/usage → 400 invalid_time_range for EVERY range (ISO month-to-date, 24h; epoch → invalid_from_date); /v1/billing/charges → 404 'Plan not found'; and tellingly GET /v9/projects?teamId=team_eOKy21... → {"projects":[]} (empty) while /v6/deployments?projectId=prj_RY4...&teamId=... DOES return data. That empty project list + account-level 400/404s points to a PROJECT-scoped token (or one missing billing/usage read), not a code bug and not a missing Vercel dashboard env var. Fix path: Craig generates a team/account-scoped Vercel token with usage+billing read (Vercel dashboard → Account/Team Settings → Tokens, full scope), set it as VERCEL_API_TOKEN in BOTH .env.local* and the Vercel prod dashboard env; if /v1/usage stays invalid_time_range with a full-scope token, the endpoint is likely deprecated — migrate getVercelUsage to the current Vercel usage API. Until then vercel rows stay empty (pre-existing; not a [[FIX-605]] regression). <!--id:FIX-606-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Seed reads prod password from env + redacts conn-string in logs** — data:seed:franklin took the prod password as a --db-url CLI arg, so it landed in terminal scrollback + shell history + the session log in plaintext. Mirror the db:push:prod wrapper: when --allow-prod is set, read SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD (+ cached session-pooler URL) from .env.local.prod and build the connection string inside the script; password never needs to appear on the CLI again. --db-url stays a fallback. Redact the password (:pass@ -> :****@) anywhere the script logs/echoes/errors a URL. New invocation: pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:seed:franklin -- --allow-prod. Independent of the prod password rotation (the cleanup for the already-leaked value). <!--id:FIX-611-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Consolidate /jurisdictions/[id] N+1 into get_jurisdiction_page RPC** — 2026-06-21 prod incident: a broad crawl hit hundreds of distinct /jurisdictions/[id] URLs; each render fired ~11+ Supabase REST calls (parent, boundary, children, institutions, officials, proposals, meetings, initiatives, activity, spending x2), overwhelming the 60-conn pool (522s -> cascading 504s). ISR gave zero protection (every crawled id is a unique cache key -> full cache-miss render). Fix: single SECURITY INVOKER get_jurisdiction_page(uuid) RPC returns one jsonb payload with every section; page calls it once. Safe fallback: on RPC error, resolve the base jurisdiction row so the shell renders; notFound only when the jurisdiction truly doesn't exist. <!--id:FIX-634-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Cap officials donor-rollup fallback: never run the 50k-row live scan on a public render** — On /officials/[id], when official_donor_rollup_mv has no rows for the official but donations exist (stale/empty MV — exactly what the failed Sunday refresh left), the EXISTS-gated fallback ran a live fetchAllRows aggregation over financial_relationships up to 50,000 rows plus chunked financial_entities fetches, per public render. A stale MV turned every funded official's page into that monster, a co-factor in the 2026-06-21 connection-pool exhaustion. Fix: remove the unbounded live scan from the request path; when the MV is empty, read a small bounded top-N (<=50 rows) marked partial, or show a 'donor data refreshing' state. MV stays the source of truth; a stale MV degrades gracefully instead of detonating. [[FIX-518]] [[FIX-634]] <!--id:FIX-635-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Direct-pg helper to refresh official_donor_rollup_mv (stops officials 50k-row live fallback)** — After the 2026-06-21 incident, the weekly full rebuild and nightly enrichment both died on the cache-starved Pro Small, leaving official_donor_rollup_mv stale → the officials page fell back to a 50k-row live scan. A .rpc() refresh can't fix it: a function-level SET statement_timeout doesn't raise the PostgREST/gateway cap (service_role ~8s, gateway ~100s), so refreshing the ~1M-row MV through PostgREST times out (the [[FIX-444]] hazard). New direct-pg helper src/scripts/refresh-donor-rollup-mv.ts (pnpm data:refresh-donor-mv) opens a direct postgres session with statement_timeout raised and runs REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY (non-blocking for readers; unique index already exists). Models buildDbUrl on run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts. Local refresh: 1,069,109 rows in ~42s. Prod refresh is a heavy MV op → run OFF-PEAK only (see no-heavy-prod-ops-during-active-hours). <!--id:FIX-636-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Per-IP edge rate-limit on SSR entity detail pages** — After the 2026-06-21 crawl incident: the edge limiter buckets only /api/search, /api/graph*, and write verbs — the SSR entity DETAIL pages (/jurisdictions/[id], /officials/[id], etc.) are unbucketed, so a single IP can walk unlimited distinct entity IDs, each triggering the full render fan-out. Adds an entity_pages per-IP sliding-window bucket (120/min) returning 429 at the edge before any Supabase query fires. Fail-open preserved; inert until UPSTASH_* provisioned in prod (Craig). Per-IP backstop only — distributed crawls need the Vercel/Cloudflare front door; render fan-out collapse is a separate FIX. <!--id:FIX-637-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **withDbTimeout sweep across all page-render queries** — P0 cost defense (2026-06-21 cost-resilience audit). Vercel bills provisioned fluid-compute memory for the entire time a function is blocked on I/O, so an unwrapped page-render PostgREST read against a slow/degraded DB makes the render hang and accrue GB-hours — and is the only defense against a low-and-slow crawl that evades the IP rate limiters. Wrapped every render-path `.from().select()` / `.rpc()` under apps/civitics/app/** (excl. api routes, client components, generateStaticParams, build-time metadata routes) in `withDbTimeout(query, 3000, "route:purpose")` from @/lib/supabase-check, mirroring the api/graph/* pattern. 178 reads across 27 files. Each call site verified to degrade gracefully on the timeout-null return. Guardrail is [[FIX-639]]. <!--id:FIX-638-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **CI guardrail: check:render-timeouts (no unwrapped render-path reads)** — Companion enforcement for [[FIX-638]]. New `scripts/check-render-timeouts.mjs` (`pnpm check:render-timeouts`) — a grep-scan guard modeled on check-silent-reads.mjs (FIX-546) — fails CI when a page-render PostgREST read under apps/civitics/app/** isn't wrapped in withDbTimeout. Wired as a blocking step in .github/workflows/tests.yml (lint is already eslint-8/CI-blocking, but this is a dedicated scan so it's precise and greppable). Excludes api routes, client components, generateStaticParams, and build-time metadata routes (sitemap/robots/manifest). Recognizes the generic-typed `withDbTimeout<T>(` wrap form. Strict-by-default escape hatch: `// db-timeout-exempt: <reason>`. Convention documented in apps/civitics/CLAUDE.md. <!--id:FIX-639-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Housekeeping: retire SESSION_LOG, clear orphan rebuild sync-log row, record 2b rebuild** — Retire docs/SESSION_LOG.md (dead since 2026-05-17) via git mv to docs/archive/; repoint all refs to docs/done.log + docs/FIXES.md. Clear the orphaned status='running' entity_connections_rebuild row (id 902a9844-…cd14) from the cancelled 11:17 rebuild — marked failed on prod. Record the 2b prod rebuild (run-rebuild-chunks-prod.ts, ~18min, exit 0, ~2.5M edges, 1,398,864 donation) in done.log. Companion to cost-resilience-audit-2026-06-21. <!--id:FIX-640-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Wire donor-rollup MV refresh into rebuild-entity-connections cron via direct-pg** — official_donor_rollup_mv was refreshed by refresh_official_donor_rollup_mv() via admin.rpc() (PostgREST, ~8s/100s caps) inside the nightly enrichment phase (packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts step 7 MV loop) — the exact capped path that times out and staled the MV (the officials-page donor section then drops into its 50k-row live-scan fallback, a cost driver). [[FIX-636]] added a working direct-pg refresher (pnpm data:refresh-donor-mv) but it was manual-only. Move the refresh off the capped nightly PostgREST path: add a data:refresh-donor-mv:ci step to rebuild-entity-connections.yml (08:00 UTC Sun+Wed, direct-pg, SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD already present) after the rebuild, and drop refresh_official_donor_rollup_mv from the nightly enrichment loop. Cadence drops daily to twice-weekly but the MV source (FEC donations in financial_relationships) only updates Sunday, so no staleness regression. Do NOT re-architect rebuild-entity-connections.ts ([[FIX-588]] owns that). <!--id:FIX-641-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Persist Vercel monthly spend (charges_total_usd) as a tracked platform metric** — getVercelUsage() parses charges_total_usd from /v1/billing/charges but platform-snapshot.ts only persisted the 9 quantity metrics, so there is no live Vercel spend figure on the Platform Costs card. Now writes vercel.monthly_spend_usd via updateUsage(...,'api') and adds the platform_limits threshold row (free+pro, unit=usd, warn 73%/crit 90% against a $30 pause-ceiling anchored on the $10 Spend-Management overage cap). Spend data only flows once the Vercel token has usage+billing scope (blocked on [[FIX-606]]). No auto-trip wired — Vercel native Spend Management pause is the backstop, this is the earlier warning layer. <!--id:FIX-642-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Leading-signal early-warning alert on db_connections (ungated, fast)** — Monthly cumulative usage is a lagging indicator and its threshold alerts are gated behind EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED. Added a leading-signal alert in the platform-snapshot cron: pages when supabase.db_connections (source='api') crosses LEADING_DB_CONN_THRESHOLD (default 45 = 75% of the 60-conn ceiling, below the 80% warning band), fires REGARDLESS of EMAIL_ALERTS_ENABLED (connection exhaustion → site 503s), and is rising-edge debounced via a dedicated platform_alert_state key so it pages once per spike. Complements [[FIX-642]]; complement to Vercel native Spend Management (no duplication). Deferred: moving snapshot cadence from GHA */10 (drifts to hours) to Vercel Cron now that we're on Pro, for genuinely steady leading cadence. <!--id:FIX-643-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Vercel usage parser keyed on wrong FOCUS fields — all metrics wrote 0 (redirected from FIX-606)** — Root cause of the never-populating Vercel Platform Costs card (misfiled as [[FIX-606]] 'regenerate the token' — the token is valid, team-scoped OWNER, billing/charges returns 200). extractFromCharges() read c['ChargeDescription'] + c['UsageQuantity'], but the FOCUS /v1/billing/charges lines carry ServiceName + ConsumedQuantity (+ BilledCost/EffectiveCost) — so nothing matched and all 9 quantity metrics wrote 0 with source='api'. Rewrote the parser: ServiceName→metric mapping with unit conversion (GB→bytes, hr→sec/ms), charges-first ordering (the dead /v1/usage 400s but had short-circuit priority), and a new effective_cost_usd (sum of EffectiveCost = true run-rate incl. prorated Pro base). monthly_spend_usd now persists EffectiveCost (~$8/mo) not BilledCost ($0, already watched by Vercel's native $10 cap). Verified end-to-end against live prod API. Retuned the [[FIX-642]] threshold to run-rate semantics ($50/70%/90%). <!--id:FIX-644-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **ISR the force-dynamic entity pages (districts, institutions) off the per-hit re-render path** — districts/[id] and institutions/[id] were force-dynamic (every hit fully re-renders → fluid-compute GB-hours). Neither emits per-user server content: districts reads only public data; institutions' only dynamic dependency was a per-render createAdminClient() pipeline_state read (FIX-432). Switched both to ISR (revalidate=300) via the publishable client (no cookies → no static opt-out), moving the institutions admin read off the render path. initiatives/[id] stays force-dynamic (auth.getUser() drives the author edit UI — genuinely load-bearing); officials/[id] already ISR. Models on jurisdictions/[id] (FIX-634). <!--id:FIX-645-->

MUST preserve the FIX-635 bounded donor fallback (DONOR_FALLBACK_LIMIT=200, never the 50k scan) and the official_donor_rollup_mv read. - [x] 🟠 L — **Collapse institutions/[id] render N+1 into get_institution_page(uuid) RPC** — institutions/[id] has two render paths (AgencyView ~13 queries, GoverningBodyView ~11) plus the existing get_institution_recent_votes RPC.
