- [x] 🟠 M — **Correct Vercel usage numbers + alert on fluid-compute cost driver** — Post-FIX-644 the Vercel Platform-Costs card populates but the numbers are wrong and the real cost driver (fluid provisioned memory) is unwatched. Evidence-first investigation against live prod /v1/billing/charges (2026-06-21) overturned the prompt's suspected causes: unit conversions are CORRECT (every hardcoded conversion matches the real ConsumedUnit); no double-counting (147 lines = 7 days x 21 regions, all leaves). The real bugs: (1) billing/charges returns only a trailing ~7-day window, never month-to-date (a 90-day request returns the same 7 days; historical ranges 404), so persisted 'monthly' values were a 7-day total compared against MONTHLY allotments; (2) platform_plan=vercel:pro but only 4 of 9 metrics had a pro row, so the override DROPPED the other 6 -- incl. fluid_memory_gb_hrs -- off the card entirely; (3) monthly_spend persisted 7-day EffectiveCost (incl. prorated Pro base + add-ons) mislabeled, while BilledCost (the $ the spend cap watches) is $0. Fixes: project the trailing window to a 30-day run-rate (source='estimated', raw window kept in metadata); add pro rows for all 9 metrics (edge_requests=10M real Pro allotment, rest Hobby-ref context since Pro is credit-based $20-credit not per-metric allotments); show billed-vs-effective labeled; per-ServiceName $ breakdown; ungated rising-edge leading alert on fluid-memory + fluid-cpu DOLLARS vs the $20 credit. Companion to [[FIX-644]] / cost-resilience-followups-2026-06-21. <!--id:FIX-648-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Dead /api/platform/vercel route still keys on dead FOCUS fields (unused)** — apps/civitics/app/api/platform/vercel/route.ts builds its metrics map on ChargeDescription/UsageQuantity/UsageUnit/BilledCost — the same dead FOCUS fields [[FIX-644]] fixed in the shared getVercelUsage() helper (real fields: ServiceName/ConsumedQuantity/ConsumedUnit). So its per-metric quantities are all 0 / 'Unknown'. Confirmed during [[FIX-648]] that NO component fetches this route (grep clean), so it's dead code — the live card reads via useDashboardData→/api/platform/usage. Either delete the route or rebuild its parser on ServiceName/ConsumedQuantity (and note billing/charges is a trailing ~7-day window, not month-to-date, per FIX-648). Low priority since unconsumed. <!--id:FIX-649-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags() ~80min is the nightly long pole after FIX-651** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-651]] prod verification (off-peak rule-tagger run, 2026-06-22): with the three statement_timeout-cascade callsites moved to direct-pg the rule tagger now COMPLETES (exit 0, 2.84M tags, no statement/gateway/upstream timeout) where 06-22 died at the 2h cap — but total wall-clock was still ~87 min, and `[timing] phase: tagFinancialEntities` alone was **4819.9s (~80 min)**. The financial-industry bulkUpsert within it was only 27.1s; the cost is the pre-existing server-side `rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags()` ([[FIX-444]] direct-pg path) doing DELETE('size') + INSERT…SELECT of ~2.33M size tags on the bloated `entity_tags` (2.4GB / 3.39M rows, audit 2026-06-22) on cache-starved Pro Small — vs the >2-min historical baseline, a ~40x regression. autovacuum VACUUM ANALYZE on entity_tags was observed running concurrently (overdue), so part of the 80min is bloat/IO contention; the daytime run + a preceding refresh_spending_totals may also have inflated it vs a true 02:00 UTC nightly. Risk: the enrichment-phase has a 120-min cap; rule tagger ~87min + AI tagger + AI summaries + chord MVs could still SIGTERM even with the cascade gone. FIX-651 cleared the *cascade*; this is the residual *budget* risk. Options (cheapest first): (1) check whether entity_tags autovacuum is stranded/behind like entity_connections was in [[FIX-650]] and VACUUM + tune scale_factor; (2) make the size rebuild incremental (only entities whose donation total changed since the last run) instead of full DELETE+reinsert every nightly; (3) per-entity size-tag MV. Measure a clean off-peak baseline post-autovacuum before choosing. Cross-ref [[FIX-651]] [[FIX-444]] [[FIX-650]] [[FIX-589]]. <!--id:FIX-652-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Move size-tag rebuild off the nightly (relocate to rebuild-entity-connections.yml, or incremental diff)** — FIX-652 Rung 3 — deferred pending a measurement only a real Sunday nightly can give. FIX-652 shipped Rung 1 (entity_tags autovacuum tuning + reclaim) and Rung 2 (source-change gate: skip rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags when the donation signature is unchanged → ~6/7 nights become a no-op). The remaining risk is the **Sunday** run: donations DO change Sunday (weekly FEC bulk in the 'fec' phase), so the gate does NOT skip, and the full ~80-min size rebuild still lands inside the 120-min **enrichment** phase alongside LittleSis, tag-industry, MV refreshes, AI tags, AI summaries. That Sunday enrichment phase is plausibly over budget (it is exactly why the entity_connections rebuild was already pulled out of the nightly — see [[FIX-291]]). DECIDE AFTER observing a real post-FIX-652 Sunday enrichment-phase wall-clock: if Sunday has headroom, close this no-op. If not, pick ONE — (A) **Relocate**: move the size rebuild out of the nightly rule tagger into rebuild-entity-connections.yml (Sun+Wed, 4h budget, direct-pg, off-peak). It is donation-derived, same source + cadence as the donor-rollup MVs already refreshed there; the FIX-652 gate travels with it (makes the Wed run a no-op). RECOMMENDED — cleanest, removes Sunday risk entirely with no correctness change (size tags only change on the Sunday FEC ingest, so daily refresh was always wasteful). (B) **Incremental diff**: upsert only entities whose bucket changed + delete tags for entities that left the donor set, instead of DELETE-all + INSERT-all — more code, pick only if relocation does not fit the workflow shape. Correctness invariant (FIX-443): identical final tag state — verify by count (~2.33M) + bucket spot-diff. <!--id:FIX-653-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags does a full DELETE+INSERT every night — gate it (different signal than FIX-652)** — Sibling of [[FIX-652]]. rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags() (packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts, tagPreVoteConnections ~L918, via runHeavyRebuild) does the same full DELETE + INSERT…SELECT nightly as the size rebuild did — ~80s on prod. NOT bundled into FIX-652 because the source-change gate signal is DIFFERENT: pre_vote depends on votes (changed DAILY by the Congress.gov votes ingest) joined against donations, so a donation-only watermark would never let it skip, and a votes-watermark would rarely skip either (votes move most nights). Investigate whether a cheap gate is even worth it here (it may genuinely need to run most nights), or whether it should ride along with whatever Rung-3 relocation [[FIX-653]] chooses. Measure its standalone wall-clock first — if it is a small fraction of the budget once FIX-652 removes the size pole, this may be a no-op closure. Correctness invariant: identical final tag state. <!--id:FIX-654-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Local auth test harness — mint a logged-in local session (unblocks authed-branch verification)** — Authenticated UI branches can't be exercised on the local stack (surfaced verifying FIX-574's verified-constituent branch; recurs for FIX-571 and every claim/verification flow). Two blockers: (1) local [[auth.captcha]] now holds the REAL Turnstile secret so a scripted/headless OTP send carries no valid token and fails; (2) the seeded aaaaaaaa-* users 500 on admin generate_link ('Database error finding user') — they're public.users rows without complete GoTrue auth.users/identities. Fix: a local-only harness (packages/data/src/scripts/mint-local-session.ts, data:auth:mint) using the captcha-free admin path admin.createUser -> admin.generateLink(magiclink) -> /auth/confirm verifyOtp(type=email), creating fresh GoTrue-complete users in the @civitics.test namespace. Optional --grant-constituent <jurisdiction_id> for verified-constituent sessions. Hard-guarded to 127.0.0.1 (refuses prod). No captcha/config change. Outputs a ready-to-navigate /auth/confirm URL (or --tokens). <!--id:FIX-659-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Index + LATERAL-rewrite get_jurisdiction_page agency spend CTE** — get_jurisdiction_page(US-federal) hit its own 8s statement_timeout (57014). Prod EXPLAIN isolated the spend CTE: 118 federal agencies fan into 1,287,741 contract/grant financial_relationships rows, and the global ORDER BY amount_cents DESC LIMIT 100 forces materializing+sorting all 1.28M (measured 213,060ms). No index fixes the original query shape (INCLUDE(amount_cents): 26.4s; full covering index-only: 27.4s — both still scan 1.28M). Fix: ordered covering index (from_id, amount_cents DESC NULLS LAST) INCLUDE(to_id,to_type,relationship_type,occurred_at) WHERE from_type='agency' AND relationship_type IN ('contract','grant') + rewrite the CTE to per-agency LATERAL top-100 then global top-100 (identical result, reads ~5.7k rows). Prod after: 94ms. Migration 20260627000100. <!--id:FIX-661-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Align get_jurisdiction_page statement_timeout with the page's 3s client withDbTimeout** — The page wraps the RPC in withDbTimeout(...,3000) but the function cap was 8s. The JS race only controls when the render proceeds — it does NOT cancel the in-flight backend — so a slow call returned the degraded shell at 3s while the DB kept working ~5s more, holding its pooled connection (the pool pressure FIX-634 set out to remove, just fewer/longer). With FIX-661 the heavy page is ~60-80ms warm; the only >3s calls are fully-cold IOWait-bound renders the 3s client already abandons. Lowered function statement_timeout 8s->3s (ALTER only) so the DB frees its connection in lockstep with the client. Invariant: function cap <= client cap. Migration 20260627000200. Cold-render IOWait itself is FIX-663. <!--id:FIX-662-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Materialize get_jurisdiction_page spend aggregation (per-jurisdiction MV)** — Durable follow-up to FIX-661/662. The LATERAL rewrite made federal spend 213s->94ms, but cold full-page renders on the cache-starved Pro Small (256MB shared_buffers, ~54% hit) still spike on IOWait reading other sections' cold pages (e.g. Rhode Island cold ~9.5s, warm ~110ms) — abandoned by the 3s client. The durable fix is to materialize the per-jurisdiction page payload (or at least the spend aggregation) so the request path is a single PK lookup, following the donor-rollup MV precedent (FIX-499/FIX-506) and the materialization pattern in packages/db/CLAUDE.md (per-entity MV + refresh hook + live-compute fallback). Lower priority now that the catastrophic federal case is fixed. Also evaluate get_official_page/get_agency_page/get_gb_page (FIX-646/647) — they share the cost-not-count limitation; get_institution_page (FIX-647) has the same from_type='agency' spend pattern. **Part-2 design (2026-06-27, FIX-664/665 sweep):** the sibling sweep found only get_agency_page shared the catastrophe (FIX-664); get_official_page/get_gb_page needed no shape change. Prod profiling for the MV: only **62 of 10,509** active jurisdictions carry any institutions/officials/proposals — the other ~10.4k are empty district/county shells that render trivially and need NO materialization. Cold dominators are `boundary` (PostGIS SVG from MULTIPOLYGON — RI 52ms warm CPU + large geometry read), `get_jurisdiction_activity` (multi-table aggregate, ~17ms warm), and officials/institutions scattered-heap scans — all STABLE (change only via nightly pipelines). **Recommendation:** a regular cache TABLE `jurisdiction_page_cache(jurisdiction_id uuid PK, payload jsonb, refreshed_at timestamptz)` — NOT a Postgres MATERIALIZED VIEW (REFRESH locks + rebuilds every row, no selective refresh), mirroring donor_rollup_mv/spending_totals. Rename the current body to `get_jurisdiction_page_live(uuid)`; `refresh_jurisdiction_page_cache()` UPSERTs payloads for the ~62 content-bearing jurisdictions by calling `_live`; public `get_jurisdiction_page(uuid)` reads cache by PK and falls back to `_live` on miss (covers new/empty jurisdictions before first refresh). Refresh hook: nightly orchestrator tail, after entity_connections + existing MV refreshes (~62 payloads × ~100ms ≈ 7s, negligible). Freshness caveat: only `get_jurisdiction_activity` is potentially intra-day — if it must stay live, splice live activity over the cached payload at read time (1-17ms) and materialize everything else. Implementation deferred to its own PR. <!--id:FIX-663-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **get_agency_page spend: align to FIX-661 ordered index (NULLS LAST)** — FIX-661 sweep, sibling RPC. get_agency_page's `spend` CTE (single from_id top-100 by amount) used bare `ORDER BY amount_cents DESC` (NULLS FIRST), which does NOT match the FIX-661 covering index [[FIX-661]] keyed (from_id, amount_cents DESC NULLS LAST). Prod EXPLAIN on the heaviest agency (743,723 contract/grant rows) showed a full per-agency scan (371,862 rows x2) + 24MB on-disk merge sort = 556 ms. One-word fix to `DESC NULLS LAST` makes it an Index Only Scan reading 100 rows = 0.48 ms (~1100x). No LATERAL/new index needed (single from_id). Also lowered the function statement_timeout 8s->3s to match the page client withDbTimeout (FIX-662 invariant). Migration 20260627000300. <!--id:FIX-664-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Align get_gb_page + get_official_page statement_timeout caps to client withDbTimeout** — FIX-661 sweep cap-only tail [[FIX-664]]. Prod EXPLAIN proved neither RPC needs a spend-shape change: get_gb_page has no financial_relationships section; get_official_page's global top-10 contract/grant scan already early-terminates on financial_relationships_amount (9.3 ms). But both kept the 8s function cap (FIX-647/FIX-646) above their page clients' withDbTimeout — get_gb_page 3000, get_official_page 5000 — so the DB burned Pro Small I/O for seconds after the client already returned null and degraded the render (the waste FIX-662 removed for the jurisdiction page). Lowered get_gb_page->3s, get_official_page->5s. ALTER FUNCTION only. Migration 20260627000400. <!--id:FIX-665-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Harden FEC chunk upserts: retry+throw instead of silent failed-and-continue** — The FEC bulk indiv stage pre-upserts recipient-committee entities in 500-row chunks via PostgREST. The local FIX-677 backfill exposed a silent-partial bug: a chunk that timed out on transient IO/autovacuum contention was caught, logged, counted as `failed += chunk.length`, and skipped — 500 of 6,219 committees never entered `entityIdByCmte`, so their donors' donations were silently dropped downstream at `if (!toEntityId) continue;` while the donation-write count stayed green. Pro Small is more IO-bound, so this will recur during the prod backfill. Fix: wrap the per-chunk `.upsert()` in `upsertPacEntitiesBatch`, `upsertDonationRelationshipsBatch`, and `upsertIndependentExpendituresBatch` with retry-with-backoff (new `retryWithBackoff` in pipelines/utils.ts), then THROW on exhausted retries instead of counting-failed-and-continuing — aborting loudly lets the idempotent run be re-tried rather than shipping partial data. Adds an `indivCmteSkippedUnresolved` integrity counter to the indiv→committee build loop (should always read 0) so the backfill has a crisp done-gate. Excludes the direct-pg donor/indiv-to-committee writers and heavy-rebuild RPCs by design. Makes the prod FIX-677 backfill self-protecting. <!--id:FIX-686-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Schedule entity_connections rebuild via pg_cron (Mon full / Wed incremental); neutralize GHA schedule** — Register two pg_cron jobs calling [[FIX-687]]'s procedure: rebuild-ec-full at 0 8 * * 1 (Monday) and rebuild-ec-incremental at 0 8 * * 3 (Wednesday). De-stacks the full rebuild off Sunday so it runs AFTER the Sunday FEC bulk ingest lands instead of contending with it. Neutralize .github/workflows/rebuild-entity-connections.yml by removing the two schedule: cron triggers; keep workflow_dispatch as manual break-glass. PR1 of the cron-resilience program. <!--id:FIX-688-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Raise nightly-sync fec-phase timeout-minutes 120→150 (stopgap)** — Interim cushion for the Sunday FEC-bulk indiv stage that routinely overruns the 120-min fec-phase budget. Below GHA's 6h cap. Stopgap until the ingest-resumability PR (PR4, watermark-resumable Vercel Workflow ingest) lands the durable fix. Pairs with [[FIX-686]]. <!--id:FIX-689-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **RAM-safe workflow_dispatch FEC backfill against prod** — New .github/workflows/fec-backfill.yml (workflow_dispatch, cycles input) runs the FEC indiv bulk stage on a 16GB GHA runner against prod, unblocking the [[FIX-677]] historical super-PAC type-10 receipt backfill (local box is RAM-blocked: 12GB indiv heap + local Docker overcommit). New data:fec-bulk:ci script (no --env-file, --allow-prod) satisfies the createAdminClient prod-write guard and bypasses the nightly Sunday day-gate. Doubles as the load-test + prod verification vehicle for [[FIX-687]]'s pg_cron rebuild. <!--id:FIX-690-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **pg_cron entity_connections rebuild dies at 2-min cluster statement_timeout** — The pg_cron `rebuild-ec-full` job ([[FIX-688]]) FAILED on its first scheduled firing (2026-06-29 08:00 UTC) after exactly 2 min: `canceling statement due to statement timeout` inside `rebuild_ec_donations_full_window`, called from `run_entity_connections_rebuild` ([[FIX-687]]). Root cause: the cluster-default `statement_timeout` is 2 min (`pg_settings.reset_val=120000ms`), inherited by the `postgres` role pg_cron runs as (the role sets no timeout of its own). The procedure body's `SET statement_timeout='90min'` and each chunk function's `proconfig` timeout (donations_full_window=45min) do NOT survive pg_cron's COMMIT-per-chunk execution: after the procedure's internal COMMITs the GUC reverts to the 2-min cluster default, so the first donations-window INSERT (needs tens of min) is killed at 2 min and the procedure aborts before its terminal data_sync_log UPDATE. Evidence: the `postgres` rolconfig holds only search_path (no statement_timeout); the OLD direct-pg TS path scripts/rebuild-entity-connections.ts (run-rebuild-chunks-prod) sets the timeout per-connection and completes fine (06-24 = 39.8 min / 2.59M edges) — so the rebuild logic is correct and only the pg_cron timeout inheritance is broken. Side effects of the abort: leaves entity_connections autovacuum_enabled=false (re-enable ALTER never reached; manually restored 2026-06-29) and a stuck `running` sync-log row (reaper self-heals). Candidate fixes (decide mechanism): (a) re-issue SET statement_timeout at the top of EACH loop iteration / after every COMMIT in the procedure (cheapest, targeted) and verify the per-chunk proconfig re-applies; (b) ALTER ROLE postgres IN DATABASE postgres SET statement_timeout — broad, and a role-level bump is risky per the cache-starvation notes; (c) set the timeout on the cron command string itself; (d) keep the rebuild on the proven GHA/TS direct-pg path and partially revert the FIX-688 pg_cron schedule. Until fixed, the twice-weekly graph rebuild is non-functional on prod and edges go stale (last good rebuild 2026-06-24). Surfaced by the FIX-677 prod backfill load-test (PR1 verification of FIX-687/688). <!--id:FIX-697-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **FEC JFC individual receipts double-counted + bloat 2024 indiv backfill past GHA 6h cap** — Two coupled findings from the FIX-677 prod backfill load-test (fec-backfill.yml run 28408664237, cancelled at 5h52m, incomplete). **(A) JFC individual-receipt double-count.** The non-candidate-committee recipient set `nonCandCmtes` in packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts is built from cm24 by **CMTE_TP only** (`NON_CAND_KEEP_TYPES = {O,X,Y,Z,N,Q,V,W}`); CMTE_DSGN (designation) is never parsed from cm24 (`CM_COL` has no CMTE_DSGN entry — `parseCm24` reads only CMTE_ID/CMTE_NM/CMTE_TP/CONNECTED_ORG_NM). The documented joint-fundraising/leadership exclusion (designation ∈ {J,D,B}) is applied ONLY on the ccl candidate-authorized path (indiv.ts `parseCcl`), NOT on the cm24/non-candidate path. So joint-fundraising committees filed as CMTE_TP='N' with CMTE_DSGN='J' — confirmed on prod: HARRIS VICTORY FUND (C00744946, tp=N, 339k rows/$837M), TRUMP 47 COMMITTEE (C00867937, tp=N, $341M), TRUMP NATIONAL COMMITTEE JFC (C00873893, tp=N, $146M), TRUMP SAVE AMERICA JFC (C00770941, tp=N, $70M) — slip through and are captured as individual→committee `donation` rows (source=fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee). JFC receipts are re-itemized to participants via JFC→participant transfers, so counting individual→JFC double-counts the same dollars (~$1.4B+ across those 4 JFCs alone). Fix: add CMTE_DSGN (cm24 col 8) to CM_COL + CommitteeInfo, and exclude CMTE_DSGN ∈ {J,D,B} when building nonCandCmtes — mirroring the ccl-path exclusion the header comment already claims. **(B) Full-cycle backfill exceeds the 6h GHA job cap.** The 2024 presidential indiv backfill wrote ~$3.42B / 1.59M indiv→committee rows + ~516k indiv→candidate over 5h52m at a ~30 rows/sec relationship-upsert rate on the Micro (vs ~490/sec for the donor-entity upsert — financial_relationships index maintenance is the per-row cost), and was cancelled before completing (UDP/super-PAC type-10 tail + Schedule-E IE + post-cycle recompute unfinished; UDP C00799031 = 0 rows). The pipeline has no checkpointing, so a re-dispatch restarts from zero and hits the same wall. Finding (A) removes ~600k+ rows of JFC volume and may bring a corrected run under the 6h budget; the per-row upsert speed (~30/s) still warrants benchmarking (chunk size / index maintenance on financial_relationships). The durable fix is watermark-resumable ingest (PR4 / [[FIX-689]] interim). **Until a clean run completes, total_received_cents / total_ie_*_cents must NOT be rebuilt** — the current prod state has partial + double-counted indiv→committee rows that are invisible only because the received-totals rebuild has not run. Surfaced by the supervised FIX-677 prod runbook (PR1 verification). Cross-ref [[FIX-677]] [[FIX-236]] [[FIX-689]]. <!--id:FIX-698-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Reusable scope filters for surgical FEC indiv re-runs (tx-type / stage / cycle)** — Make the FEC indiv stage surgically scopeable so a specific failed slice can be re-ingested without a full-cycle re-run. Three env knobs, each defaulting to current full-run behavior: FEC_INDIV_TX_TYPES (override the 15,15E,10 tx-type filter), FEC_INDIV_STAGES (comma allow-list of indiv sub-stages: donor-entities, indiv-to-candidate, recipient-entities, indiv-to-committee, independent-expenditures, totals), and the existing FEC_INDIV_CYCLES/FEC_CYCLES. A run is auto-detected as 'scoped' when tx-types narrow below the default OR stages exclude any stage; scoped runs stop overwriting financial_entities.total_donated_cents/total_received_cents in the donor + recipient-committee upserts (a partial slice must not clobber a full aggregate) and rely on the totals rebuild to re-derive authoritative values. fec-backfill.yml gains tx_types/stages workflow_dispatch inputs. Addresses the [[FIX-698]](B) >6h full-cycle wall by scoping the FIX-677 super-PAC (type-10) finish small (distinct from PR4 watermark-resumability, still the durable fix for large full runs); unblocks the FIX-677 finish. <!--id:FIX-700-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **FIX-698 over-excludes D (leadership) and B (SSF) designations from indiv-to-committee receipts** — Found during the [[FIX-677]] finish enumeration (cm24 parsed via the FIX-698 code path, joined to the prod 2024 fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee set). EXCLUDE_DESIGNATIONS in packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts is {J,D,B}, applied in buildNonCandRecipientSet to drop those designations from the individual-to-committee recipient set on a double-count rationale (they re-itemize receipts to participants via downstream transfers, so counting the individual-to-cmte leg double-counts). That rationale is airtight for J (joint-fundraising committees are legal conduits; the same dollars are transferred to participants who re-itemize them) but WRONG for B and shaky for D. B = Lobbyist/Registrant SSF PACs (e.g. NATIONAL ASSOCIATION OF REALTORS PAC 6.6M, AIPAC PAC 5.7M, NATIONAL AUTOMOBILE DEALERS PAC 5.6M) and D = Leadership PACs (e.g. Trump NEVER SURRENDER INC 33M small-dollar) are ordinary contributing PACs: money comes in from members/donors, the PAC independently contributes to candidates. individual-to-PAC and PAC-to-candidate are two DISTINCT money legs, exactly like the type-N other-PACs and type-O super PACs FIX-698 KEEPS. No double-count. So excluding D and B drops legitimate direct individual receipts (2024 prod: 537 D cmtes / 69,800 rows / 82.9M, and 1,208 B cmtes / 162,532 rows / 287.6M). The FIX-677 finish DELETEd the D/B partial cancelled-run artifacts (they were essentially absent pre-run, baseline 42 rows, and inconsistent with current code) to keep prod consistent and restore status quo. Fix: narrow EXCLUDE_DESIGNATIONS to {J} only (keep D and B as valid recipients), extend jfc-exclusion.test.ts to assert D and B are KEPT (currently it asserts they are excluded), then a corrected complete indiv run backfills the D/B individual receipts. The complete-run cost is the same >6h GHA wall as [[FIX-698]](B) — durable path is watermark-resumable ingest [[FIX-689]]. Cross-ref [[FIX-698]] [[FIX-677]] [[FIX-236]]. <!--id:FIX-701-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals exceeds 90min on Micro (4M rows) — donor total_donated_cents chronically stale** — Surfaced during the [[FIX-677]] finish (fec-backfill run 28493948960, 2026-07-01). The totals stage logged `rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals failed: canceling statement due to statement timeout` after running the full 90min (05:01->06:31 UTC). Root cause is NOT a missing override: the fn proconfig on prod IS statement_timeout=30min, but per packages/data/src/lib/heavy-rebuild.ts (header lines 12-14 + 124-125) statement_timeout is armed ONCE on the top-level statement using the SESSION value at statement start, so the function-level ALTER FUNCTION SET does NOT re-arm the outer SELECT rebuild_*() timer. runHeavyRebuild sets the session to 90min, so the effective cap is 90min. The rebuild itself (temp-table SUM of from_type=financial_entity + relationship_type=donation rows, now 4,056,034 rows = ~2x the ~2M it was tuned for at ~18min in 20260513010000) exceeded 90min on the cache-starved Micro (256MB shared_buffers / ~54pct hit, see [[FIX-503]]). Impact: (1) total_donated_cents is NOT refreshed, so it is chronically stale/understated on prod — this fires every fec-bulk run INCLUDING the nightly fec-phase, not just scoped runs; (2) worse on scoped runs (FIX-700 skips per-row aggregate overwrite, so the totals rebuild is the ONLY writer of donor outbound totals) — after the type-10 backfill the 92,982 type-10 donors show understated totals (e.g. ISENBERG $154,905 stored vs $197,522 live; PORTER $4,200 vs $10,700). Fixes: chunk the rebuild ([[FIX-263]] entity_connections pattern) or add a targeted variant that only re-sums entities with changed outbound (the interim remediation for the FIX-677 donors is a targeted UPDATE of the affected from_id set); durable path is chunked/resumable like the pg_cron entity_connections rebuild [[FIX-687]]. NOTE received-totals rebuild ([[FIX-269]] sibling) still succeeds (~56s, far fewer target rows) so super-PAC received data is fine. Cross-ref [[FIX-700]] [[FIX-269]] [[FIX-444]]. <!--id:FIX-702-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **pg_cron entity_connections FULL rebuild fails at 90min donations wall (no per-window COMMIT); statement_timeout also defeats chunk-failure resilience + strands autovacuum** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-677]] finish Phase 4 (the definitive prod proof of [[FIX-697]]). Scheduled a one-shot pg_cron CALL run_entity_connections_rebuild(full) at 06:57 UTC 2026-07-01. GOOD: it survived 90min with NO 2-min death, so FIX-697 (postgres-role statement_timeout=90min default surviving COMMITs) genuinely works. BAD: it FAILED at exactly 08:27:00 (=06:57+90min) in rebuild_ec_donations_full_window with canceling statement due to statement timeout (cron.job_run_details jobid 3). ROOT CAUSE: the procedure (supabase/migrations/20260628000100) runs the donations chunk = prepare()+16 windows+finalize() in ONE transaction with NO per-window COMMIT (intentional for rollback atomicity, lines 163-183). statement_timeout re-arms ONLY after a COMMIT; the last COMMIT before the chunk was the autovacuum-pause at line 124 (~run start), so the ENTIRE donations chunk shares ONE 90min budget. The code comment at lines 164-166 claiming each window gets its own fresh statement_timeout is FALSE (that only holds if a COMMIT separates them, as the OLD TS direct-pg path did via autocommit-per-window). Post-FIX-677 the donation edge set grew (America PAC ~93k donor edges, plus all type-10 super-PAC donors) so the chunk now exceeds 90min on the cache-starved Micro ([[FIX-503]]); the last good full rebuild was 39.8min on 06-24 (pre-FIX-677, TS path). SECONDARY BUG: the per-chunk EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS resilience (lines 191-196) is DEFEATED by statement_timeout — once the timer expires, every subsequent statement (the COMMIT, other chunks, the terminal UPDATE) instantly re-times-out, so the procedure aborts entirely and NEVER writes its terminal failed row: the data_sync_log row was stranded running (had to hand-mark failed), the whole run rolled back (edges unchanged at 2,790,628), and entity_connections autovacuum was left stranded OFF (line-207 re-enable never reached — the recurring [[FIX-590]] hazard; cleaned up manually). FIXES: (a) COMMIT after each donations window to restore the TS autocommit-per-window semantics so each window gets a fresh 90min timer (trades all-or-nothing atomicity for resumability — acceptable, matches historical TS behavior, next rebuild re-runs), or use finer windows (32/64) each well under 90min; (b) in the failure path reset statement_timeout before the terminal UPDATE + autovacuum re-enable so cleanup always runs (or move them to a guaranteed path); (c) fix the misleading comment. IMPACT: [[FIX-687]]/[[FIX-688]] pg_cron FULL rebuild does NOT complete on prod — the Mon rebuild-ec-full job would fail every week at this wall; graph donation edges cannot refresh via pg_cron. Both jobs remain paused. Interim reconciliation path = the TS direct-pg rebuild (per-window autocommit). Cross-ref [[FIX-697]] [[FIX-687]] [[FIX-688]] [[FIX-291]] [[FIX-590]]. <!--id:FIX-703-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **pg_cron full rebuild tail wedges Micro: finalize stale-VM 4h + donor-rollup MV REFRESH CONCURRENTLY OOM** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-703]] supervised prod full rebuild (2026-07-02). [[FIX-703]] fixed the donations windows (per-window COMMIT + postgres role-default statement_timeout 90min->6h; the CALL-start timer is immutable inside a CALLed procedure so per-COMMIT/in-proc-SET/proconfig can't re-arm it — only the role default works). The donation/super-PAC edges DID land (edges 2,790,628 -> 4,794,034; donation 1,682,742 -> 3,686,148, closing [[FIX-677]] Phase 4). BUT the rebuild's TAIL wedged prod and the run died at the 6h CALL wall, then the MV step OOM-wedged Micro (had to restart compute via Management API). Two durable problems, both re-triggered by every scheduled full run (rebuild-ec-full Mon 08:00) so BOTH jobs remain PAUSED until fixed: (1) FINALIZE (rebuild_ec_donations_full_finalize recipient_count UPDATE) runs while autovacuum is PAUSED (FIX-590) right after the 16 windows delete+insert all ~3.69M donation edges — the newly-written pages aren't all-visible so its Index Only Scan on entity_connections_from_type_from_id_to_type_to_id_connecti_key degrades to per-row heap fetches -> ~4h on the doubled edge set (with a FRESH vacuum it was 33min; measured). A single non-committable statement, so per-window COMMIT can't help. Fix options: re-enable autovacuum + VACUUM entity_connections BEFORE finalize (VACUUM can't run inside the procedure -> move finalize to a separate pg_cron step after a vacuum, or drop the FIX-590 autovacuum pause, or make finalize incremental/cheaper). (2) REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY official_donor_rollup_mv builds a full second copy of the doubled donor set -> OOM'd Micro so hard NO new connection (pooler session+txn ECHECKOUTTIMEOUT, Management API 544, econnrefused) could be established; app served stale/edge-cache only; required a compute restart. On restart the killed refresh rolled back so the MV kept its PRIOR snapshot (currently STALE — missing the new super-PAC donor money). Fix options: non-CONCURRENT REFRESH (lighter memory, faster, but ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on the MV during refresh — acceptable off-peak), or bound work_mem/maintenance_work_mem for the refresh, or upgrade compute, or make the rollup incremental. INTERIM STATE: donation edges + recipient_count fresh & committed; donor-rollup MV stale (needs a controlled off-peak refresh); jobs paused; interim reconcile still via the TS direct-pg path. Cross-ref [[FIX-703]] [[FIX-697]] [[FIX-687]] [[FIX-590]] [[FIX-677]] [[FIX-443]]. <!--id:FIX-704-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Donor-rollup orphan sweep: hard-deleted FR rows / deleted entities leave stale rollup rows the watermark can't see** — FIX-704's incremental donor rollup dirties recipients via FR.updated_at, so a HARD-DELETEd financial_relationships row (or a deleted recipient entity) never dirties its recipient — the rollup keeps the stale rows forever. Surfaced immediately on the 2026-07-03 prod bootstrap: 58,369 carried rows across 287 recipients had ZERO qualifying FR rows — 285 were FIX-672-deleted junk committees and the other 2 were officials still showing the fake $8-9.98B quarantined IEs (Akhlaghy $9.98B topped the ie_support leaderboard). One-off anti-join DELETE cleaned it (scratchpad/fix704_cleanup_and_enable.sql). Durable fix: fold the anti-join orphan sweep into the monthly ec-recipient-count-reconcile pg_cron slot or the weekly donor-rollup-refresh tail — DELETE FROM official_donor_rollup_mv r WHERE NOT EXISTS (qualifying FR row for r.official_id), chunked/committed like the rest of the procedure. Same class of gap exists in the FIX-372/373 edge incrementals but those have the Monday FULL rebuild as their safety net; the rollup has no full-path cadence, hence this sweep. Cross-ref [[FIX-704]] [[FIX-672]]. <!--id:FIX-705-->

Serves treemap-pac global sector/party modes via get_pac_treemap_by_{sector,party}. Local CONCURRENTLY refresh measured 1m48s. <!--id:FIX-718-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Scoped FEC indiv re-runs are no-op'd by the FIX-193 Last-Modified watermark** — A scoped FEC bulk run (FEC_INDIV_TX_TYPES / FEC_INDIV_STAGES / FEC_INDIV_RECIPIENT_CMTES set) is an explicit surgical re-ingest, but the FIX-193 indiv watermark short-circuit skips the entire indiv stage when FEC's indiv{yy}.zip Last-Modified is unchanged since the last run. A CLOSED cycle's indiv file never changes again, so ANY scoped re-run of a closed cycle is silently no-op'd — the [[FIX-701]] 2024 D/B re-capture dispatch hit exactly this (indiv stage skipped → zero D/B rows restored; only the unrelated Schedule-E stage ran). Fix: bypass the watermark short-circuit when isScoped (un-scoped nightly still short-circuits as before). Surfaced live during the FIX-701 supervised prod re-capture. <!--id:FIX-722-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **runFecBulkPipeline exits 0 on a caught fatal — GHA marks a failed run 'success'** — runFecBulkPipeline() catches its own fatal errors, calls failSync(), and RETURNS a result object instead of rethrowing; the standalone entrypoint then does `.then(() => process.exit(0))` unconditionally. So a pipeline that dies mid-run (e.g. a financial_entities chunk hitting the prod statement_timeout) logs 'FEC bulk pipeline fatal error: …', writes a failed data_sync_log row, and STILL exits 0 → the GHA step (fec-backfill.yml / nightly fec-phase) shows green. Surfaced during the [[FIX-701]] re-capture: a run that failed at the pas2 upsert reported conclusion=success and I only caught it by reading the log. The Mark-killed backstop only covers SIGTERM/timeout kills, not caught fatals. Fix: the entrypoint should inspect the PipelineResult (or the pipeline should rethrow) and process.exit(1) when the run failed / failSync fired, so CI status is trustworthy. Keep the graceful cleanup, just exit nonzero on failure. <!--id:FIX-727-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Monthly financial-entity-totals reconcile is too slow on cold Small (repoint set-based before enabling)** — The FIX-702/726 monthly full-reconcile (`reconcile_financial_entity_totals()`, windowed per id-range) is registered but LEFT PAUSED on prod: on cold Pro Small each window's donation zero-pass (merge/nested anti-join over ~156k positive entities + ~164k FR rows) runs multi-minute even after the FIX-702/726-perf partial indexes + range-scoped anti-join (20260704000100), extrapolating to ~60-80min for the 16-window full pass. The one-time prod bootstrap (2026-07-04) instead used the proven set-based old functions (`rebuild_financial_entity_{donation,received}_totals()`) which did ONE FR scan each — received in seconds, donation in ~65min (inflated by the FIX-701 re-capture's huge dirty set; routine monthly would be far smaller). Decide the durable monthly design: (a) repoint the reconcile procedure to CALL the set-based functions (simplest, one big-ish txn, fine off-peak), or (b) keep windowed but make the zero-pass a single set-based hash-anti-join (not 16 cold passes). Then enable `financial-entity-totals-reconcile`. The WEEKLY `financial-entity-totals-incremental` is live+correct and is the durable path; the monthly is only the hard-delete orphan safety net (rare), so this is not urgent. See [[FIX-702]] [[FIX-726]]. <!--id:FIX-734-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **entity_connections donation edges incomplete for individual→committee (recipient_count under-count)** — The FIX-734 supervised prod run (2026-07-05) surfaced this: entity_connections is MISSING donation edges for a large set of individual→committee (to_type='financial_entity') donations. Confirmed on prod — individuals with a live donation FR to a real, existing PAC (NAR PAC, PwC PAC, NEVER SURRENDER INC, …) have NO corresponding entity_connections 'donation' edge (EC has only ~1.15M committee-donation edges from ~881k donors, far short of FR). Because recipient_count is EC-derived (rebuild_entity_connections block 1b, FIX-194), it inherits this incompleteness: the reconcile_recipient_count EC-based orphan sweep zeroed 527,626 individuals, ~124k of whom had live PAC donations. Root cause is likely the FIX-704 edges-only windowed rebuild + incrementals not materializing (or pruning) individual→committee donation edges while individual→official edges stay complete. Impact: recipient_count (a sort key + displayed count in the graph individual-donors / connections panels) under-counts PAC-only donors; connector-mode may miss multi-PAC donors. Fix: audit the donation-edge rebuild windows to confirm they emit ALL to_type donation edges, backfill the missing individual→committee edges, and add a completeness check (EC donation-edge distinct-donor count vs FR). Blocks re-enabling ec-recipient-count-reconcile. Cross-ref [[FIX-704]] [[FIX-734]] [[FIX-736]]. <!--id:FIX-735-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Make recipient_count FR-derived (not entity_connections-derived); re-enable a safe orphan sweep** — recipient_count (distinct donation recipients per individual) is currently EC-derived (rebuild_entity_connections block 1b + the FIX-704 dirty-scoped donations incremental + reconcile_recipient_count bump), which makes it hostage to entity_connections completeness ([[FIX-735]]). The FIX-734 EC-based orphan sweep was reverted (20260705000100) precisely because no-EC-edge does NOT imply no-donation. On 2026-07-05 prod recipient_count was recomputed FR-authoritatively as a one-off (COUNT(DISTINCT to_id) over each individual's donation financial_relationships, windowed, per-window COMMIT → scratchpad/restore_recipient_count.sql), giving 1,960,472 positive individuals with ZERO rc>0-but-no-FR rows. Durable rework: (1) repoint the ONGOING maintenance to FR — the donations edge incremental currently updates recipient_count from EC dirty edges, which will drift it back toward the incomplete-EC value; make both the incremental and the reconcile compute from financial_relationships (index-only on financial_relationships_derivation, proven fast/windowed on prod). (2) Re-introduce an orphan sweep that is SAFE because FR is complete: zero rc>0 individuals with no donation FR (a true orphan under the authoritative source). (3) Then re-enable ec-recipient-count-reconcile (renamed appropriately) — it is operationally PAUSED until this lands. Decide also the intended semantics vs the FIX-194 comment ('distinct officials') — current code counts all recipient types (officials + committees); the connector feature arguably wants all. Cross-ref [[FIX-194]] [[FIX-704]] [[FIX-734]] [[FIX-735]]. <!--id:FIX-736-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **USASpending 2GB first-run checkpoint/resume** — The USASpending 'full' contract archive (~2 GB compressed) never completes a first-run within any GHA budget: it processes ~200k rows then gets SIGTERM'd at the job cutoff, writes no checkpoint, and restarts as a 'first run — no prior state' every invocation, so per-category delta state is never established and the file is re-streamed from zero forever. FIX-A pulled USASpending out of the nightly enrichment phase into its own workflow_dispatch (usaspending-bulk.yml) to stop it starving the enrichment budget, but that is a containment move, not the durable fix. The durable fix is FEC-backfill-style resumability: stream the full archive with a persisted row/byte watermark (packages/data/.usaspending-bulk-state.json already tracks per-category delta date — extend it to carry an in-progress full-run offset), so a killed run resumes mid-file and eventually checkpoints a completed full baseline, after which the existing delta path takes over. Until then the first full baseline must be seeded by a long supervised workflow_dispatch run. See [[FIX-A]]. <!--id:FIX-739-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Split enrichment into heavy/light sub-jobs + pull USASpending out** — The nightly enrichment phase hit its 120-min GHA cutoff (2026-07-05) from cumulative INGEST overrun — not aggregation (PR2 already moved derivation to pg_cron). Three ingest stages compound: LittleSis (~35 min: 17-min match-index build + stream 1.9M rels + upsert 601k external_relationships), EDGAR, and USASpending's 2 GB full-file first-run (killed at the cutoff ~200k rows in, never checkpoints → restarts as first-run nightly). Fix (FIX-292 pattern): (1) pull the USASpending bulk stage OUT of the enrichment phase entirely and give it its own workflow_dispatch (usaspending-bulk.yml) — checkpoint/resume is the deferred durable fix [[FIX-739]]; (2) split the remaining enrichment phase into a HEAVY sub-job (LittleSis) and a LIGHT sub-job (EDGAR + CourtListener + OpenStates + agencies/OPM/PLUM/elections/committees/agency-* + tag-industry + the daily MV-refresh/rule+AI-tagger/AI-summaries tail), each with its own timeout-minutes, chained needs: + if:!cancelled() so one sub-job timing out doesn't starve the next. Orchestrator gains --phase=enrichment-heavy / enrichment-light scoping; standalone usaspending entry mirrors data:fec-bulk:ci. Pairs with [[FIX-B]] (LittleSis direct-pg) and [[FIX-C]] (EDGAR bigint parse). <!--id:FIX-740-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **LittleSis external_relationships/review_queue upserts drop rows at prod statement_timeout — route through direct-pg** — LittleSis upsertExternalRelationships() streams ~601k external_relationships through PostgREST .upsert() in 500-row chunks; on prod, slow chunks hit the ~8s role/statement_timeout cap and were SILENTLY dropped (failed += chunk, ~4,000 rows lost on the 2026-07-05 run) — the exact FIX-462 chunk-upsert bug class the FEC indiv path already fixed. Route both LittleSis chunk upserts (external_relationships arbiter (source,source_id); external_relationships_review_queue arbiter (source,source_entity_id,source_relationship_id)) through the direct-pg path (lib/direct-pg-upsert.ts withDirectClient + bulkUpsert, session statement_timeout raised past the gateway cap), same as upsertHop1FinancialEntities already does. Preserves the {inserted,failed} counter shape — failed>0 now signals a real constraint problem, not a timeout drop. Data-loss/correctness. See [[FIX-740]], [[FIX-462]]. <!--id:FIX-741-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **EDGAR officer comp parse overflows bigint (scientific notation) and drops whole chunks** — EDGAR parseUsdCents() in pipelines/edgar/util.ts returns Math.round(n*100) with no upper bound. A mis-parsed/oversized DEF 14A compensation cell yields a value beyond Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER; JS serializes it as scientific notation ('8.49e+22') which Postgres rejects on the total_compensation_cents BIGINT column ('invalid input syntax for type bigint: "8.49e+22"'), failing the ENTIRE 500-row edgar_executive_officers upsert chunk — dropping real officer rows + 339 filings alongside the one bad value (observed 2026-07-05). Fix: parse defensively — reject values that aren't safe integers within bigint range (return null + log), so the garbage officer is skipped and the chunk lands. Correctness. See [[FIX-740]]. <!--id:FIX-742-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **force_weekly dispatch knob for on-demand supervised heavy nightly runs** — The heavy/weekly ingest stages (FEC bulk, IRS-990, LittleSis, EDGAR-weekly, OpenStates API, agencies/OPM/PLUM/elections/committees/agency-*, tag-industry) are day-gated to Sunday in `runNightlySync` (the single `isWeekly = getDay()===0` gate driving the `if (isWeekly)` block in packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts). A manual `workflow_dispatch` on a non-Sunday therefore runs only the light daily path. Add a `force_weekly` boolean `workflow_dispatch` input on nightly.yml (default false) passed as `NIGHTLY_FORCE_WEEKLY` to all three phase jobs; the orchestrator gate becomes `runWeekly = isSunday || NIGHTLY_FORCE_WEEKLY==='true'` so the full heavy path can be run supervised in a low-traffic window without waiting for the calendar. Default/unset === today's behavior byte-for-byte. Heavy, prod-writing when forced — supervised/off-peak only. <!--id:FIX-743-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **ai-classifier entity_tags industry preload full-scans → 38s → 8s prod timeout (tag_ai fails every Sunday)** — The FIX-743 supervised run exposed tag_ai failing on prod with 'ai-classifier industry-tagged FE preload read failed (rows 0-999, 3 attempts): canceling statement due to statement timeout'. Root cause: runAiClassifier (tag-industry; it logs under the tag_ai sync-log name) preloads already-industry-tagged financial_entity ids from entity_tags with WHERE entity_type='financial_entity' AND tag_category='industry' ORDER BY id (packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-classifier.ts ~L164, via selectAllOrThrow). No index serves that predicate+order, so the planner does an Index Scan on entity_tags_pkey (for ORDER BY id) and filters, Rows Removed by Filter=76,503 to return the first 1,000 — EXPLAIN ANALYZE on prod = 38,744 ms cold. That blows the 8s authenticator/service_role statement cap (see [[reference_prod_role_statement_timeouts]]) so the PostgREST read is cancelled after 3 attempts and the whole stage aborts. Runs in the weekly block so it fails every Sunday-equivalent run, silently skipping industry tagging. There are 30,815 industry FE tags today. Fix: add a covering index entity_tags (entity_type, tag_category, id) so the equality-on-two-cols + id-order preload is a bounded index range scan (<<8s) — no code change needed; the sibling PAC preload is fine (1.3s). Belt-and-braces alt: route the preload through the direct-pg heavy-rebuild path. Cross-ref [[project_prod_buffer_churn_baseline.md]] (entity_tags index leads with entity_type). Surfaced by [[FIX-743]]. <!--id:FIX-745-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Extract the daily derivation tail from enrichment-light into its own sub-job (Sunday budget squeeze)** — FIX-743's forced-heavy run proved enrichment-light is tight against its 120-min budget on weekly (Sunday) days: the weekly ingest block (EDGAR + CourtListener + OpenStates + elections + committees ~90 min) plus the daily derivation tail can't both fit — the run was SIGTERM'd mid-tag_rules, so ai_summaries and the two deferred MV refreshes never ran. Give the daily tail its own sub-job/budget, mirroring the [[FIX-740]] heavy/light split. Adds a NightlyPhase enrichment-tail value + runEnrichmentTail gate; moves the Phase 2 daily block (comment bridge scorer, tag_rules, tag_ai, ai_summaries, refresh_donor_party_rollup_mv + refresh_entity_connection_stats_mv) out of runEnrichmentLight — light keeps the ingests only (EDGAR through tag-industry). GHA nightly.yml gains an enrichment-tail-phase job chained after enrichment-light-phase (needs + if:!cancelled(), own timeout, NIGHTLY_FORCE_WEEKLY propagated). Plain enrichment/all still run heavy+light+tail (canary path unchanged). Complementary to [[FIX-717]]/[[FIX-718]] — once those two MVs are incrementalized onto pg_cron they leave the tail entirely. <!--id:FIX-746-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Browse W0: entity_search_index + facet rollup + refresh machinery** — Wave 0 of the search/browse explorer redesign. Builds: (1) entity_search_index table (kind x entity_id, display_name, search_tsv, per-kind facet columns, committee_ids uuid[], amount_cents/amount_label per FIX-667 precedence, connection_count, activity_at, primary_source) with trigram GIN on display_name, GIN on search_tsv + committee_ids, and facet/sort btrees; (2) browse_facet_counts rollup (kind x facet_key x facet_value x count + per-kind totals) for zero-query facet counts; (3) rebuild_entity_search_index() idempotent rebuild fn wired into the nightly tail. Individuals reach browse results via a capped financial_entities trigram passthrough when a text query is present. Substrate only — no UI change; /api/search untouched. Pairs with [[FIX-749]] (BrowseState/registry + /api/browse endpoint). <!--id:FIX-748-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Browse W0: BrowseState + entity-kind registry + /api/browse endpoint** — Wave 0 endpoint layer over [[FIX-748]]'s entity_search_index. Ships: (1) a canonical URL-serializable BrowseState { scope, facets, q, sort, cursor } — the shape that later replaces both SearchFilters and GroupFilter; (2) a registry-driven entity-kind definition (apps/civitics/src/lib/browse/registry.ts) defining kind/label/facets/sortable-columns/ledger-columns/graphSeedable, absorbing graph-seedable-kinds.ts data and re-exporting for back-compat; (3) a scope compiler mapping the By-Branch tree path (current GROUP_TREE semantics: People/Money/Government/Legislation/Initiatives) to index predicates; (4) keyset pagination (base64 (sort_value, entity_id) cursor) replacing offset; (5) GET /api/browse returning { rows, facets, totals, counts_mode, cursor, refreshed_at } with registry-validated params and s-maxage=60/SWR=300. Financial-individual passthrough when scope includes them + text query. No UI change; /api/search untouched. Unit tests for scope-compiler round-trip + cursor codec + registry validation. <!--id:FIX-749-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Regen @civitics/db types for entity_search_index + browse RPCs** — Follow-up to [[FIX-748]]/[[FIX-749]] (Browse W0). The /api/browse route casts the admin client to `any` because entity_search_index, browse_facet_counts, and the get_browse_page/get_browse_facets/rebuild_entity_search_index functions aren't in packages/db/src/database.ts yet. Regen db types (see reference_regen_db_types_from_local memory recipe: gen from local via --db-url, splice the committed __InternalSupabase header) so W1 gets typed row/RPC access and the `as any` casts can drop. Non-urgent — build is clean and the route works; purely a type-safety debt for the next wave. <!--id:FIX-750-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **fec_bulk indiv ingest: checkpoint/resume across nightly runs (PR4)** — The nightly fec-phase job SIGTERMs at its 150-min budget on heavy Sundays (GHA 28755577316: donor-entities upsert 52m + indiv-to-candidate 47m + indiv-to-committee killed 32m in = 88% of wall; download+extract+stream is only ~2.3m / ~1.5%). A killed run restarts from zero the next Sunday, so a heavy week's ingest may never complete — the 150-min budget ([[FIX-689]]) is a stopgap. Fix: make runFecBulkPipeline checkpoint the indiv stage and complete across multiple nightlies. Resume state lives in pipeline_state.fec_bulk_run_state (DB, never a runner-local file — the [[FIX-739]] USASpending trap); run identity = (cycle, FEC indiv Last-Modified + etag), a new FEC drop discards the state. Two-tier progress: per-sub-stage completion markers using the [[FIX-700]] scope.ts stage vocabulary + intra-stage chunk cursors threaded through bulkUpsert for the three direct-pg writers, persisted per committed chunk (GHA SIGTERM gives no reliable grace window). Resume trigger: the nightly orchestrator gates the fec_bulk block on (isWeekly || fecResumePending) and narrows FEC_CYCLES/FEC_INDIV_CYCLES to the pending cycle, so Monday/Tuesday nightlies auto-finish Sunday's work. On resume, re-download (R2 cache) + re-stream rebuilds the in-RAM maps deterministically; completed upsert stages rebuild their id maps via batched direct-pg reads instead of re-running; a stored-total mismatch resets that stage's cursor to 0 (idempotent). Scoped runs ([[FIX-700]]/[[FIX-701]]) bypass the machinery entirely; [[FIX-193]] watermark semantics unchanged (advance only on full completion, then state cleared). Phase-0 evidence gate REJECTED the R2 aggregation-checkpoint artifact (stream is ~1.5% of fec_bulk wall). Same commit bundles [[FIX-727]] (standalone entrypoint exit code). <!--id:FIX-754-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **promote-candidates whole-chamber mass event on the 2026-07-05 nightly — find the pair generator, cap/warn the step** — The scheduled 2026-07-05 Sunday nightly's congress-officials promote-candidates step ([[FIX-248]], direct-pg via [[FIX-463]]) detected and promoted 537 (candidate, elected) pairs — the whole chamber — at ~17s/pair of transactional FK rewrites, burning 2h04m of the fec-phase budget in the DAILY block before fec_bulk started (surfaced by the [[FIX-754]] Phase-0 log work). Whole-chamber promotion should be a rare event; enumerate the generator branches (backlog drain of detected-but-failing pairs; fresh candidate-row generation by a cn{yy} ingest whose loadOfficialsByFecIds map missed sitting members — note the FIX-677/FIX-701 prod backfills ran cn24+cn26 in full days before; fresh elected-row generation from a bioguide re-match failure). Outcome: fix the generator + add a per-run promotion cap if recurring-capable, or document the one-off and add a loud warning when pairsDetected exceeds a sanity bound so a mass event is visible instead of buried in the non-fatal catch. <!--id:FIX-755-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **fec_bulk pas2/entity/IE writers still on PostgREST — route through direct-pg** — The 2026-07-05 scheduled nightly's fec_bulk died fatally in the cycle-2024 pas2 relationship writer under a statement-timeout storm (PostgREST path, ~8s prod role cap, retries exhausted) — plausibly caused by the same run's whole-chamber promotion ([[FIX-755]]) saturating the Micro with FK churn. upsertPacEntitiesBatch, upsertDonationRelationshipsBatch, and upsertIndependentExpendituresBatch are the last PostgREST-exposed writers in fec_bulk; route them through withDirectClient + bulkUpsert like the indiv writers ([[FIX-462]], [[FIX-741]], same session-level raised statement_timeout). Preserve the [[FIX-686]] loud-abort contract — throw when any chunk fails after the run so a dropped entity chunk can't silently orphan downstream donations — and keep the FIX-700 skipAggregateOverwrite behavior byte-for-byte. Cosmetic fold-in: the fec-bulk fatal catch prints [object Object] for non-Error throws; unwrap them the way the nightly orchestrator's errMsg does. See [[FIX-754]] for the surfacing run. <!--id:FIX-756-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **nightly orchestrator exits 0 on a failed/partial phase — GHA goes green on silent ingest failure** — The pipelines/index.ts nightly entrypoint does .then(() => exit(0)) unconditionally, so a phase whose results.errors is non-empty (phaseStatus 'partial' — e.g. the 2026-07-05 fec_bulk fatal) still exits 0 and the GHA job shows green. [[FIX-727]] fixed this for the standalone fec-bulk entrypoint only; the nightly path (data:nightly:*:ci) has the same bug. Now that GHA does source-ingests only, a silently-failed ingest = undetected stale source. Fix: exit 1 exactly when results.errors.length > 0 — one rule, aligned with the existing phaseStatus derivation ([[FIX-292]]). A transient blip (e.g. plum_book) going red is accepted and desired — trustworthy status is the point. The GHA chain is unaffected: downstream jobs use needs + if !cancelled(), which runs after a failed (not cancelled) upstream, and the mark-killed post-step is if always(). <!--id:FIX-757-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **nightly congress officials update wholesale-replaces officials.metadata — wipes district_jurisdiction_id for all House reps** — Sibling of the [[FIX-755]] source_ids clobber, found during its investigation: the congress officials update path sends metadata: {} through .update() for all 537 members nightly, wholesale-replacing the jsonb. Verified on prod 2026-07-06: 0 of 437 active House Representatives carry metadata->>'district_jurisdiction_id' (the FIX-217 TIGER congressional-district link written by link_federal_reps_to_districts()), because the nightly wipe erases it and the districts pipeline that restores it is annual/manual. Any officials.metadata key another writer owns suffers the same fate (promoted rows' candidate provenance, etc.). Fix mirrors FIX-755 — omit metadata from the UPDATE payload (or merge instead of replace, deciding key ownership) — then re-run link_federal_reps_to_districts() against local + prod to restore the links (runtime action, both DBs separately). <!--id:FIX-758-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **fec_bulk end-of-run newFecIds persist is a stale read-modify-write on officials.source_ids — replace with server-side jsonb merge** — The end-of-run 'Storing N FEC ID associations' loop (fec-bulk/index.ts) writes source_ids as { ...o.source_ids, [storageKey]: fecId } from the pipeline-START officialMap snapshot — a stale read-modify-write that drops any source_ids key written by anything else mid-run (fec_bulk runs for hours on Sundays; the congress path's writes, promotions merging fec_candidate_id, etc. all land in that window). Same jsonb-clobber class as [[FIX-755]]/[[FIX-758]], opposite pipeline. Fix: replace the snapshot spread with a per-row server-side merge — UPDATE officials SET source_ids = source_ids || jsonb_build_object(key, val) WHERE id = ... via withDirectClient — eliminating the lost-update window entirely rather than shrinking it with a re-read. The loop is tens of rows, so per-row statements are fine. <!--id:FIX-759-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Unordered .range() pagination across packages/data loaders can skip/duplicate rows — add stable .order() everywhere** — Multiple packages/data loaders paginate with .range() and no .order() — fec-bulk loadOfficials + candidates.ts loaders, congress/promote-candidates loaders, enrichment/seed-backlog, littlesis/writer, tags/rules + ai-classifier, irs990/writer, edgar/companies, plum-book, agency-leadership, elections, ai-summaries, and more. Without a stable sort Postgres gives NO ordering guarantee across pages, so rows can be skipped or duplicated as page boundaries shift between queries — the exact pattern [[FIX-545]] fixed for the congress officials preload (a skipped official there re-INSERTed members; here it silently under-loads match indexes and backlogs). Sweep the whole package: add .order() on a unique column (the PK) to every .range()-paginated read that lacks one. Behavior-neutral otherwise. <!--id:FIX-760-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **promote_candidate_to_elected() FK-rewrite surface audit — post-May polymorphic tables (entity_comments, entity_positions, ...) may be uncovered, orphaning rows on every promotion** — The RPC's FK-rewrite table list is [[FIX-248]]-era, last extended by [[FIX-463]] (entity_tags/enrichment_queue/ai_summary_cache). Tables that reference officials polymorphically and landed AFTER it — entity_comments ([[FIX-519]]), entity_positions/position_events ([[FIX-523]]), and anything else post-May — may not be in the rewrite. Promotion DELETEs the elected row with no old-to-new UUID map kept, so every uncovered surface orphans its rows permanently. A month of weekly whole-chamber UUID churn (~278 members/week, [[FIX-755]]) plus the in-progress 88-pair drain (25/night) makes coverage urgent: extend the function BEFORE more drain nights run. Method: derive the authoritative official-referencing surface list from the LIVE DB (pg_constraint FKs on officials(id) + the polymorphic entity_type/from_type/to_type='official' column conventions greppable in the codebase — not migration archaeology), diff against the function body, then ONE read-only prod batch counting per-surface rows pointing at UUIDs absent from officials. Gaps → migration extending the function + per-surface remediation decision for existing orphans; coverage complete AND orphans 0 → close as no-op with the audit evidence. <!--id:FIX-761-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **USASpending bulk drops all CSV parts after the first (multi-part Full archive truncation)** — USASpending bulk ingests only the FIRST CSV part of each multi-part Full archive, silently dropping the rest. USASpending splits each FY Full zip into 1,000,000-row CSV parts, but extractCsvFromZip (packages/data/src/pipelines/usaspending-bulk/index.ts) takes only the first .csv entry and autodrain()s the others. Confirmed on the supervised 2026-07-08 run 28911039672: BOTH categories read EXACTLY 1,000,000 rows (contracts 823,083 upserted, assistance 68,932 upserted, 0 failed) — a truncation tell; a range-read of the two zips central directories showed FY2026_All_Contracts_Full = 3 CSV parts and FY2026_All_Assistance_Full = 4 parts. So prod holds only ~1/3 of contract awards and ~1/4 of the assistance scan (the majority of grant-shaped awards in parts 2-4 are lost). Latent since inception — every prior successful run (April/May) also processed part 1 only; the job reports success because each part-1 stream completes cleanly and nothing signals the dropped parts. Fix: enumerate ALL .csv entries in extractCsvFromZip (extract each to its own path, run processCsvFile over each, accumulate FileResult), or concatenate parts while stripping the per-part header. Each part carries its own header row so columns:true parsing works per-file unchanged. Cost: a full run then processes ~3M contracts + ~4M assistance rows, roughly 3-4x wall-clock (~90 min, still inside the 350-min cap) and ~3x prod write volume — run at low-traffic hours. Complements checkpoint/resume [[FIX-739]] and the manual-workflow split [[FIX-740]]; independent of the direct-pg timeout fix [[FIX-586]]. Secondary: .usaspending-bulk-state.json is gitignored and runners are ephemeral, so every CI dispatch logs No prior state first run and always runs Full (delta path dead in CI) — folds into [[FIX-739]]. <!--id:FIX-766-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **fix:add allocator race — count FIX-id markers from origin/main, not just the stale worktree FIXES.md** — A worktree fix:add allocates the next FIX-id from its own working-tree docs/FIXES.md only (allocateNextId in scripts/fix-add.mjs), which is stale relative to origin/main for the life of the worktree — so a concurrent hand-add or another session's fix:add on main collides on the same id (the FIX-762/766 race, de-collided in dd26c0f0). Fix: allocateNextId does a quiet best-effort git fetch and additionally counts <!--id:FIX-(\\d+)--> markers from origin/main:docs/FIXES.md, taking max(originMain, workingTree, done.log, archive), and warns to stderr when origin/main is ahead of the working tree so the divergence is visible. Markers only — never the loose /FIX-\\d+/ grep on FIXES.md, which once burned an id on a forward-ref [[FIX-NNN]] (per [[FIX-368]]). Offline/detached (no origin) degrades to today's behavior. Test via pnpm fixes:test or a small unit around the marker count. Cross-ref [[FIX-762]] [[FIX-766]] (the collision), [[FIX-368]] (marker-only allocation rule), [[FIX-462]] (worktree collision-guard model). <!--id:FIX-771-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Swap live get_connection_counts request-path callers to entity_connection_stats_mv** — Browse P1. Prod 500s selecting a high-connection entity in search — POST /rest/v1/rpc/get_connection_counts statement-timeouts (live-COUNTs both directions of ~5.68M entity_connections; [[FIX-499]] class 0.7s warm / 12-25s cold, 8s authenticator cap). Swap the live request-path callers to entity_connection_stats_mv ([[FIX-509]]) point reads: api/search/entity (detail rail, the user-facing 500) and api/graph/connections (neighbor-degree gate, chunk .in() at 200 for URL length). MV miss = zero edges = 0; MV error degrades to 0/hidden, never the live RPC. get_connection_counts left in place (deprecated for request paths, comment migration); its last caller api/search/route is already [[FIX-769]]-deprecated and retires with [[FIX-773]]. Cross-ref [[FIX-503]] [[FIX-510]] [[FIX-775]]. <!--id:FIX-774-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Audit: request-path live-aggregate callers over entity_connections / financial_relationships** — Browse P1 FIX-B (deliverable = this audit + the filed gap follow-ups). Enumerated every apps/civitics/app/api/** request-path caller that live-aggregates (COUNT/SUM/GROUP BY, or client-side agg over paged rows) the two big tables. Already-materialized/covered: graph/entities + graph/treemap + treemap-pac entity mode (entity_connection_stats_mv / official_donor_rollup_mv, [[FIX-509]] [[FIX-510]] [[FIX-518]]), browse/typeahead (entity_search_index). Already-tracked (not re-filed): [[FIX-507]] contract-flow MVs, [[FIX-732]] URI-too-long, get_browse_facets financial ILIKE (~733ms under budget), [[FIX-497]]/[[FIX-500]] group donor rollup. Swapped by [[FIX-774]]: search/entity, graph/connections. Genuine remaining gaps filed separately below. Cross-ref [[FIX-774]]. <!--id:FIX-775-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **graph/connections depth=2 intermittently 500s on prod — edge-hydration timeout under IOWait** — Discovered during Browse P1 ([[FIX-774]]) prod verification, SEPARATE from that swap. GET /api/graph/connections?entityId=X&depth=2 intermittently returns HTTP 500 at ~5.3s on prod for normal officials (repro'd on a 30-conn and a 130-conn official — same input succeeds on retry, so it is a load-dependent timeout, not a deterministic error). Successful runs are correct (right shape, collapse-gate working), so the fault is downstream of the [[FIX-774]] MV neighbor-count swap: the depth-2 edge-hydration fans out to ALL neighbors and pulls their edge rows via fetchAllPaged over entity_connections ([[FIX-428]]), which trips the route's 5s withDbTimeout under prod IOWait ([[FIX-499]] class, [[reference_prod_role_statement_timeouts]]). A 30-conn source still explodes to ~1684 nodes / ~1698 edges at depth 2. Options: tighten the depth-2 auto-expand ceiling, cap total hydrated edges lower, or materialize a bounded neighborhood. Pre-existing (edge-hydration code unchanged by [[FIX-774]]). Cross-ref [[FIX-775]] [[FIX-428]]. <!--id:FIX-781-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Claude Code permission-prompt storm: //c/ path form never matches + compound-hook gaps** — Post-security-change prompt storm had two root causes. 1: permission rules and additionalDirectories in the MSYS //c/Users form silently never match — the working form is c:/Users with forward slashes, verified via headless claude -p probes which turn prompts into observable denials; and .claude/settings.local.json is TRACKED, so worktrees run whatever form is committed. 2: compound-allow-hook v7 gaps — exec/xargs wrappers, for/do/done loops, comment lines, tee/seq, backticks inside single quotes, and curl -X POST to loopback all deferred. Hook v8 covers those; regression suite at .claude/hooks/tests (node runner.mjs, allow/defer case files). Temp claude scratchpad dir added to additionalDirectories; dead one-shot rules trimmed; /scratchpad/ gitignored. <!--id:FIX-789-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Put the USASpending delta on a weekly cron on usaspending-bulk.yml itself** — usaspending-bulk.yml was manual-dispatch-only since [[FIX-740]] pulled the 2 GB first-run out of the nightly. [[FIX-739]] then landed the DB-backed checkpoint/resume state (pipeline_state.usaspending_bulk_state), so the delta baseline is live and a scheduled run is a cheap delta (~32s when no new archive has landed). Add a weekly schedule: cron to the workflow ITSELF (decision: NOT rejoining the nightly — its 120-min budget is what FIX-740 escaped). Thu 10:00 UTC — off-peak, clear of the 02:00Z nightly, the Mon/Wed 08:00 EC-rebuild + Tue rollup pg_cron windows, and the Sunday FEC block. Existing concurrency group serializes a scheduled firing against an in-flight manual dispatch. Header comment updated (it still described FIX-739 as deferred). <!--id:FIX-790-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **apps/civitics typecheck red on main — public-payload.test.ts (FIX-788) fails tsc strict index checks** — pnpm typecheck fails with 9 errors in apps/civitics/src/lib/public-payload.test.ts (TS2532 possibly-undefined indexed access, TS2339 on never) since commit 5710c21c ([[FIX-788]]). Latent because next build excludes src/lib tests and run-tests.mjs (tsx) executes without typechecking — the suite passes at runtime. Surfaced by the FIX-772 landing recipe's typecheck gate, 2026-07-10. Fix is test-file-local (guards or non-null assertions after the stripViewerKeys generic narrows). <!--id:FIX-793-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **session:check should surface the latest tests.yml conclusion on main** — The FIX-793 typecheck breakage sat red on main for 3 consecutive tests.yml runs (~21h, since 5710c21c) without anyone noticing: the gate itself fired correctly, but nothing pulls CI status into the session loop — no branch protection, no failure notifications, and pnpm session:check reports branches/worktrees/PRs but not workflow runs. Add a read-only probe to scripts/session-check.mjs (gh run list --workflow=tests.yml --branch=main --limit 1) that WARNs (never blocks) when the latest main run is not success, so a red main surfaces at the start of the next session instead of after the next branch rides broken CI. Degrade silently if gh is unavailable. Surfaced by the [[FIX-793]] closeout. <!--id:FIX-794-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Invert CDN cache model: page allowlist in config, API handlers own their cache headers** — Follow-on to [[FIX-795]]: the denylist model fails unsafe — every new per-user route is a latent [[FIX-786]] cross-user cache leak until someone remembers to denylist it, and config headers() rules are method- and status-blind (captured live: cdnHot stamped on POST /api/comments/[id]/rate responses and on a 429 RSC response). Invert: the catch-all dies; cdnHot applies ONLY to an explicit allowlist of viewer-independent public PAGE prefixes; unlisted routes get nothing stamped (Next's own private/no-store for dynamic routes then stands) — a new route is safe by default, forgetting to list it costs performance never correctness. API routes own their cache headers in code ([[FIX-787]]/[[FIX-788]] pattern): the public-API cdnHot stamps (statements, questions, browse, typeahead, positions/rollup, summaries) move into the handlers where they can be GET-only and 200-only. Config stops matching /api except the pinned no-store guards (belt-and-braces). Rule made structural: cache headers only on payloads with zero viewer-dependence, set as close to the response as possible. <!--id:FIX-796-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Router prefetches drain the entity_leaf rate-limit cap — a single browser 429s itself** — Captured on prod: link-dense pages (e.g. the USA jurisdiction page) fire dozens of Next.js router prefetches (Next-Router-Prefetch: 1 RSC GETs) that drain the [[FIX-683]] entity_leaf Upstash cap (45/min) — one human's browser 429s itself (retry-after: 45, x-ratelimit-remaining: 0). Fix: separate bucket, not exemption — requests carrying the prefetch header count against their own generous bucket (~300/min) instead of the entity_leaf/entity_pages caps; real navigations keep the existing caps. Spoofing the header just moves a crawler to a different capped bucket — no bypass. Also: the middleware 429 must carry explicit no-store cache headers so a rate-limited response can never be edge-cached and served to other users (the [[FIX-796]] status-blind stamp landed on a 429 in the captures). <!--id:FIX-797-->

### COMMUNITY & AUTH

- [x] 🟠 L — **Unified entity_comments substrate (C0)** — Three legacy comment systems (civic_comments / official_community_comments / civic_initiative_arguments) unified into one polymorphic table `entity_comments` (CHECK'd discriminator entity_type IN proposal/official/jurisdiction/institution/financial_entity/district). Adds `comment_ratings` (two-axis ternary agree/valuable), denormalized rating_summary jsonb, flag auto-trip (>=3 unresolved content_flags -> needs_review), constituent-badge snapshot column, dormant bridge_score/map_x/map_y for C1. RLS per 20260531000000 pattern. Idempotent backfill from all three legacy tables + arg votes/flags; legacy tables FROZEN (INSERT/UPDATE policies dropped, data kept). Repoints proposal_comment_stats view + proposal_trending_24h MV to entity_comments. Phase C0 of the comments framework. <!--id:FIX-519-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Unified comments API (C0)** — New `/api/comments` surface over [[FIX-519]] entity_comments: GET (entity_type/entity_id/lens/sort/keyset cursor), POST (auth, kind+stance+body validation, depth<=3, app-layer rate limits 20 comments/200 ratings/10 flags per day, constituent-badge snapshot via has_active_constituent_grant + admin client since entity_grants is service-role-read), [id] PATCH (15-min edit window or withdraw), [id]/rate PUT (two-axis upsert), [id]/flag POST (content_flags content_type=entity_comment). Legacy comment/argument routes converted to thin @deprecated read-compat adapters over entity_comments. Pseudonymous display_name only, never email. Shared vocab module packages/db/src/comment-kinds.ts (per-entity-type + per-initiative-stage allowed kinds, rate-limit constants). <!--id:FIX-520-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **EntityComments unified UI (C0)** — One `EntityComments` client component replacing CivicComments / OfficialComments / ArgumentBoard, consuming [[FIX-520]] /api/comments. Typed-kind composer (pill row, default discussion), stance chip, constituent badge, two-axis agree/valuable rating controls (auth-gated optimistic), flag reason menu, newest/top sort toggle, constituent lens toggle. Rolled out to proposal/official/initiative detail pages (initiatives keep stage-aware vocab + for/against grouped view over the same data) and added as a small collapsed-by-default section to /jurisdictions/[id] and /institutions/[id] (no comments before). Old components left in place, @deprecated, imports removed. No financial_entity/district UI this PR. <!--id:FIX-521-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Remove deprecated comment + position UI and legacy comment-route adapters** — Post-C0/C1a cleanup: deleted the deprecated CivicComments / OfficialComments / ArgumentBoard components and the 5 legacy adapter routes (/api/proposals/[id]/comments, /api/officials/[id]/comments, /api/initiatives/[id]/arguments + the argument vote/flag sub-routes) — orphaned after [[FIX-521]] rewired all detail pages to EntityComments / [[FIX-520]] /api/comments. Also removed the superseded PositionWidget component and /api/proposals/[id]/position route (its POST wrote to the C0-frozen civic_comments → RLS-denied 500 on every submit) now that C1a's PositionSection + /api/positions own the proposal position UI. Verified nothing referenced the old shapes by grep before deleting. Cross-ref [[FIX-519]]. <!--id:FIX-522-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **C1-A: entity_positions spine (intensity, deltas, alignment repoint)** — C1 Wave A position spine. New entity_positions (one row per user+entity, intensity smallint -3..3, conditions_md, constituent snapshot), append-only position_events (a delta is an event with attributed_comment_id). Single write path set_entity_position() RPC (SECURITY DEFINER) enforces anti-collusion delta guards: attribution needs a prior position row on the entity, attributed comment must belong to the same entity, never the user's own comment, <=5 attributed events/user/day, account age >=7 days. Aggregate-only public reads: get_entity_position_rollup() (7-bucket distribution, median, pct_with_conditions, min-n=10) + per-comment rating_summary.deltas denormalized by trigger. compute_alignment_score repointed off frozen civic_comments to entity_positions (sign-match; stance=0 excluded from total_votes). Follows C0 [[FIX-519]] substrate. <!--id:FIX-523-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **C1-A: positions API (set / get-own / rollup routes)** — REST surface for the [[FIX-523]] position spine. /api/positions PUT/POST validates payload then calls set_entity_position via the caller's server client (createServerClient, so auth.uid() is the user — not the admin client); maps RPC exceptions to 400/401/429 with stable error codes. GET (entity_type+entity_id) returns the caller's own position or null. /api/positions/rollup GET calls the anon-allowed rollup RPC with lens. Responses never include other users' positions/events. RPC guards are the throttle — no extra app-layer rate limiting this wave. <!--id:FIX-524-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **C1-A: position card + rollup strip + terms-of-consensus + delta attribution UI** — UI for the [[FIX-523]] position spine on proposal + initiative detail pages. PositionCard: 7-stop stance selector (-3..+3, strong oppose<->strong support, neutral notch) + conditions textarea; on stance change where the entity has comments, optional skippable attribution picker. PositionRollupStrip: 7-bucket distribution bar + median + n + All/Verified-constituents lens toggle, below-min-n empty state. TermsOfConsensusPanel: top conditional/amendment comments by valuable score + pct_with_conditions. Delta badge ('changed N minds') on comment rows from rating_summary.deltas. Initiative position card hidden on draft, visible from deliberate onward. <!--id:FIX-525-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **C1-A: kind/stance normalization (drop support/oppose kinds)** — Stance is the canonical axis; double-encoding poisons Wave B's bridge scorer inputs. Remove support/oppose from every kinds vocab in packages/db/src/comment-kinds.ts; initiative composer gets stanceEnabled=true instead. Idempotent data pass rewrites existing entity_comments rows with kind IN (support,oppose) -> kind=discussion, setting stance from the old kind where stance IS NULL. Initiative stance-grouped display derives side purely from stance after this. Part of C1 Wave A ([[FIX-523]]). <!--id:FIX-526-->

Invoked via direct pg.Client (heavy-rebuild path) from data:score-comments and the nightly enrichment phase right after the MV refreshes; records pipeline_state key=comment_scorer watermark. Also folds in the Wave-A rollup polish: get_entity_position_rollup median percentile_cont -> percentile_disc so the median is an actual -3..+3 bucket. <!--id:FIX-527-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **C1 Wave C: statement-mode spine + slow-mode state (schema)** — Polis-lite agree/disagree/pass layer for the ~95% who never write a comment.
