RPCs: submit_statement (creation gated — rate-limited 5/day, own-comment promotion only, NO AI extraction), set_statement_vote (open to any auth user, returns fresh vote_summary), get_entity_statements (public list ordered by vote volume, caller's own vote + constituent lens, NO clustering — that's C3). Triggers: vote-summary on statement_votes; slow-mode activity on entity_comments INSERT (trips at 20 comments/hr for 2h, expiry derived on read); statement flag-autotrip on content_flags (>=3 -> needs_review). 'entity_statement' added to flag_content_type (own migration). Replay-safe on empty DB. Constants mirrored in comment-kinds.ts. Builds on C0 [[FIX-519]] / C1 [[FIX-523]] [[FIX-527]]. <!--id:FIX-533-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **C1 Wave C: statements API + slow-mode flag + rescore-on-trip** — /api/statements GET (anon-allowed list via the caller's server client so my_vote populates; also returns the slow-mode flag) + POST (auth submit, RPC exceptions mapped to 400/401/403/404/409/429 with stable codes); /api/statements/[id]/vote PUT; /api/statements/[id]/flag POST (content_type=entity_statement, reuses the comment flag rate-limit + own-flag block). getSlowMode() lib (single PK lookup; slow_mode AND slow_mode_until > now()) read in each detail page's SSR loader and passed to the client — no request-path aggregation. Rescore-on-trip wired in the comments POST path (not the trigger, to avoid AFTER-INSERT recursion on entity_comments): when a comment flips slow mode off->on, fire recompute_comment_bridge_scores once so the map/highlights are fresh during the spike. Depends on [[FIX-533]]. <!--id:FIX-534-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **v2 grant spine: enum values, platform-admin helper, expire_lapsed_grants + nightly sweep** — Spine half of the v2 claim flow. Migration 2: has_active_platform_admin_grant(p_user_id) (STABLE SECURITY DEFINER, unquoted search_path, 2s timeout) + expire_lapsed_grants() (flips lapsed active grants to expired, writes grant_events event=expired per grant, returns count) + pending-queue partial index. Sweep called from /api/cron/nightly-sync after the canary write — logged, never fails the canary. No new Vercel cron (Hobby 2-cron cap). <!--id:FIX-557-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Official self-serve claim flow: claim route + officials-page affordance + profile status** — Consumer half 1 of the v2 claim flow (spine: [[FIX-557]]). POST /api/officials/claim: auth -> validation -> 5/hr rate limit via grant_evidence -> official lookup -> duplicate check (409 on active/pending) -> exact auth-email vs officials.metadata->>'email' match. Fast path: active grant + gov_email evidence outcome=approved + grant_events auto_approved. Slow path: pending grant + evidence outcome=pending (method gov_email iff auth domain ends .gov, else manual_review) + event submitted; justification (required, <=1000 chars) in evidence.notes; metadata records domain + domain-match flag only (PII rule: nothing beyond auth email + justification). expires_at on approval = COALESCE(term_end, current_term_end) when future, else now()+2y. GET /api/officials/claim-status for the client island. UI: claim affordance on /officials/[id] (ISR-safe client island) + claim status card on /profile via users_read_own_grants RLS. <!--id:FIX-558-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **/admin/grants review queue: pending official claims + approve/reject + audit trail** — Consumer half 2 of the v2 claim flow (spine: [[FIX-557]], claim flow: [[FIX-558]]). /admin/grants page (createAdminClient, force-dynamic) listing pending official claims: claimant email + display name, target official name+link, evidence method, domain-match flag, justification, submitted_at; plus recent grant_events audit list. POST /api/admin/grants/[id] with action approve|reject. Approve: status=active, granted_at=now(), expires_at per term rule, evidence outcome=approved + reviewed_at/reviewer_id, event approved with admin actor_id. Reject: status=revoked, evidence outcome=rejected, event rejected. Gate (page AND routes): ADMIN_EMAIL match OR has_active_platform_admin_grant(user.id); notFound()/403 otherwise, mirroring pipeline-health. <!--id:FIX-559-->

- [x] 🟡 M — **SF-P3 shadow moderation harness (moderation_audit + read-only pass)** — Read-only regression engine for the moderation casebook (state-of-franklin-bible §11/§12, SF-P3). New append-only `moderation_audit` ledger + a `data:moderation-harness` pass that instantiates each F1-F12 fixture inside a transaction, computes what the live deterministic rules would do, compares to the fixture's expected verdict, rolls the fixture content back, and writes one audit row (persisting outside the rollback). Confers ZERO consequence - read + rollback + log only. Emits a dated report greppable for MISMATCH. Handled fixtures (F1/F3/F4/F7) expect MATCH; GAP fixtures (F8/F9/F10/F12) are logged as known-failing MISMATCH that drive SF-P4/P5/P10; partials (F2/F5/F6/F11) evaluate the evaluable sub-rule. Depends on [[FIX-572]] (is_synthetic), [[FIX-599]] (SF-P2 labels), [[FIX-600]] (count exclusion) - reads/evaluates them, changes none. <!--id:FIX-601-->

Local seed only; prod run gated. <!--id:FIX-607-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **SF-P4 brigade detector (shadow, F8/F9)** — Net-new, false-positive-aware brigade/coordination detector (SF-P4, state-of-franklin-bible §11.1 F8/F9, §12). Detection-only, shadow-first: a public.detect_brigade_candidates() SQL fn scores candidate clusters and a data:brigade-scan TS runner logs them to an append-only brigade_candidates table. Confers NO automatic consequence (no flag/collapse/score change) — enforcement is a deliberate human-gated follow-up, out of scope. Two modes over a shared cross-target co-occurrence core: F8 fast coordinated pile-on (tight-window same-direction rating cluster) and F9 slow-burn (repeated cross-target co-occurrence of the same account set below any burst window). Conservative + FP-aware: up-weights tight timing, new-account cohorting, low content diversity, cross-target co-occurrence; down-weights established/constituent-snapshot accounts, valence variety, single organic target. Excludes synthetic authors via author_excluded_from_standing so the Franklin seed is never flagged. Flips SF-P3 harness F8/F9 from MISMATCH to MATCH and adds a NEGATIVE fixture (legitimate coordinated surge must NOT flag). Signal gaps recorded as future work: no IP/device/fingerprint logged, no persisted verified-constituent flag (cf [[FIX-571]]). Depends on [[FIX-572]] (synthetic predicates) and [[FIX-601]] (harness + moderation_audit). <!--id:FIX-608-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **SF-P5 bridge-score anti-gaming (rater trust + cross-stance independence)** — bridge_score = valuable_rate x map_y is farmable (harness F10, bible §11.1 F10/§12): a small ring splitting stances evenly and all marking valuable=+1 yields map_y=1, valuable_rate=1 -> bridge_score=1.0 with zero genuine disagreement. SF-P1/[[FIX-572]] excludes synthetic raters but the F10 attack uses non-synthetic throwaway/new accounts, so synthetic-exclusion alone does NOT fix it. KEY: map_y and valuable_rate are ratios, so uniform per-rater trust weighting cancels - the lever that flips F10 is an ABSOLUTE trusted-mass FLOOR per stance side. Harden recompute_comment_bridge_scores: weight each endorser by account-age trust (created_at; 0 under 7d, ramp to 1.0 at 30d), compute trust-weighted sides tw_pos/tw_neg, require least(tw_pos,tw_neg) >= 1.0 (each side backed by >=1 trusted-equiv endorser) before a comment qualifies. A fresh-account ring (tw=0 both sides) cannot clear it; a genuine cross-bloc endorsement by aged/varied accounts still scores. map_x/map_y recomputed from trust-weighted sides. Standalone (SF-P4/[[FIX-608]] brigade signal is complementary - its same-direction cluster detector structurally misses the balanced-split farm - not a dependency). Harness: F10 gap->handled (MISMATCH->MATCH); F2 raters aged (else it regresses - mechanically identical to F10); add positive-control fixture (aged/varied cross-bloc must STILL score). Conservative: a null score is not a penalty; a false gamed that tanks a real bridge is the worse error. SF-P5, bible §11.1 F10/§12. Depends on [[FIX-572]], [[FIX-601]]. <!--id:FIX-612-->

- [x] 🟠 S — **Middleware writes rotated auth cookies to response only — post-then-get flows can read as anon (FIX-784 root cause)** — The auth-refresh block in apps/civitics/middleware.ts writes Supabase's rotated session cookies to the RESPONSE only (response.cookies.set), not to the forwarded request. This deviates from the canonical @supabase/ssr Next.js pattern, which also does request.cookies.set + rebuilds NextResponse.next({request}) so the refreshed session reaches route handlers / server components in the SAME request. Consequence: when the access token is expired and middleware refreshes it (rotating + consuming the old refresh token), the handler re-reads the pre-rotation cookie, its getUser() falls back to the already-consumed refresh token, and it authenticates as ANON. This is the root cause behind [[FIX-784]] (saved views: the post-save reload GET that lost the rotation race read anon → empty-200 → wiped list). FIX-784 made saved-views immune by broadcasting deltas instead of re-fetching, but the middleware bug is latent for ANY post-then-get / rapid-successive authed flow. Fix: adopt the canonical request+response cookie write in setAll. References [[FIX-784]]. <!--id:FIX-785-->

The page query at [apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx](../apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx) filtered to `relationship_type='donation'` exclusively, hiding all FIX-240 Schedule E rows. The `official_id` top-level column was dropped. `OfficialCard.tsx` still queries `.eq("official_id", id)` for the donor-count + total-donations totals — PostgREST silently returns `0` rows for the non-existent column, so the official card shows `0 donors` / `$0` regardless of actual data. Same class of bug FIX-223 fixed for the homepage Wave 3 (`official_homepage_stats_mv` filtered on `to_type='official' AND to_id=...` instead of the dropped column). Surfaced during the FIX-301 audit at `docs/audits/request-path-aggregations.md`; noted there as a correctness finding outside the perf scope of that audit. Two-path fix: (a) easy — change `.eq("official_id", id)` to `.eq("to_type","official").eq("to_id",id).eq("relationship_type","donation")` matching the FIX-223 MV's filter; (b) better — read `official_homepage_stats_mv` so the card uses the same precomputed numbers the homepage already trusts. Blanche`, `Crew v. Ratliff`, `Diaz-Jimenez v. Blanche`, `Pesticide Action & Agroecology Network v. Jackson Women's Health Organization`) — likely from legistar. - [x] 🔴 S — **fec_bulk PGRST116 on DC jurisdiction seed — narrow existence-check by parent_id** — seedJurisdictions() in [packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts](../packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts) uses .maybeSingle() on (fips_code, type) to detect existing state-equivalent rows. For STATE_DATA entries with type='district' (DC + the 5 [[FIX-376]] territories), this collides with TIGER-derived sub-district rows that inherit the parent's fips_code. Prod has 'District of Columbia Delegate District (at Large)' (DC-98) at fips_code='11', type='district', parent_id=DC's canonical id — second match → PGRST116. Surfaced by [[FIX-382]] Data Health visibility into fec_bulk failures. Root cause: existence check ignores hierarchy depth — top-level state-equivalents and TIGER sub-districts both match. Fix: add .eq('parent_id', federalId) to narrow the check to direct children of the federal jurisdiction. The DC-98 row is legitimate TIGER data and stays. No migration needed — pure code fix. Other callers of seedJurisdictions() (10 nightly pipelines) all hit the same swallowed-error path: stateIds.get('DC') silently returns undefined when the catch fires, so DC-related data has been silently degraded across the board on prod since DC-98 landed 2026-05-09. <!--id:FIX-383-->

Result: Stacey Plaskett + 49 territory FEC candidates currently sit on the federal jurisdiction row instead of their territory rows. Distinct from [[FIX-383]] (which was the PGRST116 swallow on DC seed) — this is a pure name-mismatch lookup miss on the territory path. Two viable fixes: (a) one-off backfill UPDATE in the spirit of the DC backfill from [[FIX-383]] cleanup, or (b) small code change in [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/candidates.ts) adding a `stateAbbr`-keyed second lookup so "VI" matches regardless of full-name string. Preferred shape is (b) + a one-shot backfill for the existing mis-attributed rows so future runs are self-healing. Affects 5 territories ([[FIX-376]] scope: VI, PR, GU, AS, MP) wherever Congress.gov / FEC string differs from STATE_DATA.name. <!--id:FIX-385-->

Norton would self-heal via nightly congress/officials updates; the 34 candidates would not (fec-bulk upserts but never overwrites existing jurisdiction_id). Cross-ref [[FIX-383]] (root cause), [[FIX-385]] (parallel territory-delegate name-mismatch). <!--id:FIX-389-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Search and summary routes filter/select bare comment_period_end on proposals (metadata-only post-cutover)** — Pattern-1 silent-zero (JSONB-path-vs-top-level-column). `apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts:377,382,383,389` selects, range-filters (.gte/.lte) and orders proposals by bare column `comment_period_end`; `apps/civitics/app/api/search/entity/route.ts:94` and `apps/civitics/app/api/proposals/[id]/summary/route.ts:76` select it too (the summary route also selects bare `agency_id`). Post-cutover both are metadata-only on proposals — migration `20260421000002_stage1_03_proposals.sql` defines no such columns and the old top-level column was dropped at the `20260422000000` promotion; migration `20260520000000` documents this exact class (bare filter silently no-op-d by PostgREST). Impact: public proposal search date-range filter and ordering silently do nothing, the comment-deadline field is always null, and the summary route never generates AI summaries because its isOpen check and agency lookup both read null. Fix: use `metadata->>comment_period_end` (expression index exists, FIX-359) and `metadata->>agency_id`. Precedent [[FIX-303]], [[FIX-359]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28, adversarially verified vs migrations. <!--id:FIX-425-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Rule-tagger selects nonexistent votes.proposal_id (real column is bill_proposal_id)** — Pattern-1 silent-zero. `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts:383` runs `.from(votes).select(official_id, proposal_id, vote)` but `proposal_id` is not a column on votes — the real FK is `bill_proposal_id` (used correctly at line 795 of the same file). So `v.proposal_id` is undefined and the `votesByOfficial` / `yesVotesByProposal` maps (lines 403-414) are keyed on undefined; the bipartisan / cross-party vote-pattern tags are computed from broken indexes and write wrong or zero tags to entity_tags (surfaced on official profiles). Fix: project `bill_proposal_id` and update the map keys. Precedent [[FIX-303]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-426-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Rule-tagger loads officials/financial_relationships/proposals unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (`supabase/config.toml` max_rows=1000). The rule-based tagger `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` loads whole tables with no .range()/.limit() then consumes them as complete Maps: `:365` officials, `:388` financial_relationships donation→official (~1M rows on prod), `:778` proposals (~73k). Past 1000 rows the loads silently truncate, so donor-pattern tags (pac_heavy/grassroots/large_donor_funded), bipartisan tags and pre_vote_timing tags derive from a tiny prefix — at ~1M financial_relationships it sees ~0.1% of donations. Fix: paginate via .range() loops (or aggregate server-side) before building the lookup maps. Precedent [[FIX-422]], [[FIX-219]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28, verified vs config.toml + migrations. <!--id:FIX-427-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph connections route loads entity_connections unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000). `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/connections/route.ts` runs unbounded .select() on entity_connections (a derived table that scales to ~1.9M edges) at lines 239,240 (neighbor connection counts), 264,265 and 339,340 (auto-expand + top-10 edge hydration). Each result is consumed as complete — counts feed the auto-expand decision, rows feed connMap for graph rendering. For any high-degree node the result silently caps at 1000, so neighbor counts undercount (wrong collapsed/expanded state) and edges past 1000 vanish from the rendered graph with HTTP 200. Fix: add .limit(1001) overflow detection or .range() pagination per entity. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-428-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **notify-followers cron loads user_follows unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap, latent)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000), latent. `apps/civitics/app/api/cron/notify-followers/route.ts:51` and `:115` load user_follows filtered only by entity_type with no pagination, then dedupe into Sets treated as the complete follower set for notification fan-out (admin client bypasses RLS, so the PostgREST 1000-row cap applies). Once any entity_type accumulates >1000 follow rows, followers past the first 1000 silently get no notifications. Dormant today (follow volume < 1k) but a correctness landmine as the platform scales. Fix: .range() pagination loop. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-429-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **ai-tagger active-officials count load unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap, cost-gate estimate)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (max_rows=1000), internal. `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-tagger.ts:616` loads active officials via `.select(id).eq(is_active,true)` with no limit (the sibling proposals load at line 613 uses .limit(2000)), then computes the untagged-officials count from `(data ?? []).filter(...).length` at line 623. If active officials exceed 1000 the count is wrong, skewing the AI-tagger cost-gate estimate (the non-onlyNew branch at line 628 already uses head:true count correctly). Internal estimate only, no user-facing data. Fix: use { count: exact, head: true } for cardinality, or paginate if the id list is needed. Precedent [[FIX-422]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-430-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **withDbTimeout callers ignore error/timeout — silent empty/zero (connections graph, homepage donor stat)** — Pattern-3 silent-zero (withDbTimeout returns data:null on timeout but callers ignore error). `apps/civitics/app/api/graph/connections/route.ts:224` throws only on donationsRes.error, then lines 226-228 merge votesRes.data and oversightRes.data blind — on a votes/oversight timeout the connection graph silently drops those edge types and still returns HTTP 200 (partial graph presented as complete); the chunked financial_entities loads at :391 have the same per-result unchecked-error issue. `apps/civitics/app/page.tsx:354` renders the homepage donor-records hero stat as 0 on timeout via `liveDonorRes.count ?? 0`. Fix: check error before consuming and surface a stale/empty state instead of a silent zero. Precedent [[FIX-327]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-431-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Institution page reads pipeline_state via non-admin client — RLS silent-zero** — Pattern-5 silent-zero (RLS). `apps/civitics/app/institutions/[id]/page.tsx:485` reads pipeline_state with the non-admin createServerClient from line 236. pipeline_state has RLS enabled and zero SELECT policies (migration `0012_entity_tags.sql:107`, commented internal-pipeline-metadata service-role-only), so the anon/authenticated read returns null with no error — plumLastChange (plum-book last-updated date on the institution page) is always blank regardless of DB state. The FIX-398 precedent table external_source_refs was since fixed (migration `20260526000002_xsr_public_read_rls.sql` added a public read policy); this is a new instance of the same class. Fix: read pipeline_state via createAdminClient for this one field. Precedent [[FIX-398]]. Found by silent-zero sweep 2026-05-28. <!--id:FIX-432-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Rule-tagger tagProposals() loads proposals unbounded (PostgREST 1000-row cap)** — Pattern-2 silent-zero (config.toml max_rows=1000). `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` tagProposals() ran `.from(proposals).select(...)` with no .range()/.limit() (~73k proposals) then iterated the result, so urgency/new/agency-sector/national-scope rule tags were only ever written for the first 1,000 proposals (1.4%). Distinct from [[FIX-427]], whose :778 line-ref is tagPreVoteConnections' proposal-title lookup, not tagProposals. Fixed alongside FIX-426/427: paginated via fetchAllPaged + authoritative DELETE-then-reinsert (stale time-sensitive urgent/closing_soon/new tags no longer accumulate). Verified local: 73,027 proposals now processed, 4,619 tags. <!--id:FIX-436-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Rule-tagger tagPreVoteConnections + tagFinancialEntities still load unbounded after FIX-426/427** — Remaining Pattern-2 silent-zeros in `packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/rules.ts` not resolved by the FIX-426/427 tagOfficials rewrite. (1) tagPreVoteConnections loads financial_relationships (:746), votes (:759) and proposals (:778) unbounded — the :778/pre_vote_timing portion is explicitly named by [[FIX-427]] but needs a different fix: the donation×vote within-90-days cross-join is ~65M qualifying pairs (collapsing to ~371k distinct financial_entities) and the current code does one upsert round-trip PER pair, so naive .range() pagination would attempt ~65M upserts. Needs an SQL rollup that aggregates to distinct entities + batched upsert. (2) tagFinancialEntities loads financial_relationships (:626, from_type=financial_entity) and financial_entities (:636) unbounded → donation-size + industry rule tags computed from the first 1,000 rows only. Fix: server-side aggregation / .range() pagination as appropriate. Precedent [[FIX-427]], [[FIX-436]]. <!--id:FIX-437-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **compute_alignment_score RPC references nonexistent votes.proposal_id (real column bill_proposal_id)** — Same bug class as [[FIX-426]], different site. `supabase/migrations/20260425102000_compute_alignment_score.sql:27` joins `votes v ON v.proposal_id = cc.proposal_id`, but votes has no proposal_id column — the real FK is bill_proposal_id (confirmed vs live schema: votes columns are id, bill_proposal_id, official_id, vote, voted_at, …). The function is SECURITY DEFINER plpgsql, so the embedded query is parsed at execution → every call to the FIX-042 USER-node alignment tooltip errors at runtime with 'column v.proposal_id does not exist' (alignment_ratio/matched_votes never compute). Fix: new migration with CREATE OR REPLACE projecting bill_proposal_id (cc.proposal_id stays — civic_comments DOES have proposal_id). Found during FIX-426 schema verification. <!--id:FIX-438-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **votes.proposal_id→bill_proposal_id rename completeness — 6 read sites** — FIX-426 fixed only tagOfficials + rollup RPCs; the post-cutover rename was never applied repo-wide. This sweeps the remaining read paths — graph/matrix + graph/connections routes, OfficialCard/OfficialGraph two-step embeds, notify-followers cron, and officials/[id] page (two broken proposals!bill_proposal_id embeds the original ticket missed) — and absorbs [[FIX-438]] (compute_alignment_score join). Also fixed two sibling silent-schema breaks surfaced in graph/matrix: officials.state and officials.chamber columns do not exist post-cutover (both live in metadata) and 500'd the route before the votes fetch was even read; and graph/connections read vote_question off metadata when it is a first-class column (procedural filter was failing open). votes embeds via proposals do NOT resolve (no direct FK) — two-step manual fetch is the prescribed shape. <!--id:FIX-440-->

### DB PERFORMANCE / COMPUTE

- [x] 🟠 M — **Supabase IOWait diagnosis — read-only audit pass** — Read-only diagnostic against prod (Pro Small, 1 GB RAM, 256 MB shared_buffers) covering top IO consumers (pg_stat_statements), burst contention (pg_stat_activity sampling), working set vs shared_buffers, unused/oversized indexes, table bloat ([[FIX-346]] re-measure), cron schedule overlap, and rebuild/snapshot shape. Output: [docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md](audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md) with 12 findings ranked by expected IO impact (top 3: incrementalise rebuild_entity_connections, verify get_connection_type_counts MV is on the read path, add proposals(metadata->>agency_id) expression index). FIX-346 closable per Section F — autovacuum has caught up. Follow-up implementation FIXes will be specced from the audit in a separate Cowork session. Script preserved at packages/data/src/scripts/iowait-diagnosis.ts (pnpm data:iowait-diagnosis) for re-runs. <!--id:FIX-352-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`get_connection_type_counts()` RPC reads `connection_type_counts` table only** — Re-issue `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for the RPC so its body is the pure 16-row read from `public.connection_type_counts` (originally landed in [[FIX-338]]), and tighten function-level `statement_timeout` from FIX-298's 120s safety net down to 5s. Audit 2026-05-24 §B #2 showed 1,028 calls × 5,992 ms × 38.9M blks — exactly the live-aggregate cost the FIX-338 materialization was supposed to remove, indicating a missed deploy or pg_stat_statements history from before FIX-338 landed. Idempotent on environments already on the materialized read path. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100000_fix_a_connection_type_counts_rpc_uses_mv.sql`. Expected drop on prod: ~30% of total request-path IO. <!--id:FIX-358-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`proposals` metadata expression indexes (`agency_id` re-affirmed, `comment_period_end` new)** — Add expression indexes on every `proposals (metadata->>'<key>')` that appears in a runtime WHERE filter. Two distinct shapes confirmed by codebase grep + EXPLAIN ANALYZE: (a) `agency_id` equality — already had `idx_proposals_metadata_agency_id` from [[FIX-303]], re-issued `IF NOT EXISTS` to guarantee prod presence; (b) `comment_period_end` range (`.gt` / `.lt` / `.order`) — new index `proposals_metadata_comment_period_end_expr`. Local EXPLAIN before/after on the `comment_period_end > now` shape: `Parallel Seq Scan` (11,531 buffers, 22.5 ms) → `Index Scan` (46 buffers, 0.24 ms), 250× buffer reduction. Eliminates the bulk of the 26,068 seq_scans + 1.64B tup_read of `proposals` flagged in audit 2026-05-24 §B #3 + #8 + §D hot tables. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100001_fix_b_proposals_metadata_expr_indexes.sql`. <!--id:FIX-359-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`entity_connections (from_id, connection_type)` compound index** — Investigation outcome: **Branch A — index missing**. `pg_indexes` on `entity_connections` shows `entity_connections_from (from_type, from_id)` leading with `from_type`, but the audit's query #9 shape (`WHERE from_id = X AND connection_type = Y LIMIT/OFFSET`) omits `from_type`, leaving the planner to do an Index Scan on `(from_type, from_id)` and per-row filter by `connection_type`. Local EXPLAIN before/after on a busy `from_id`: Index Scan on `entity_connections_from` (15,998 buffers, 117.7 ms, 2,998 rows discarded by Filter) → Index Scan on the new `entity_connections_from_id_connection_type` (3 buffers, 0.06 ms). 5,300× buffer reduction. Addresses audit 2026-05-24 §B #9 — the worst cache-hit ratio (3.9%) in the top 25 queries. Migration: `supabase/migrations/20260524100002_fix_c_entity_connections_from_id_connection_type_idx.sql`. <!--id:FIX-360-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Drop 7 unused-and-large indexes (~1.05 GB working-set shrink)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-A** (audit `docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E). Drops seven indexes verified `idx_scan = 0` in the audit window and re-verified at ship time via `docs/audits/scratch/2026-05-24-round2-preflight.ts` against prod. Ships in `supabase/migrations/20260524230000_fix_a_drop_unused_indexes.sql` — plain `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS` with `SET lock_timeout = '5s'`. Drops: `entity_connections_strength` (468 MB), `financial_relationships_metadata_gin` (246 MB), `entity_connections_amount` (123 MB), `financial_entities_display_trgm_individual` (117 MB), `financial_relationships_occurred_at` (72 MB), `shadow_proposals_search_vector` (12 MB), `external_source_refs_metadata_gin` (11 MB). The point is structural cache-pressure relief: 256 MB shared_buffers ÷ 12.5 GB top-3-table working set ≈ 2%; freeing ~1 GB of index pages leaves more room for the indexes actually backing hot queries. <!--id:FIX-364-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **`donor_fingerprint_pattern` (166 MB) kept — in active use by EDGAR matcher (FIX-B redirect)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-B — closed-as-redirected.** The audit (`docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E) flagged `financial_entities_donor_fingerprint_pattern` (text_pattern_ops, 166 MB) as a candidate drop pending grep. Pre-ship grep found a live `.like("donor_fingerprint", "${probe}|%")` call at `packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/matcher.ts:88` — the [[FIX-253]] EDGAR exec-to-FEC donor matcher, which uses this exact index for prefix-range scans on canonical name. Index kept; no migration shipped. Closed as `Verified: closes-as-redirected` to [[FIX-367]] for "is the pattern index hot enough to justify its size, or can the LIKE callsites be rewritten to use the unique index's equality shape?" investigation. <!--id:FIX-365-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Drop 3 true-duplicate indexes (officials, agencies, graph_snapshots)** — **IOWait Round 2 / FIX-C** (audit `docs/audits/2026-05-24-iowait-diagnosis.md` §E). Three pairs re-verified byte-identical at ship time via `docs/audits/scratch/2026-05-24-round2-preflight.ts` against prod. Drops the legacy unprefixed names — `officials_full_name_trgm` (6.5 MB) and `agencies_name_trgm` (120 kB) — keeping the `idx_<table>_<col>_<type>` form (the `0008_search_indexes.sql` codebase convention). Also drops `idx_graph_snapshots_code` (8 kB, non-unique) since the UNIQUE constraint `graph_snapshots_code_key` already serves equality lookups and enforces the dedup invariant. Hygiene cleanup (~6.8 MB total); main value is removing the footgun where future renames would need to track two indexes. Ships in `supabase/migrations/20260524230001_fix_c_drop_duplicate_indexes.sql`. <!--id:FIX-366-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Investigate `donor_fingerprint_pattern` hot-ness — can EDGAR matcher's LIKE calls be rewritten to use the equality index?** — Follow-up to [[FIX-365]] (IOWait Round 2 / FIX-B closed-as-redirected). The 166 MB `text_pattern_ops` index exists solely to back `.like("donor_fingerprint", "<canonical>|%")` in the [[FIX-253]] EDGAR exec-to-FEC matcher (`packages/data/src/pipelines/edgar/matcher.ts:88`). EDGAR runs weekly; per-run query count is bounded by the def14a parser output. Open questions: (1) measure actual `pg_stat_user_indexes.idx_scan` for `financial_entities_donor_fingerprint_pattern` over a weekly cycle — if it's only firing on EDGAR runs, the 166 MB amortized over a weekly hot-path workload may not be worth keeping; (2) can the LIKE prefix scan be rewritten as an equality-on-fingerprint shape if the matcher pre-computes the canonical-name fingerprint prefix and uses the existing UNIQUE `donor_fingerprint_unique` index instead; (3) does the existing canonical-name → fingerprint normalization in `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts` produce enough ZIP-suffix variants per donor to make the prefix scan necessary, or are there typically only 1-3 distinct ZIPs per donor (which a small `IN ()` over equality lookups would handle equally well)? Decision criteria: if EDGAR-run `idx_scan` is < 1000/week, drop the pattern index and rewrite the matcher to use either the unique index or a small range of equality probes. <!--id:FIX-367-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Close FIX-367 — donor_fingerprint_pattern index analysis** — Investigation work for [[FIX-367]] — does the 166 MB `text_pattern_ops` index on `financial_entities.donor_fingerprint` justify its working-set cost vs rewriting the EDGAR matcher to use an equality-shape index? Outcome will be either (a) keep the index, close FIX-367 as `closes-as-recognized` because the prefix scan is structurally necessary, or (b) allocate a new FIX-D for the matcher rewrite and close FIX-367 as `closes-as-redirected`. Either way, the rewrite itself is out of scope for this PR. <!--id:FIX-371-->

- [x] 🟠 M — **Merge officials casing dupes — execute the merge plan from FIX-325 investigation** — Reads the cluster shape from docs/audits/<date>-officials-casing-dupes.md (FIX-325 investigation script) and executes the merge. Rewrite FK references — use the FK rewrite block from supabase/migrations/20260525051720_executive_seed_and_delegate_jurisdictions.sql Block C as the canonical pattern, generalized across the cluster rather than a single (elected,candidate) pair. **Out of scope, do NOT auto-merge:** (a) clusters whose tier-array contains both 'elected' AND 'candidate' for different real people — [[FIX-248]] promotion handles that pair specifically; (b) FEC candidate cycle-row clusters (e.g. Chris Capparell 7× across 7 cycles — same person, different fec_candidate_id per cycle is the FEC norm, not a bug) — these need a per-cycle dedup strategy that preserves the historical cycle binding rather than a blanket merge. Local 5/25 baseline: 1,829 dupe clusters / 4,108 rows. Top sources by row count: fec_candidate_id (1,955 rows), openstates_id (267), plum_id (202), congress_nomination_id (112), fec_id (88), congress_gov (78). Pattern to mirror: [[FIX-271]] cross-source resolution backfill. <!--id:FIX-375-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **us-states.ts STATE_DATA: seed 5 territorial jurisdictions (AS/GU/MP/PR/VI) so congress writer routes territorial delegates correctly** — **Shipped in same PR as [[FIX-321]] data fix — filed for completeness/audit.** Adds the 5 U.S. territory entries (American Samoa, Guam, Northern Mariana Islands, Puerto Rico, U.S. Virgin Islands) to packages/data/src/jurisdictions/us-states.ts STATE_DATA with type='district' (mirrors DC's existing convention — jurisdiction_type enum has no 'territory' value). This is the upstream writer-side counterpart to the migration in [[FIX-321]] Block B: before this fix, congress/officials.ts at line 131 (stateIds.get(member.state) ?? federalId) fell through to the 'United States' umbrella jurisdiction for territorial delegates because seedJurisdictions only ever seeded the 50 states + DC. The migration creates the 5 jurisdiction rows + re-routes the 5 misrouted delegate rows AS A ONE-SHOT; this writer fix ensures the next congress sync (and every subsequent one) keeps them mapped correctly. No code change in congress/officials.ts itself was needed — the existing stateIds.get(member.state) lookup uses full state names which Congress.gov returns verbatim for territorial reps (e.g. 'Puerto Rico', 'Guam'). <!--id:FIX-376-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Eleanor Holmes Norton (DC) misrouted to 'United States' jurisdiction on prod** — Adjacent finding surfaced during [[FIX-321]] prod verification (5/25). Local already had Norton at the DC district jurisdiction (fips=11, type='district'); prod had her at the federal 'United States' umbrella (fips=00, type='country'). Root cause: on prod, the congress.gov officials ingest wrote her to federalId at a time before OpenStates' DC pass had landed (DC, like the 5 territories prior to FIX-376, wasn't in STATE_DATA initially as a state — only added as type='district' later). Once the writer's stateIds.get('District of Columbia') started resolving, new congress runs would have written her correctly, but the existing prod row was never UPDATEd. Shipped as a one-shot migration alongside the FIX-321 PR: supabase/migrations/20260525053643_norton_dc_delegate_jurisdiction.sql. Idempotent guard: only fires when she's currently at the federal umbrella. <!--id:FIX-377-->

Writes `proposals.summary_plain` → requires a prod backfill re-run (data-state change, run local+prod separately). Confirm each API actually supplies the text before building. Recommended order: OpenStates (free) → Congress CRS → reassess regulations. <!--id:FIX-435-->

Stage 3 (table collapse, agencies removal, 31 callsite refactor, slug backfill, /institutions index) are separate follow-up FIXes. <!--id:FIX-418-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **/institutions/[id] governing-body substance + middleware UUID guards for /districts /officials /proposals** — Two pieces under one ID, building on [[FIX-418]] (institutions view) and [[FIX-424]] shared cards. Agencies-typed retains existing rich content from FIX-418 (untouched). (2) Extends [[FIX-433]] middleware UUID-format guard to /districts/[id], /officials/[id], /proposals/[id] (single combined regex). /institutions/[id] middleware guard DEFERRED by design: its page resolves non-UUID slugs to UUIDs via a governing_bodies.slug DB lookup, which the edge can't replicate, so a format guard would 404 legitimate slug URLs — the page keeps not-found ownership. SCHEMA-REALITY CORRECTIONS vs the spec (verified live, not 0001): votes has NO governing_body_id/proposal_id — it carries bill_proposal_id + official_id + roll_call_id + vote_question (a real column, not metadata->>); votes scope to an institution via officials.governing_body_id. officials.party is a lowercase enum (democrat/republican/independent/...), not 'Democratic'/'Republican'. Live vote values are yes/no/not_voting/present only. bill_number lives in bill_details(proposal_id), not metadata->>legis_num (unpopulated). institution_extensions is empty (0 rows) so the committees tree self-omits until backfill. notFound-under-Suspense inventory confirmed: 4 affected routes (jurisdictions already fixed by FIX-433; districts/officials/proposals fixed here; institutions deferred); donors/[id] + initiatives/[id] are NOT affected (no loading.tsx, so notFound already returns a real 404). VERIFICATION: pnpm build clean; migration applies local; RPC returns correct party-line/unanimous in ~500ms on US Senate; all page queries run clean against live DB; middleware guards return 404 for malformed and let slugs through. Local browser render of /institutions/[id] is blocked by a PRE-EXISTING env issue (Cannot find module ./vendor-chunks/@smithy+core — pnpm + Next 14 next start/dev vendor-chunk bug, also flagged by Invalid next.config serverExternalPackages warning); confirmed via stash test that ORIGINAL code 500s identically, so unrelated to this change. Prod render to be confirmed via post-deploy curl. RPC migration to prod (schema change) pending explicit user approval. <!--id:FIX-439-->

- [x] 🟢 S — **Add R2 cache layer for FEC bulk files** — Follow-up to FIX-181. The indiv pipeline currently downloads `indiv{yy}.zip` (~2 GB) from `fec.gov/files/bulk-downloads` on every run. R2 plumbing exists in [packages/db/src/storage.ts](packages/db/src/storage.ts) but is unused by the FEC pipeline. Add a HEAD-based freshness check: on each run, HEAD `civitics-cache/fec/indiv{yy}.zip` in R2 + HEAD the FEC URL; if R2 is fresh (Last-Modified ≥ FEC's), download from R2 instead. After successful FEC download, upload to R2 in the background. Saves ~10 minutes per repeat run + insulates against FEC bulk-download outages. Requires `@aws-sdk/lib-storage` for multipart upload. Same pattern can be retrofitted to pas2/cm/weball. Defer until cadence justifies it (pipeline runs more than once a quarter). <!--id:FIX-192-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Verify weekly FEC cron handles indiv stage cleanly + add a `closed-cycles skip` knob** — FIX-181 lands `FEC_INCLUDE_INDIV=true` as the pipeline default, so the weekly nightly orchestrator at [packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts:464-468](packages/data/src/pipelines/index.ts#L464-L468) (which runs `FEC_CYCLES={prev},{current}`, currently 2024,2026) now downloads two indiv zips totalling ~5.5 GB and streams ~80M rows per Sunday run. - [x] 🔴 L — **Surface individual donors in search + ingest super-PAC indiv contributions (Form 3X Schedule A)** — Anchor symptom: Elon Musk's ~$277M to America PAC (2024) is in the public FEC bulk feed but does not appear in Civitics search. Two independent root causes ganged up: (a) [api/search/route.ts:484-489](../apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts#L484-L489) unconditionally excluded `entity_type='individual'` whenever no entity_type filter was set, making all ~540K individual donors invisible by default — fixed by only excluding individuals in pure browse mode (no query + no industry/amount filter); search also now tries the reversed `LAST, FIRST` form of any 2-token query so "Elon Musk" matches FEC's stored `MUSK, ELON`. (b) [fec-bulk/indiv.ts:200-203](../packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/indiv.ts#L200-L203) filtered every indiv line through `cmteToCandId` (ccl P/A only), silently dropping individual contributions to super PACs, party committees, and other non-candidate-authorized PACs — fixed by adding a parallel `nonCandCmtes` set (CMTE_TP O/X/Y/Z/N/Q/V/W in cm{yy} but NOT in ccl P/A) and routing those lines into a new (donor × committee × cycle) aggregation. New writer `upsertIndividualToCommitteeDonationsBatch` writes them as `financial_relationships` rows with `to_type='financial_entity'`, `source='fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee'`. No SQL migration — schema and unique-index arbiter already cover the new `to_type` combo. `rebuild_entity_connections()` derives the donor → super_PAC graph edge automatically on the next nightly rebuild. Joint-fundraising (J), leadership (D), and B designations stay excluded — their money is re-itemized via transfers downstream and would double-count. **Prod data backfill requires `pnpm data:fec-bulk` against prod to land the new super-PAC indiv rows** (the next Sunday cron picks it up automatically). <!--id:FIX-236-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Normalize donor `canonical_name` to natural-order + add trgm GIN index** — Donor `canonical_name` is currently set from the FEC `LAST FIRST` fingerprint (writer.ts:217), so a natural-order search like "Elon Musk" can't use an index-served prefix match — FIX-236 worked around it with a per-query OR fallback that escapes commas. Cleaner long-term: reorder to `FIRST LAST` form at write time, add `CREATE INDEX … USING GIN(canonical_name gin_trgm_ops)`, and have search query `canonical_name` directly. Requires a one-off backfill of the ~540K existing individual rows (run as a `data:` script — SELECT id, fingerprint → UPDATE canonical_name = reorder(fingerprint)). Display_name stays as the raw FEC source-cased value. <!--id:FIX-238-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Donor `donor_fingerprint` fragmentation by middle initial + ZIP changes** — `donorFingerprint = upper(NAME) + "|" + ZIP5`. "MUSK, ELON @ 94027" and "MUSK, ELON R @ 94027" become two `financial_entities` rows; "MUSK, ELON @ 94027" and "MUSK, ELON @ 78704" (donor filed from a second residence) also split. Decide on a dedup approach (strip middle initial / suffix? cluster by name + city + employer? add a `dedup_cluster_id` derived nightly?). Investigate before backfilling — wrong heuristic can collapse genuinely-distinct people (common surname + same first name in same ZIP). <!--id:FIX-239-->

America PAC's $277M was spent supporting Trump via independent expenditures, none of which appear in pas2 (24K/24Z transactions). `oth{yy}.zip` carries IE transactions (Schedule E) and `oppexp{yy}.zip` carries detailed operating expenditures. Stream them line-by-line like indiv, aggregate by (committee × candidate × cycle), upsert as `financial_relationships` with `relationship_type='donation'`, `metadata.source='fec_bulk_ie'`. Closes the graph trail from any megadonor through their super PAC to the candidate(s) the PAC supports. <!--id:FIX-240-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Verify next Sunday FEC cron lands green on GHA after FIX-192/193 + heap bump** — FIX-192 (R2 cache), FIX-193 (FEC_INDIV_CYCLES + Last-Modified watermark), and the [.github/workflows/nightly.yml](../.github/workflows/nightly.yml) heap/timeout bump (NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192, timeout-minutes=120) all ship as one bundle and are locally verified.
