- [x] 🟠 S — **`ai-summaries` pipeline still references dropped `proposals.comment_period_end` column** — `packages/data/src/pipelines/ai-summaries/index.ts` `fetchOpenProposals` query throws `column proposals.comment_period_end does not exist` post-cutover. Same drift pattern as FIX-112 (which fixed `tags/rules.ts`) but in a different file. Result: queue-mode staging skipped all open proposals — only officials got enqueued (0 proposals vs ~1k expected). Update the query to use the post-cutover column (likely `metadata->>'comment_period_end'` or similar). <!--id:FIX-161-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **Treemap drops officials with $0 donations — "Full Senate" shows ~25, not 100** — [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap/route.ts:198-211](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap/route.ts#L198-L211) builds `rows[]` by iterating `totalByOfficial`, the map populated only by donation rows. Senators with no `financial_relationships` rows never get pushed. Post-cutover Pro data has incomplete FEC seeding, so 75 of 100 senators are silently missing from the treemap; "Full House" similarly under-counts. Fix: iterate `officialById` (full set) and default `totalCents=0` when missing. Also drop the unconditional `rows.slice(0, 200)` at line 211 — it caps Full House at 200 of 435 reps. <!--id:FIX-172-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **PAC treemap ignores industry filter — "Finance PACs" shows same view as "Energy PACs"** — [packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx:239-242](packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx#L239-L242) calls `/api/graph/treemap-pac?groupBy=sector` for every PAC group with no industry parameter, and [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts) doesn't accept one. Result: every industry PAC group ("Finance", "Energy", "Healthcare", etc.) renders the same global all-sectors view, completely silent. Fix: (a) `treemap-pac/route.ts` accepts `?industry=Finance`, filters PAC ids via `fetchEntityIdsByIndustryTag`, returns one group with all matching PACs; (b) `TreemapGraph.tsx` forwards `primaryGroup.filter.industry` into the URL when present. <!--id:FIX-173-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **PAC sector treemap caps per sector at 20 — drill-down hides bulk of data** — [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts:95-109](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts#L95-L109) limits each sector to top 20 donors. With ~5,000 tagged PACs this is severe under-representation when a user drills into a sector cell. Raise per-sector cap to 100 in the global view, and to all when single-industry filter is applied (FIX-173). Surface `topShown / total` counts in response metadata so the UI can render "showing top N of M". <!--id:FIX-174-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Group route's state filter doesn't honor FIX-124 fallback chain — "By state" groups under-count federal officials** — [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/group/route.ts:69](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/group/route.ts#L69) only filters on `metadata->>state.eq.${state}`. FIX-124 added `metadata.state_abbr` and `jurisdictions.short_name` as fallbacks for federal officials; this route ignores both. Federal senators/reps whose state is only in `state_abbr` are excluded from any state-filtered group. Matches HIT_LIST entries "By state group not working" and "treemap all officials by state — state unknown". Fix: extract `deriveState` and the `.or(...)` clause from the treemap route into a shared helper and reuse here. <!--id:FIX-175-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Remove `group-federal-judges` built-in group — backing data does not exist** — [packages/graph/src/groups.ts:103-114](packages/graph/src/groups.ts#L103-L114) defines a "Federal Judges" group with `entity_type: 'official', party: 'nonpartisan'` and no chamber. Federal judges are not in the `officials` table (they're not elected), so the filter returns whatever active nonpartisan officials happen to exist (state nonpartisans, etc.) — wrong cohort. Remove from `BUILT_IN_GROUPS` and `GROUP_TREE` until the data is properly seeded with a `role` filter or dedicated table. <!--id:FIX-176-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **`sizeBy` controls in treemap settings have no effect** — [packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx:150-157](packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx#L150-L157) returns `1` for both `connection_count` and `vote_count` because the API never sends those counts. The user can switch sizeBy in GraphConfigPanel and nothing happens. Critical once FIX-172 lands: many senators will have $0 donations, so the only way to render them at non-zero size is `sizeBy=connection_count` or `vote_count`. Fix: `/api/graph/treemap` returns `connection_count` and `vote_count` per official from a batched `entity_connections` aggregate; wire into `TreemapRow` and `getSizeValue`. Floor any zero-resolved size with `Math.max(1, value)` so cells stay visible. <!--id:FIX-177-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Federal-official donation seeding is incomplete on Pro — most senators have zero `financial_relationships` rows** — Surfaced during the FIX-172 audit. The FIX-172 fix shows all 100 senators in the treemap, but ~75 will render at $0 because no donations have been ingested for them. The FEC bulk pipeline runs against `pas224.zip` (PAC-to-candidate); individual contributions and Senate-only itemized files may be missing or filtered out. Investigation: count `(SELECT COUNT(*) FROM officials WHERE role_title='Senator' AND id IN (SELECT to_id FROM financial_relationships WHERE relationship_type='donation' AND to_type='official'))` on Pro vs local. If gap is large, audit which FEC files are being ingested and which year/cycle filters apply. Likely candidate: `packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/` only loads PAC contributions, missing individual contributors that fund most Senate campaigns. <!--id:FIX-178-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **PAC industry-tag coverage gap — untagged PACs invisible in every industry viz** — Surfaced during the FIX-173 audit. [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts:58](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap-pac/route.ts#L58) silently skips PACs without an industry tag (`if (!ind) continue;`). Same `entity_tags` lookup gates `/api/graph/group` PAC mode (FIX-167 routed everything through tags after the `financial_entities.industry` column was dropped). Investigation: count tagged vs untagged PACs (`SELECT entity_type, COUNT(*) FROM financial_entities LEFT JOIN entity_tags ON ... WHERE entity_type IN ('pac','party_committee') GROUP BY tag IS NULL`). The enrichment_queue drains AI-tag PACs (FIX-101) but if the queue isn't drained or rules-based fallback misses some, those PACs vanish from every industry-filtered view. Fix path: surface untagged count in route metadata and prioritize draining the PAC tag queue. <!--id:FIX-179-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Pin-the-primary: which focus item drives single-entity vizes** — Single-entity vizes (treemap, sunburst, chord) currently follow the last-added focus item. Useful default but the user can't override it without removing/re-adding entries. Add `primary?: boolean` on `FocusEntity` / `FocusGroup`, render a ★ pin in FocusTree, mutually exclusive within type (one pinned entity + one pinned group at most). GraphPage selects pinned-then-last-added. Foundation for FIX-185 / FIX-186. <!--id:FIX-184-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Treemap Cohort × Filter slots — investigation feature** — The treemap can answer questions like "which Senate Democrats got the most money from Finance PACs?" only if cohort and filter are decoupled. Add `cohortFocusId` and `filterFocusId` to `TreemapOptions`. Two slots in GraphConfigPanel; auto-assign by `filter.entity_type` (officials → cohort, PACs → filter). Extend [apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/graph/treemap/route.ts) with a `?filter=...` mode that constrains the donation aggregate to donors matching the filter group (industry tag, party, etc.). Falls back to current single-primary behavior when only one slot is filled — no breaking change. <!--id:FIX-185-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Treemap Compare mode** — When 2+ entities focused, render one top-level cell per focused entity, each subdivided by their donors. Shared donors get matching colors so overlap is visible at a glance. Toggle via new `TreemapOptions.compareMode`. Best for "how aligned are Warren's and Cruz's donor bases?" investigations. Disabled by default; activates in GraphConfigPanel when 2+ focus entities present. <!--id:FIX-186-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Dashboard self-test `connections_pipeline_healthy` permanently red post-cutover** — The check at [apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts) looks for a `data_sync_log` row with `pipeline='connections'`. Since FIX-100 (2026-04-24) connections derivation is the SQL function `rebuild_entity_connections()` invoked by the nightly orchestrator, which logs its outcome under `pipeline_state.cron_last_run.value.results.pipelines.entity_connections_rebuild` and a `data_sync_log` row keyed `pipeline='nightly_cron'` — nothing ever writes the `connections` key the self-test expects. The standalone `pnpm data:connections` TS pipeline at [packages/data/src/pipelines/connections/index.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/connections/index.ts) is also stale dead code (references dropped columns `financial_relationships.official_id`, the votes↔proposals join, `entity_connections.evidence` — all schema names from before the 2026-04-22 promotion). Fix: redirect the self-test to read `cron_last_run.results.pipelines.entity_connections_rebuild.status`, retain the `vote_yes count > 50000` sanity check. Follow-up: delete the broken `data:connections` script + `packages/data/src/pipelines/connections/index.ts` (separate cleanup; matches FIX-111 spirit). <!--id:FIX-187-->
- [x] 🟡 S — **Switch `packages/data/tsconfig.json` to bundler module resolution so subpath exports resolve** — [packages/data/tsconfig.json:6](packages/data/tsconfig.json#L6) uses legacy `moduleResolution: "Node"`, which doesn't read package.json `exports`. [packages/ai/package.json:7-13](packages/ai/package.json#L7-L13) declares subpath exports (`./cost-gate`, `./client`, `./cost-config`, `./cost-tracker`), so any `import … from "@civitics/ai/cost-gate"` fails type resolution under the data tsconfig — surfaced as 3× TS2307 errors at [ai-summaries/index.ts:30](packages/data/src/pipelines/ai-summaries/index.ts#L30), [tags/ai-classifier.ts:20](packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-classifier.ts#L20), [tags/ai-tagger.ts:23](packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-tagger.ts#L23). Validated: `module: "ES2022"` + `moduleResolution: "Bundler"` clears all three (TS5095 forbids bundler with module:CommonJS, so module needs the bump too). Runtime impact: `tsx` already understands both module modes, and `data` has no `tsc` build step — so flipping is safe. Worth confirming no other code paths assume CommonJS emit. <!--id:FIX-188-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Stop importing `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and `@supabase/supabase-js` directly from `packages/data`** — Three files reach across workspace boundaries to packages they don't list as direct deps: [tags/ai-classifier.ts:18](packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-classifier.ts#L18) and [tags/ai-tagger.ts:21](packages/data/src/pipelines/tags/ai-tagger.ts#L21) import `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (a dep of `@civitics/ai`); [scripts/copy-pac-tags-to-prod.ts:20](packages/data/src/scripts/copy-pac-tags-to-prod.ts#L20) imports `@supabase/supabase-js` (a dep of `@civitics/db`). Runtime works because pnpm's flat `.pnpm` store happens to expose them, but TS resolution fails (3× TS2307). Both directly violate the project conventions in `packages/db/CLAUDE.md` ("Import from `@civitics/db` — never directly from `@supabase/supabase-js`") and the same principle for `@civitics/ai`. Fix paths: (1) refactor the two AI tagger files to use `@civitics/ai`'s `createAiClient()` like `ai-summaries/index.ts` already does — keeps the Anthropic SDK behind the wrapper; (2) refactor `copy-pac-tags-to-prod.ts` to use `@civitics/db`'s admin client (or expose a small `createTwoEnvClients()` helper there since the script needs concurrent local + prod connections). <!--id:FIX-189-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Ingest individual contributions (`indiv24.zip`) — currently only PAC contributions are loaded** — Spun out of FIX-178. The 2GB FEC individual file is the largest data-volume gap in donation coverage; for many senators (esp. Sanders, Warren, Schiff) PAC money is a fraction of their total raise — the bulk comes from itemized individual contributions. Pending Cloudflare R2 setup (`packages/data/CLAUDE.md` §"Full 2GB FEC Individual File"). Plan: download `indiv{cycle}.zip` to R2, stream-parse line-by-line, match donor names to existing `financial_entities` (or upsert with `entity_type='individual'`), upsert per (donor, candidate, cycle) aggregate into `financial_relationships`. Reuse the same multi-cycle loop landed by FIX-178. Blocked on R2 account; reopen once payment method is set up. <!--id:FIX-181-->
- [x] 🟡 L — **Restructure `financial_entities` to dedicate an `individual_donor` sub-shape** — Follow-up to FIX-181. Once individual donors land, `financial_entities` will hold two populations with very different cardinality and access patterns: O(10⁴) named PAC/corporate entities (queried by name, industry tag, joined to relationships in dashboards) and O(10⁷) individual donors (only ever resolved by donor-fingerprint during ingest, never browsed by name). Mixing them under one CHECK-constrained `entity_type` table means `entity_tags`, search indexes, and the canonical-name UNIQUE constraint all carry millions of irrelevant rows. Options to evaluate: (a) split into `financial_entities` (PAC/corp/union/etc.) + `individual_donors` (separate table with `(name_fingerprint, zip5)` PK, `metadata` for employer/occupation), with `financial_relationships.from_type` extended to `'individual_donor'`; (b) keep one table but add a partial-index strategy keyed on `entity_type IN ('individual')` so PAC queries skip the individual rows; (c) move individuals into a `metadata`-keyed shape on `financial_relationships` itself (no entity row at all — denormalize donor name/employer onto each donation row). Decide once we have real cardinality numbers from the FIX-181 first run. <!--id:FIX-191-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Graph display strategy for individual donors** — FIX-181 added 540K individual donors to `financial_entities`. Naive rendering on the connection graph would explode visually — e.g. Sanders alone has ~975 donors as direct neighbors. Plan options to evaluate together (most likely a combination): **(a) bracket-aggregate nodes per official** — collapse single-recipient donors into a few summary nodes per official ("Mega $10k+", "Major $2.5k–$10k", "Mid $500–$2.5k", "Small $200–$500") with sum + count; click-through opens a side panel with the actual donor list rather than expanding the graph. **(b) connector-donor surfacing** — only render individual donors who gave to 2+ officials as graph nodes; single-recipient donors collapse into the bracket aggregates from (a). This is the network-interesting set; preliminary estimate ~5-10% of donors → 25-50K connector nodes (still needs filtering by total $ or # recipients). **(c) on-demand pinning** — let users search a donor by name (or pick from "top N donors" in the official-detail panel) and pin that donor as a graph node showing all their connections. Solves the "single out for special treatment" use case. **(d) employer-level aggregation** — group individuals by normalized EMPLOYER field into synthetic nodes ("Goldman Sachs employees: $Xk to N officials"); requires text normalization but unlocks "industry money via individual donors" investigations distinct from the PAC graph. **(e) treemap/sunburst-only path** — keep individual donors entirely off the connection graph; surface them only in the existing donor-aggregate vizes ([TreemapGraph.tsx](packages/graph/src/TreemapGraph.tsx) already aggregates by industry — extend to individual donor brackets). Decide based on whether shared-donor-base is a key investigation lens (then b/c are essential) or whether the graph is meant to stay PAC/official-level (then a/e suffice). API/storage impact: needs `connection_count` per individual donor pre-computed in `entity_connections` rebuild for (b) to be cheap. <!--id:FIX-194-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Lower / remove the $5k-itemization floor in `streamPas224`** — [packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts:503](packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/index.ts#L503) drops every pas2 row under $5,000 to thin the stream. FEC's itemization threshold is $200, not $5,000 — so we're voluntarily dropping the entire $200–$4,999 band of PAC-to-candidate contributions. Most leadership-PAC and ideological-PAC donations are sub-$5k. Lowering the floor to $200 (or removing it entirely and relying on the candidate-set filter) would meaningfully expand donor coverage on the official-detail donor tab. Risk: marginal storage growth in `financial_relationships`; verify storage budget after a local re-run. <!--id:FIX-182-->
- [x] 🟢 S — **Make `financial_entities` name-side indexes partial on `entity_type <> 'individual'`** — Implementation of the FIX-191 decision. After FIX-181 landed 435K individuals (96% of `financial_entities` rows), three indexes carry rows no app call site queries: `financial_entities_display_trgm` (~29 MB GIN), `financial_entities_canonical` (~18 MB), `financial_entities_canonical_name_type` (~16 MB). PAC dedup uses `fec_committee_id`; individual dedup uses `donor_fingerprint` — neither path needs canonical_name. Display trigram is only ever consumed by global / PAC search routes, all of which target named entities. Make all three partial on `WHERE entity_type <> 'individual'`. Local result: trgm 29 MB → 1.8 MB, search "goldman" 72 ms → 0.23 ms. <!--id:FIX-195-->
- [x] 🟠 S — **Global search financial-entity tab silently broken — wrong column names** — Surfaced during the FIX-195 audit. Both `searchFinancialEntities` in [apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts:316-347](apps/civitics/app/api/search/route.ts#L316-L347) and the duplicate inline version in [apps/civitics/app/search/page.tsx:458-486](apps/civitics/app/search/page.tsx#L458-L486) `select("id, name, entity_type, total_amount_cents").ilike("name", ...)` — but the actual columns are `display_name` and `total_donated_cents`. The `(db as any)` cast hid the type error, so the queries returned `[]` silently and the financial tab in `/search` and `GlobalSearch.tsx` produced no hits. Fix: use the right columns at the SQL layer and map to the wire shape (`name`, `total_amount_cents`) on the way out, plus `.neq("entity_type", "individual")` so the query hits the partial trgm index from FIX-195 and avoids flooding global search with hundreds of individual donors sharing a common surname. Drop the `(db as any)` cast. <!--id:FIX-196-->
- [x] 🟡 M — **Rewrite OpenStates pipeline to use bulk data dumps** — current pipeline (`packages/data/src/pipelines/openstates/index.ts`) hits the OpenStates API per-state, per-endpoint with pagination — ~50 states × 2 endpoints × N pages. OpenStates publishes daily bulk dumps at `data.openstates.org` (CSV / JSON, free, no rate limits). Same playbook as FEC API → FEC bulk and FIX-118 USASpending API → bulk: download, stream-parse, upsert. Should be ~5–10× faster end-to-end and eliminates the rate-limit ceiling on full state-legislator refreshes. <!--id:FIX-160-->

Anthropic / Vercel rows are live; Supabase wasn't. Fix lands a new `packages/db/src/supabase-usage.ts` with two helpers — `getSupabaseSqlMetrics()` (cheap: `pg_database_size`, `SUM(storage.objects.size)` via the existing admin client, no new env vars) and `getSupabaseManagementMetrics()` (hits `api.supabase.com/v1/projects/{ref}/{analytics,config}` for API request counts, function invocations, disk utilization; needs new `SUPABASE_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` env var, fails graceful if missing). Aggregator [apps/civitics/app/api/platform/usage/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/platform/usage/route.ts) calls both inline before responding (same shape as `getMonthlyAnthropicSpend`); new [apps/civitics/app/api/platform/supabase/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/platform/supabase/route.ts) adds force-refresh / cache-bust handlers. New migration `20260502000000_supabase_live_metrics.sql` adds `platform_limits.has_public_api BOOLEAN`, marks `egress_bytes` as `false` (Supabase exposes no public egress API as of May 2026), and adds rows for `storage_bytes` (free + pro), `api_requests_total`, `function_invocations`, `disk_used_bytes`. `getSourceDisplay` in [packages/db/src/platform-usage.ts](packages/db/src/platform-usage.ts) extended so the egress row shows "Manual (no API)" instead of going amber-stale every week. **Required: create a Personal Access Token at supabase.com/dashboard/account/tokens and add to .env.local + Vercel as `SUPABASE_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`.** <!--id:FIX-190-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Pipeline writers: missing `startSync` calls and inconsistent name strings** — Surfaced while verifying FIX-180. The writer-side ground truth (from `grep startSync packages/data/src/pipelines/`) shows three classes of bug that made the new Data Health card look empty even when pipelines were running: **(a)** [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/officials.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/officials.ts) (`runOfficialsPipeline`) and [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts) (`runVotesPipeline`) never call `startSync` — only `committees.ts` does — so two of the three Congress sub-pipelines never log to `data_sync_log`. **(b)** [packages/data/src/pipelines/ai-summaries/index.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/ai-summaries/index.ts) never calls `startSync` at all, so the AI Summaries row will always show "never" until logging is added. **(c)** Naming convention is mixed across the writer set — hyphens in `agencies-hierarchy`, `federal-register`, `govtrack-cosponsors`, `opensecrets-bulk`, `tag-ai`, `tag-rules`; underscores in `congress_committees`, `tiger_districts`, `fec_bulk`, `usaspending_bulk`, `openstates_bulk_people`. FIX-180 normalizes at the read layer via the `PIPELINES` registry's `aliases` array, but standardizing writers on `snake_case` would let us drop the alias indirection. Plan: **(1)** add `startSync("congress_officials")` / `startSync("congress_votes")` / `startSync("ai_summaries")`; **(2)** rename hyphenated writers to underscored equivalents in one mechanical commit + update `aliases` arrays; **(3)** consider extracting a shared `runPipeline(name, body)` wrapper so the start/complete/fail boilerplate is enforced. <!--id:FIX-183-->

### INFRASTRUCTURE & PERFORMANCE

- [x] 🟠 M — **Rate limiting on public API routes** — implemented 2026-04-16 in `middleware.ts`: sliding-window in-memory limiter (30/min search, 5/min graph/narrative, 60/min graph); 429 + Retry-After; Upstash upgrade path documented <!--id:FIX-048-->

- [x] ⬜ L — **Connection pooling audit** — Supabase uses PgBouncer; verify all server-side Supabase clients are using the pooled connection string for non-transaction workloads <!--id:FIX-052-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Enrichment queue + admin endpoints** — shifts AI tag/summary work off API, routine-ready <!--id:FIX-064-->
- [x] 🟠 L — **Split /api/claude/status into core + quality** — `/core` (meta, db, pipelines, ai_costs, activity) at 60s; `/quality` (quality, self_tests, chord) at 15min; reduces Warren search + chord RPC from every 60s to every 15min <!--id:FIX-082-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Wave A — Caching restoration (Poor RES on dashboard, slow site overall)** — Every server-rendered page exports `force-dynamic` (often paired with `revalidate=0`), so Vercel's edge cache is bypassed app-wide. Combined with `Cache-Control: no-store` in [next.config.mjs:80](apps/civitics/next.config.mjs#L80) the CDN sees nothing. Plan: switch dashboard to `revalidate=1800`, proposals/officials lists + detail pages to `revalidate=300`, drop `force-dynamic` from `/graph` + `/search` (no SSR queries). Replace blanket `no-store` with selective rule (`no-store` only on `/api/auth/*`, `/dashboard`, `/api/admin/*`, mutating routes; `s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=600` on read-heavy pages). Add `React.cache()` wrappers (`apps/civitics/app/proposals/_lib/get-proposal.ts`, `apps/civitics/app/officials/_lib/get-official.ts`) to dedupe `generateMetadata`/page-component duplicate queries. Gate middleware [supabase.auth.getUser()](apps/civitics/middleware.ts#L216) on presence of an `sb-*` cookie — currently runs on every anonymous request and adds 50–150ms to TTFB. <!--id:FIX-197-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Wave B — Dashboard SSR + Suspense** — Dashboard is the worst-RES page because [DashboardClient.tsx](apps/civitics/app/dashboard/DashboardClient.tsx) drives all stats panels via [useDashboardData](apps/civitics/app/dashboard/useDashboardData.ts), which fires fetches in `useEffect` after hydration. LCP candidate is currently "Loading…" text. Move queries server-side: refactor `useDashboardData` into per-section async server functions (`getStats()`, `getPipelineHistory()`, `getAiCosts()`, `getQualityData()`) called from `Promise.all()` in the page component, then pass results as props. Add `loading.tsx` skeletons to `dashboard/`, `graph/`, `profile/`, `districts/[id]/`, `officials/[id]/`, `proposals/[id]/`. Wrap each independently-fetchable dashboard section in `<Suspense>` for streaming. <!--id:FIX-198-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Wave C — Bundle & client-island diet** — `/graph` and detail pages eagerly import all 9 D3 viz types (~180 KB) at top of [GraphPage.tsx:5-33](apps/civitics/app/graph/GraphPage.tsx#L5); [OfficialGraph.tsx](apps/civitics/app/officials/components/OfficialGraph.tsx) and [AgencyGraph.tsx](apps/civitics/app/agencies/[slug]/components/AgencyGraph.tsx) drag D3 onto pages where users may never expand the graph. Wrap each viz in `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })`; build a small viz registry in `packages/graph/src/registry.ts` so GraphPage lazy-loads by viz id. Add `experimental.optimizePackageImports: ["@civitics/graph", "@civitics/maps", "@civitics/ui", "d3"]` and `serverExternalPackages: ["mapbox-gl", "@deck.gl/core", "@deck.gl/layers", "@deck.gl/mapbox"]` to [next.config.mjs](apps/civitics/next.config.mjs). Add `next/font/google` (Inter) to [layout.tsx](apps/civitics/app/layout.tsx) with `display: 'swap'`; update tailwind.config.js to use the CSS variable. Replace 3 raw `<img>` tags ([GlobalSearch.tsx:33](apps/civitics/app/components/GlobalSearch.tsx#L33), SearchDetailPanel, SearchResultCard) with `next/image`. Demote NavBar from a full client component to a Server Component with a small `MobileMenuToggle` client island. Remove dead `apps/civitics/src/app/` glob from tailwind.config.js. <!--id:FIX-199-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Wave D — Data layer & infra** — JS-side aggregation of `page_views` (millions of rows) at [proposals/page.tsx:130](apps/civitics/app/proposals/page.tsx#L130) is wasteful and routine traffic uses the Supabase API gateway rather than the transaction pooler. Add migration creating `proposal_popularity_24h` materialized view (`SELECT proposal_id, COUNT(*) FROM page_views WHERE entity_type='proposal' AND occurred_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '24 hours' GROUP BY proposal_id`) with a unique index and a 30-min refresh schedule; update [proposals/page.tsx:130](apps/civitics/app/proposals/page.tsx#L130) to query the view. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` `get_pv_top_transitions` and `get_pv_entry_pages` against prod; add index on `page_views (entity_type, occurred_at)` if seq-scans appear. Update `packages/db/src/client.ts` to use Supabase transaction pooler (`aws-0-us-west-2.pooler.supabase.com:6543`) for server clients via new `SUPABASE_DB_POOLER_URL` env (added to Vercel separately). Refactor [officials/[id]/page.tsx:334-349](apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx#L334) to a single LEFT JOIN on `financial_entities` instead of paginated batch lookups. Add `output: 'standalone'` to [next.config.mjs](apps/civitics/next.config.mjs). <!--id:FIX-200-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **CDN cache override fix — Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control bypasses the framework's no-store** — Wave A (FIX-197) added `Cache-Control: s-maxage=…` rules in [next.config.mjs](apps/civitics/next.config.mjs), but for any dynamic route Next.js writes its own `Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store` on the response which overrides next.config's headers — verified with `curl -I` returning `X-Vercel-Cache: MISS` on every page. Anonymous TTFB on /proposals stayed at 250–540ms because the edge never cached. Switch the rule from `Cache-Control` to `CDN-Cache-Control` + `Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control` (which the framework header does NOT override — same mechanism FIX-8 used in reverse to bust the dashboard cache). Browsers still see the framework's no-cache, but Vercel's edge caches the response for the s-maxage window and serves cached HTML to the next visitor. Without this, none of FIX-197's caching investment is actually live. <!--id:FIX-201-->
- [x] 🔴 S — **/districts/[id] First Load JS is 546 kB — defer mapbox-gl off the prefetch graph** — Even with `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })`, App Router still includes the dynamic chunk in the route's static prefetch tree, so Next.js counts the ~450 kB of mapbox-gl + CSS in First Load JS for the page. Replace the `next/dynamic` call with a thin client wrapper (`apps/civitics/app/districts/components/DeferredDistrictMap.tsx`) that does the `import()` inside `useEffect` — that's invisible to the prefetch graph, so the chunk only loads after first paint. Page placeholder masks the gap. <!--id:FIX-202-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Drop unnecessary `cookies()` calls so list/detail pages can use proper ISR** — Pages like [proposals/page.tsx](apps/civitics/app/proposals/page.tsx), [officials/page.tsx](apps/civitics/app/officials/page.tsx), [proposals/[id]/page.tsx](apps/civitics/app/proposals/[id]/page.tsx), [officials/[id]/page.tsx](apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx) all call `createServerClient(cookies())` even though they don't actually use the user session — civic content is public, no routes are auth-protected. Calling `cookies()` opts the page into dynamic rendering, blocking real `revalidate=300` ISR. After FIX-201 lands the CDN fix this is less urgent (the edge cache covers most of the win) but switching these to `createClient()` with the publishable key would unlock true static generation, which is faster than even an SSR-with-CDN-cache hit — the page is served from disk, not function. Touch `packages/db/src/client.ts` if a no-cookie server-side helper doesn't exist yet. <!--id:FIX-203-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **Dashboard SSR prefetch — replace useDashboardData waterfall with server-fetched initial state** — Deferred from Wave B (FIX-198). [DashboardClient.tsx](apps/civitics/app/dashboard/DashboardClient.tsx) currently fires 4 fetch() calls in `useEffect` after hydration; LCP candidate is the "Loading…" text. Refactor: extract per-section async server fns mirroring [/api/claude/status/core/route.ts](apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/core/route.ts) (which itself imports from `_lib/sections.ts` — already a server-callable surface), call them in the page's existing `Promise.all`, pass results to DashboardClient as `initialData` props. Modify [useDashboardData.ts](apps/civitics/app/dashboard/useDashboardData.ts) to accept `initialData` and use it as `useState`'s initial value — skip the first useEffect fetch when present. Wrap each independently-fetchable dashboard section in `<Suspense>` so panels stream in instead of all-or-nothing. <!--id:FIX-204-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **GraphPage per-viz dynamic import registry** — Deferred from Wave C (FIX-199). [GraphPage.tsx:5-33](apps/civitics/app/graph/GraphPage.tsx#L5) eagerly imports all 9 viz types (~180 kB of D3 + sankey). Default view is Force; the other 8 ride along on every visit. Build a small `vizComponents` registry in `packages/graph/src/registry.ts` keyed by `VizType` returning `lazy(() => import(...))`, then in GraphPage swap the static imports for `const VizComponent = vizComponents[active]` and a `<Suspense>` boundary. Tricky bit: today every viz is mounted simultaneously with `opacity:0` to enable instant tab switching + persistent SVG refs. Lazy mounting requires either lifting refs out of the viz components or accepting a brief load on first switch (probably acceptable). Same treatment for [OfficialGraph.tsx](apps/civitics/app/officials/components/OfficialGraph.tsx) and [AgencyGraph.tsx](apps/civitics/app/agencies/[slug]/components/AgencyGraph.tsx) — they drag D3 onto detail pages where users may never expand the graph. <!--id:FIX-205-->
- [x] 🔴 M — **Dashboard `getDatabase()` returns 0 for proposals/votes/financial_relationships on prod + getOpenProposals queries non-existent column** — Surfaced verifying FIX-089. Hitting `https://civitics-civitics.vercel.app/api/claude/status` returns `database: { officials: 9892, proposals: 0, proposals_regulations: 1092, votes: 0, financial_relationships: 0, ... }` — unfiltered `count(*)` on the three biggest tables returns 0 even though local probes confirm reltuples is fresh and accurate (proposals 75k, votes 922k, financial_relationships 2.2M). The 10 parallel `count: "exact"` queries in [getDatabase()](apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts#L67-L116) saturate the PostgREST request budget on Vercel under concurrent load (works locally with looser timeouts) and `count ?? 0` swallows the error. **Same probe also caught a separate bug:** `getOpenProposals()` in [apps/civitics/app/dashboard/page.tsx](apps/civitics/app/dashboard/page.tsx) and the new `getOpenProposalCount()` filter on `comment_period_end` — but that column doesn't exist on `proposals`. The value lives in `metadata->>comment_period_end` (verified by inspecting an `open_comment` row). The whole Comment Periods section + Open Proposals card have been silently failing on this. **Plan:** (1) Switch unfiltered counts to `count: "estimated"` (uses `pg_class.reltuples`, sub-200ms, won't time out). (2) Switch the timing-out filtered count `proposals_bills` to `count: "planned"` (planner row estimate; ~24k vs 24,040 exact — close enough for a hero card). Keep `exact` on the small fast filtered counts (`proposals_regulations`, `page_views_24h`). (3) Fix `comment_period_end` references → `metadata->>comment_period_end`. (4) Surface partial state through `database.error` like `chord` already does, so the UI can render "—"/amber instead of silently showing 0 if a future query does fail. Chord RPC timeout split out as FIX-207. <!--id:FIX-206-->
- [x] 🟠 M — **`chord_industry_flows` RPC times out at 8 s on prod — kills Donation Flow card and chord-has-industry-data self-test** — Split out from FIX-206. The RPC at [supabase/migrations/20260422000008_restore_chord_treemap_rpcs.sql:25-73](supabase/migrations/20260422000008_restore_chord_treemap_rpcs.sql#L25-L73) joins `financial_relationships` (2.2M rows) × `officials` × `financial_entities` × `entity_tags`, group-bys 4 derived columns including a `CASE` on `role_title`, and orders by `SUM(amount_cents) DESC`. Local probe confirms the timeout is real (8.25s, the authenticated-role statement_timeout), not a transient PostgREST budget issue. With `chord` permanently `{"error":"...timeout","partial":true}` on prod, [getChord()](apps/civitics/app/api/claude/status/_lib/sections.ts#L632-L666) returns nothing and the FIX-089 Donation Flow card renders `$0`. **Options:** (a) Add a covering composite index on `financial_relationships(relationship_type, to_id, from_id)` — likely insufficient given the 4-table join. (b) Materialize via a `chord_industry_flows_mv` refreshed nightly during the existing rebuild_entity_connections cron — accepts up-to-24h staleness in exchange for an instant query. (c) Pre-compute into a flat summary row on each pipeline run (e.g. `platform_aggregates(key='chord_industry_flows', payload JSONB)`) and have the RPC just SELECT from it. **Recommended:** (b) — sized at ~50–100 rows by industry × party_chamber, refresh tied to the nightly cron that already writes 1.6M edges. Add `chord_total_flow_usd` to the same MV so the Donation Flow card has a reliable USD source even if the chord viz needs more shape. <!--id:FIX-207-->

### Notes

  - **Investigation outcome (2026-04-28):** the original premise was wrong. The `' v. '` matches were 408 legitimate CourtListener-sourced rows (correctly stored with `case_details`) plus ~1,170 real ordinances/resolutions that *mention* a case in their title (zoning, settlements, the abortion-rights resolution citing Dobbs) — **not contamination**. The `^on ` matches were 434 federal-bill stubs the votes ingester created with `title = vote question` instead of the bill title. They are load-bearing: each anchors hundreds of `votes.bill_proposal_id` rows (213,582 votes total). Cannot quarantine; must re-title. New script `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:retitle-stubs --apply --allow-prod` fetches real bill titles from Congress.gov v3 and updates in place, preserving the original procedural string in `metadata.original_procedural_title`. Local: 287 retitled, 147 deferred (Senate-amendment format `S 11-22` — see FIX-164). Prod: 0 stubs found; cutover already cleared them.
  - **Investigation outcome (2026-04-28):** these aren't bill amendments — they're Presidential Nomination (PN) votes (cabinet, judicial, ambassador confirmations). The Senate XML has `<document_type>=PN`, `<document_number>=11-22`, but `normalizeSenateDocType` in [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts) falls through to `S` for unrecognized types, mangling them into fake `S 11-22` bill numbers with `type=bill`. New script `pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:retitle-pn-stubs --apply` fetches the linked Senate XML, extracts `<document_title>`, sets `title=<document_title>`, `type=appointment`, corrects `legacy_bill_number` to `PN 11-22`, preserves originals in `metadata.original_*`. Local: 147 retitled (144 with the `-` form + 3 single-number cases like `S 19`). Prod: 0 stubs found. Pipeline-side root-cause fix tracked as FIX-165.
  - **2026-04-28:** done. PN branch added in the Senate parsing block (uses `<document_title>` for `title`, `type=appointment`, `billNumber=PN N`, `congressGovUrl=…/nomination/{c}th-congress/{N}`); `normalizeSenateDocType` now returns `null` instead of falling through to `S`, with a one-line warning when the new code path encounters an unrecognized type so future drift is loud rather than silent. Verified locally on the in-progress 119th run — first new PN since the fix landed as `appointment`: "Robert Cekada, of Florida, to be Director, Bureau of Alcohol, Tobacco, Firearms, and Explosives" (PN 730-14).
  - **2026-04-30:** caveat fired — next run failed with `"upstream request timeout"` (Supabase edge gateway, not Postgres). Worked around by routing the rebuild RPC through `pg.Client` directly to the session pooler when `SUPABASE_DB_URL` is set, falling back to PostgREST `admin.rpc()` for local dev where the rebuild completes in <1s. New env var added to [.github/workflows/nightly.yml](.github/workflows/nightly.yml). **Required GitHub Secret to add:** `SUPABASE_DB_URL` set to `postgresql://postgres.xsazcoxinpgttgquwvuf:<password>@aws-0-us-west-2.pooler.supabase.com:5432/postgres` (session pooler — port 5432, not 6543). Local `.env.local.prod` should also get this if you ever run `data:nightly` against prod from a laptop.

    - **2026-04-28:** the previous attempts stalled because `votes.ts` had a hard-coded session→year mapping (`{1: 2025, 2: 2026}`) — running with `CONGRESS_OVERRIDE=117` looked for 2025 House XML and 404'd every roll. Fixed in [packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts](packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts): `year = 2021 + (CURRENT_CONGRESS - 117) * 2 + (session - 1)`. Coupled with FIX-165 (PN handling) so the backfill doesn't seed thousands of new procedural-titled stubs. Local + prod backfills run end-to-end; vote count climbs from 245k to ~600k+.

  > Refinement plan: see [`docs/GRAPH_PLAN.md`](GRAPH_PLAN.md). FIX-120 → FIX-150 each map to a section there.
